The platform owns the research loop. It asks a model for the next step, runs the typed [agent tools](#d049-expose-evidence-to-agents-through-typed-tools-bound-to-the-run-context) the model proposes, decides when to stop, and keeps the history. Each model call is one fresh completion with the runtime's own tools disabled, and the model returns its proposal as structured output. Each completion passes the [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy) gateway as one call, so the gateway checks and records every payload sent to a provider.

A native tool loop in the Claude Agent SDK or Codex app-server would run tool calls inside the runtime, where the gateway does not see them. The port would then replace that loop with another one. With one platform loop, the loop validated locally is the loop that ships, and the port changes the adapter behind D041's `Adapter`. The quarry repository makes the same split between a text completion and a runtime-owned step.

The subscription adapters run the installed, unmodified `claude` and `codex` executables under the researcher's own sign-in. The platform never reads, stores, or forwards their credentials. Anthropic's legal guidance permits a user to sign in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own subscription, and its support article of 7 October 2026 says that Agent SDK and `claude -p` usage draws from subscription limits. OpenAI documents plan usage for local apps. Codex app-server is experimental, so each adapter accepts only the exact runtime version it was tested with. Each adapter starts a fresh session in an empty temporary directory, with no tools, settings, hooks, skills, plugins, MCP servers, or session persistence. An empty directory does not stop a CLI from loading the user's own configuration, such as MCP servers in `~/.codex/config.toml` or Claude's user-scoped MCP servers. Each adapter therefore disables every inherited source with flags that the capability probe verifies, and it refuses to run when a source stays enabled. It checks the tool list the runtime reports before it sends anything.

These limits are deliberate:

- A capability probe must show that both runtimes return structured output with every tool disabled, at the installed Claude Code 2.1.301 and Codex 0.162.1. [D058](#d058-call-codex-through-a-fresh-app-server-in-an-isolated-home) shows it for Codex, and [D057](#d057-call-claude-code-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-behind-a-local-first-turn-gate) for Claude Code.
- Tool outcomes do not yet carry the restrictions that the gateway checks. `datasets.Supplied` has no provider approvals, and D049 leaves rendering for providers unwired. Each rendered tool result must carry derived labels, approvals, and availability before the gateway can check it. [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read) adds them.
- Subscription calls have no price, and the usage the runtimes report does not count every underlying inference. The ledger must not record unreported usage as zero or an API-equivalent price as cash spent. D016's exact units have no unknown value today.
- The campaign runner keeps its own ledger in the workflow, and the gateway charges a separate `LedgerStore`. Wiring the loop needs one accounting owner first.
- The pilot does not use plan-included API credits. A switch to them needs the researcher's plan details and consent.

## D052. Ingest the pilot's public data into a vintage-addressed store

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P01 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#ingest-the-pilots-public-data-with-every-vintage-kept-p01). It replaces the one-file-per-dataset store of [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter), whose next download broke the replay of every earlier read record.

[quant_research.public_data](../src/quant_research/public_data.py) and four `quant-research data` commands keep the pilot's public data under a root directory that the researcher names with `--root`. `data pull` downloads each dataset and refuses a download that does not convert. It keeps the original bytes in `objects/` under their SHA-256 and writes a pull record in `pulls/<dataset>/` with the digest, the retrieval time, and the URL. The FRED API key comes from `FRED_API_KEY` in the environment. It is sent with the request and is never stored or printed, and the recorded URL leaves it out. `data ingest` converts each dataset's latest pull into D045 rows, stores the canonical file in `datasets/<dataset>/<vintage>.json`, and documents it in `catalog.json`. `data validate` records the researcher's manual attestation and keeps the evidence file's bytes in `objects/`. `data approve` approves a validated dataset through `quant_research.datasets/v1`. A download digest never validates a dataset.

`alfred.<series>` names every real-time period of a FRED series, requested with `realtime_start=1776-07-04` and `realtime_end=9999-12-31`. `french.ff3_monthly` names the monthly section of the Ken French Fama/French 3 Factors file. The commands pull `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.sahmrealtime`, and `french.ff3_monthly` by default. Each row's key is its observation date, so the three datasets join on the first day of each month. Values keep the publisher's decimal text, and ALFRED's missing value `.` becomes `null`. FRED drops trailing zeros, so `SAHMREALTIME` publishes 0.30 as `0.3`. Ken French values stay in percent, and the returns calculation converts them.

ALFRED revisions of an observation are numbered in `realtime_start` order. A value becomes available at the end of its `realtime_start` date in UTC, which is 00:00 UTC on the next day, and its `published_at` and `arrived_at` both take that time. A BLS release at 8:30 Eastern Time therefore never appears before it happened, and a cutoff at the end of a date sees every release dated that day. Each Ken French month becomes available at the end of the month, which is 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month. This convention is optimistic. The library publishes a month weeks later and rebuilds its whole history each time, and ingest keeps only the pulled cut, as revision 1 of every month. The pilot accepts it because returns score decisions and never feed the signal, and a search cutoff still hides every later month.

Conversion is the boundary check. It refuses an ALFRED response that does not cover every real-time period or whose `count` differs from its observations. It also refuses an observation whose real-time periods leave a gap or end before `9999-12-31`, and any malformed date or value. A series longer than the API's 100,000-row page therefore fails instead of paging. A Ken French file must hold one CSV whose monthly rows are consecutive months with four decimal values each.

Catalog documentation now names the dataset's vintage, which is the SHA-256 of the converted file, and the providers approved to receive the dataset. `read` opens the vintage that the documentation in force names, and it raises when the bytes under that name hash to anything else. Validation and approval therefore cover exactly those bytes. Every earlier vintage stays in the store, so a read record replays from its vintage's bytes after a newer download. Ingest compares the new documentation with the documentation in force, ignoring the actor and time. Unchanged documentation appends nothing and keeps the dataset's status, so a repeated pull and ingest of unchanged data changes no digest. Changed documentation, such as a new vintage or a new provider approval, revokes any validation or approval and documents the dataset again in one catalog revision. An unchanged ingest of a revoked dataset documents it again so it can be validated anew. Stored bytes come first and the catalog is replaced last, so a crash leaves at most an unreferenced file and the next ingest converges.

On 22 October 2026 the live check pulled and ingested all three datasets twice into a scratch root. Both runs gave the same download digests, vintages, and catalog digest. The UNRATE vintages start on 15 March 1960, and `SAHMREALTIME` has one vintage of its whole history, dated 6 September 2019. Both hold `null` for October 2025. Every agent read was refused as documented until `data validate` and `data approve` ran, and reads at 1 January 1990 and 22 October 2026 then succeeded. Those attestations only checked the commands. The researcher's data root was validated and approved on 23 October 2026 ([pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#pin-the-data)).

These limits are deliberate:

- `SAHMREALTIME` has no vintage before 6 September 2019, so no earlier cutoff sees the known answer. Reconciliation compares against its latest values.
- ALFRED's real-time dates show when ALFRED published a value, not when a firm received it.
- Provider approvals are recorded and not enforced. Tool results do not carry them until P08. [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read) carries them into every rendered tool result, and the gateway checks them on each send. Documentation names no provider until the researcher names the subscription routes. The researcher named `codex-subscription` and `claude-subscription` for the pilot's datasets on 23 October 2026 ([pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#pin-the-data)). Each ingest states the whole provider list with `--provider`, so an ingest that adds or omits a provider revokes approval.
- The test fixtures are excerpts of the real 22 October 2026 downloads. Each ALFRED excerpt sets `count` to its own length.
- The root has no lock. Two concurrent ingests of one root can lose a catalog event, and one researcher runs them.

## D053. Evaluate signal programs per decision and reconcile the Sahm series with FRED

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P02 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#recompute-the-real-time-sahm-series-and-reconcile-it-with-fred-p02). It builds on the dataset store of [D052](#d052-ingest-the-pilots-public-data-into-a-vintage-addressed-store) and the adapter of [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter).

[quant_research.signals](../src/quant_research/signals.py) defines the first signal operators as frozen dataclasses. `Series` reads one field of a dataset, and a null value stays `None`. `TrailingMean` averages the months of its window that have a value. `TrailingMin` takes the least value over the months before the evaluated month, not including it. `Subtract` takes the difference, and `Threshold` is true when its input is at or above its level. `SAHM` is the three-month mean of `alfred.unrate` minus the least three-month mean of the previous 12 months, and `SAHM_RULE` is `SAHM` at or above 0.50. Every value is a `Fraction` computed from the publisher's decimal text, so no rounding happens before comparison. A threshold's input must be a number, so a boolean never feeds arithmetic.

`decide` evaluates a program once per decision month. The decision for month m narrows the trusted run context to a cutoff at 00:00 UTC on the first day of m, which is the end of month m-1. It reads each dataset the program names through `datasets.read`, so the adapter alone decides approval, clearance, and which revision of each month is visible. It then takes the latest month the program can compute from that vintage. Each `Decision` records the decision month, the observation month, the value, the time the vintage became available, and the adapter's read record for each dataset. The availability time is the latest `available_at` among the supplied rows, which D052 sets to 00:00 UTC on the day after ALFRED's `realtime_start`. A month is computable only when every month the program reaches lies in the vintage and the value is not null. Appending a later vintage changes no earlier decision, because no earlier cutoff sees the new rows.

P02 chose between narrowing the run context per decision and a read that returns one vintage with every revision. Narrowing keeps the adapter as the only place that applies the availability rule, and every decision keeps a read record that replays. A one-vintage read would need a new operation in `datasets.py`, which P08 owns in wave 2, or it would copy the adapter's availability rule into `signals.py`. On 22 October 2026 the whole `reconcile series` run, 802 decisions and one reference read, took a median of 13.6 seconds over five runs, from 13.4 to 21.6 seconds, at load averages between 34 and 81 on 14 cores. Earlier, at a load average near 300, the decision reads alone took 41.3 seconds. Each read parses the whole stored file on one core, so the time grows with the number of decisions times the file size. A faster adapter read can move into `datasets.py` once its owner frees the file.

October 2025 has no `UNRATE` value. A window that contains a null month averages, or takes the least of, its other months. A null month is not computable itself. This convention gives FRED's November 2025 value, (4.4 + 4.6) / 2 - 61/15 = 13/30, which rounds to 0.43, and its December 2025 value, (4.5 + 4.4) / 2 - 41/10 = 7/20, which is 0.35. It also leaves October 2025 null, as FRED does. Carrying September forward gives 0.40 and 0.33, and averaging the last three reported months gives 0.37 and 0.33. Linear interpolation gives the same two values, but it needs a later month, so the convention skips the null month instead. FRED publishes negative values, which confirms that the previous 12 months exclude the current month.

[quant_research.reconcile](../src/quant_research/reconcile.py) adds a series profile. `SeriesProfile` names the program, the reference dataset, a tolerance, and the months that may leave the window with a declared reason. `SAHM_REALTIME` compares `SAHM` with `alfred.sahmrealtime` within 1/200, half of FRED's last published digit. Recomputed values lie on a grid of 1/60 and FRED's on a grid of 1/100, so their differences lie on a grid of 1/300 and none equals the tolerance. `quant-research reconcile series --root DATA_ROOT` issues a run context with the current time as its cutoff, makes one decision per month from January 1960 through the current month, and prints one canonical JSON record. The record names the window, the number of matching months, each excluded month with its reason and both values, any unexplained mismatch, the input vintages, and the catalog digest. The command exits 1 on an unexplained mismatch. The window starts at the observation month of the first decision, which is February 1960, the latest month in the first `UNRATE` vintage of 15 March 1960. FRED's December 1959 and January 1960 values were released before that vintage and are listed as before the window. The window ends at the last month of the pinned `SAHMREALTIME` download.

A month leaves the window for one of four recorded reasons:

- No decision observes it, because a later month was released before the next decision. This holds for seven months between April 1961 and September 1963, when two releases fell inside one month.
- No decision before the run's cutoff observes it. On 22 October 2026 this held for September 2026, whose decision falls in November.
- Neither FRED nor `UNRATE` has a value. This holds for October 2025.
- The profile declares it. For ten months between December 1960 and December 1981, FRED's value equals the indicator from the next `UNRATE` release, which revised the window after the vintage the decision read. Nine of them are December values revised by the February release, and the tenth is January 1963. For March 2021, FRED's 2.33 needs January 2020 at 3.6, its value before the 8 January 2021 revision. Every vintage holding March 2021 gives 71/30 or 12/5 instead.

On 22 October 2026 the live check matched 780 of the 800 months in the window and excluded 20 with these reasons. The researcher accepted the exclusions on 23 October 2026 in P04, per [D055](#d055-run-the-reference-experiment-through-one-audited-pipeline-against-a-frozen-experiment-file).

The package reconciliation moves to `quant-research reconcile package PACKAGE --reference FILE`, and its behavior is unchanged. P02 does not make the package path choose its metrics and recomputation by profile. No package records a series until P04, which adds that choice when its diagnostic exists.

These limits are deliberate:

- The declared reasons describe arithmetic that reproduces FRED's values. They do not establish how FRED computes `SAHMREALTIME`.
- A decision keeps one read record per dataset, and each record lists every supplied row's identity, about 900 for a recent `UNRATE` vintage. P04 decides which records a package keeps.
- The performance margin depends on the machine's load, because each decision parses the stored file again.
- Operators have no parameter ranges, size limit, or canonical JSON form. P14 adds them in [D064](#d064-search-signal-programs-in-a-bounded-grammar-over-a-chosen-alfred-universe).

## D054. Backtest allocations with a plain reference and a vectorbt engine

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P03 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#backtest-allocations-with-two-independent-engines-p03). It implements the returns and costs of the [pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#define-the-phase-1-reference-case) and the independent recomputation that [D050](#d050-run-the-phase-0-pilot-locally-on-public-us-macro-data) requires.

[quant_research.backtest](../src/quant_research/backtest.py) reads `french.ff3_monthly` through the dataset adapter and converts each month's percent text to an exact `Fraction`, so "2.89" becomes 289/10000. `read` returns a `Market` with the adapter's read record, or the adapter's refusal. `Market.sample` pairs an allocation, keyed by month, with those factors. A `Sample` is a run of consecutive months. Each month has a `Holding` whose value is `w`, 1 for the market and 0 for T-bills. A fractional weight, a gap, or a month without factors raises `BacktestError`. An engine is any function from a `Sample` to one `Fraction` return per month. `plain` is the reference, and it computes in `Fraction`. `vectorbt` runs vectorbt 1.2.0, the stand-in for the firm backtester. It imports vectorbt on its first call, because the CLI tests run `python -I -S` without third-party packages. Each engine finds the switches from the holdings itself, and the two engines share only the cost rate. A wrong switch rule in one engine therefore shows up as a disagreement. `compare` runs `plain` and another engine, vectorbt by default. It returns both series, the month of their largest absolute difference, and that difference.

A switch costs 10 basis points of the portfolio value at the start of the month the new allocation starts. The trade happens then, and the remaining value earns the month's return. A switch month therefore returns (1 - 1/1000) × (1 + RF + w × (Mkt-RF)) - 1, and any other month returns RF + w × (Mkt-RF). The first month and the end of the sample carry no cost. The pilot brief says "portfolio value" without naming the instant, so this decision fixes it. The additive form, RF + w × (Mkt-RF) - 1/1000, treats the fee as if it stayed invested until the end of the month. It differs from the chosen form by 1/1000 × r, where r is RF + w × (Mkt-RF). In a month with r near 0.5 percent, the difference is about 5e-6.

vectorbt's cash earns no interest, so the engine holds T-bills as a second asset. It prices the market leg at the cumulative product of 1 + RF + Mkt-RF and the bills leg at the cumulative product of 1 + RF. It groups the two legs with shared cash and places target-percent orders only in the first month and in switch months. Orders fill at the previous month's close, which is 1.0 in the first month, so a fee falls inside the switch month. Only the sold leg pays a fee, at the 1/1000 rate. The whole portfolio sits in that leg, so the fee is exactly 10 basis points of the starting value, and the purchase spends the rest. A fee on both legs would need a per-leg rate with no terminating decimal. The layout depends on `w` being 0 or 1. A fractional weight would hold both legs, let them drift between switches, and make the sold-leg fee differ from 10 basis points of the portfolio.

Both engines return `Fraction`, and vectorbt's floats convert exactly, so `compare` subtracts exact values. `TOLERANCE` is 1e-12 absolute per month, and it is the only allowance. A probe on 22 October 2026 ran vectorbt 1.2.0 on Python 3.12 with numpy 2.5.4, pandas 3.1.0, and numba 0.68.0. It matched the hand-calculated cases within 2e-16. The live check ran both engines over the full Ken French history, the 1202 months from July 1926 to August 2026. Its fixed path holds the market for six months and T-bills for six months in turn and switches in the last month, 201 switches in all. The largest difference was 3.4e-16, in May 1958. A wrong cost rule misses by about 1/1000 in a switch month, and the additive form misses by 1/1000 × r. The unit test of the additive form fails `compare` by 2.97e-5 in October 1989. An agent chose the cost instant and the tolerance under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026. On 23 October 2026, after the agent listed them for the researcher's review, the researcher told the agent to record its recommendations. The record therefore keeps the cost at the start of the switch month and the 1e-12 tolerance. It also counts this comparison as the pilot's independent recomputation, within the shared-input limit below.

On 22 October 2026, at 1-minute load averages from 5.4 to 6.2 on 14 cores, five alternating runs of each engine measured the full history. The plain engine took a median of 0.003 seconds, against the 2-second rule. A vectorbt run in an empty numba cache took a median of 5.47 seconds, from 5.45 to 5.50 seconds, against the 10-second rule. Most of that time is numba compiling vectorbt's functions on one core, once per installation. A vectorbt run with a populated cache took 0.12 seconds. Importing vectorbt took a median of 1.4 seconds, from 1.3 to 1.5 seconds, and the rule excludes it. At load averages between 74 and 199, while other sessions ran their suites, a run in an empty cache took up to 15.9 seconds and 12.9 seconds of CPU time.

numba writes its compiled functions beside vectorbt's source by default. `make merge` runs tests in a prepared environment whose fingerprint covers every file, so a cache there would fail the next run's fingerprint check. `tests/test_backtest.py` sets `NUMBA_CACHE_DIR` to `quant-research-numba` under the system temporary directory before vectorbt loads. The first suite in a new prepared environment compiles, and later suites reuse the cache. The module does not set the variable, because a library should not choose a cache for the whole process. vectorbt 1.2.0 grows the lock from 34 to 94 packages, including numba, llvmlite, scipy, scikit-learn, matplotlib, plotly, and ipython. The worker image keeps its own lock.

These limits are deliberate:

- The engines cover only allocations of the whole portfolio to the market or to one-month T-bills.
- Returns are research returns from the factor file. They include no slippage, taxes, or financing.
- `plain` gives exact values, and vectorbt's values carry float rounding. P04 decides which series its package records.
- The engines share the factor inputs and the cost rule. The comparison checks how each engine accounts for cash, positions, fees, and switches. It does not check the factor file or the cost rule. The hand-calculated tests check the rule against the pilot brief.
- The live check read a data root that an agent validated and approved for this check only. The researcher's data root, validated and approved on 23 October 2026, holds the same vintages ([pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#pin-the-data)).

## D055. Run the reference experiment through one audited pipeline against a frozen experiment file

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P04 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#run-the-reference-experiment-as-a-scoped-phase-1-p04). It joins the Sahm program of [D053](#d053-evaluate-signal-programs-per-decision-and-reconcile-the-sahm-series-with-fred), the two engines of [D054](#d054-backtest-allocations-with-a-plain-reference-and-a-vectorbt-engine), and the evaluation profile of [D015](#d015-evaluate-pilot-signals-against-their-recorded-trials) into the scoped Phase 1 that [D050](#d050-run-the-phase-0-pilot-locally-on-public-us-macro-data) defines.

[quant_research.reference](../src/quant_research/reference.py) holds the pipeline. `run` takes a case, the parsed experiment file, the catalog, the dataset store, a trusted run context, and a caller-supplied lock. It narrows the context to the experiment's cutoff, decides every window month, audits each decision's reads, and reconciles the known answer when the case has one. It then allocates, backtests the allocation on both engines, evaluates the exact returns against buy-and-hold, and saves one package with one trial and one attempt. `quant-research reference run --root DATA_ROOT --output DIR --lock FILE` runs the four cases in `CASES` and then prints one JSON array with one object per case. It exits 1 after printing when a case ends outside its expected statuses. A refused read exits 1 at once without printing the array. `--experiment FILE` runs another file in the same format, and each package's contract digest names the file that ran.

[reference.json](../src/quant_research/reference.json) holds the window, the information cutoff, the evaluation parameters, and the baseline. `load_experiment` accepts only canonical JSON, so one content has one spelling. The file's SHA-256, `af4fafb3df64b497d9a02e0c2122902a72a8968092d1b1460cc4d563d5d0c8b9`, is each trial's `data_contract_digest`, and the file's bytes are the package's contract artifact. A changed parameter therefore changes the specification ID, and `run` takes the arguments of `evaluate` from the parsed file alone. D028 rejected new specification fields and deferred a parameter string in `evaluation_profile` until something read one. This decision binds the parameters through the contract digest instead and keeps `evaluation_profile` at `pilot-evaluation/v1`. It also fixes the number format that D028 left open. The minimum effect and alpha are decimal text without redundant zeros, and the subperiod count is an integer. D028 did not specify this binding. P04 adds it.

The frozen values are these:

- The window runs from April 1960 to August 2026, 797 months. ALFRED's `UNRATE` vintages begin on 15 March 1960, so April 1960 is the first decision with a vintage. August 2026 is the last month of the pinned factor file.
- The cutoff is 00:00 UTC on 23 October 2026, the end of 22 October 2026 UTC, before the pull at 01:45 UTC. Every row of the pinned vintages is available by then, the latest on 3 October 2026. Decisions still read at the start of their months. The factor read and the known-answer reconciliation read at the cutoff. Every read record is therefore the same on any later day, and the reconciliation window still ends at the last pinned `SAHMREALTIME` month. A cutoff at the start of September 2026 would have hidden FRED's August and September values.
- The minimum effect is 0.001 a month, about 1.2 percent a year. A smaller incremental return does not justify moving the whole portfolio into T-bills on research returns with no slippage, taxes, or financing.
- The subperiod count is 4, equal parts of about 16.6 years. The strategy departs from the market only around recessions, and each part holds at least one NBER recession, so each part tests the signal. The count rests on recession dates that the researcher and the models already know, not on the strategy's returns.
- Alpha is 0.05.
- The baseline is buy-and-hold market total return, `RF + (Mkt-RF)`, computed by `plain` on an all-market allocation, which never switches and so pays no cost.

Commit `411fabc` added the file at 02:36 UTC on 23 October 2026, before any code that computes the strategy's returns existed. The freeze was decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026. The plan asked the researcher to accept the file before the real run. Under the grant, the agent made that call. The researcher accepted the unchanged file on 23 October 2026, after the run. The plan's pre-run gate was therefore not met as written. The researcher accepted the parameters after seeing the results, and only the agent's freeze came before them.

Each package holds one trial, so the Bonferroni count is 1. The Sahm rule is the known-answer case that D050 chose, not a candidate selected from a search. P02 computed the indicator and P03 backtested a fixed allocation path, and neither computed this strategy's returns. The controls are validation fixtures in their own packages, so they add nothing to the count. The count is only as complete as this declared history, as D015 says.

The decision for month m holds T-bills when the condition is true and the market when it is false. A decision without a value keeps the previous holding. A first window decision without a value rejects the run, because no initial allocation exists. In the frozen window the first decision has a value.

`audit` rejects the flawed variants. A decision breaches when it names no read, when one of its reads has a cutoff after 00:00 UTC on the first of its month, or when its release time is after that instant. The adapter mints each cutoff from the context it was given and supplies only rows available by then ([D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter)). The decisions artifact keeps each cutoff and release time, so a reader can repeat the audit from the package alone. A breach rejects the run before any return is computed.

The behavioral form of "future observations cannot change past signals" recomputes each decision under an authority narrowed to its own month. For `signals.decide` that compares the function with itself at twice the reads. It also misses a revision that leaves a boolean decision unchanged. The tests keep it as an oracle. For every case, no month changes that the audit does not flag. The audit trusts that each record came from `datasets.read`. A decider that forges a record or bypasses the adapter is outside it. P15 submits programs to `signals.decide`, not deciders.

The cases isolate one flaw each:

- `sahm-rule/v1` runs `SAHM_RULE` through `signals.decide`.
- `sahm-rule-current-vintage/v1` takes each observation month from `signals.decide` and each value from one read at the run's cutoff. Every decision names that read, so every decision breaches.
- `sahm-rule-fixed-lag/v1` always observes month m-2. It reads at the start of m when m-2 is already released. Otherwise it reads when key m-2 first appears, even when that value is null. A slipped release therefore breaches, and so does the null October 2025, whose release came after the December decision. Waiting for a value instead would never read October 2025 and would leave that slip unseen.
- `never-bills/v1` runs `Threshold(Subtract(Series("alfred.unrate"), Series("alfred.unrate")), Fraction(1, 2))`. The difference is 0 for any data, so the rule always holds the market and its returns are the baseline's. On CPython 3.12, `statistics.linear_regression` of a series on itself gives slope 1 and intercept exactly 0 with zero residuals. The incremental interval is then [0, 0], below any positive minimum effect, so the verdict is null before any data is read. Holding T-bills or charging a cost in a month without a switch would give a nonzero intercept.

The specification takes its question and signal profile from the case. Its evaluation profile is `pilot-evaluation/v1`, its data version is `quant_research.datasets/v1`, and its implementation version is `reference-pipeline/v1`. Its input digests are the vintage digests of every dataset the run read: `UNRATE`, the factors, and `SAHMREALTIME` for the reference case. [Invalidation](#d046-suspend-records-whose-support-an-invalidation-removes) can therefore find the package. The implementation digest covers every `quant_research` source file, as replay's does, and the environment record takes the caller's lock.

Artifact names are a label and the first 16 hex digits of the content's SHA-256, so equal content gets one name. The artifacts are these:

- `input` holds each vintage file read, checked against its digest. The files include rows after the cutoff.
- `contract`, `implementation`, and `lock` hold the bytes that their digests name.
- `decisions` holds each month's observation, value, and release time. Each read keeps its dataset, vintage, catalog, cutoff, context, supplied-row count, and the SHA-256 of its full record. Full records would list an average of 544 rows per decision in the live run, about 15 MB in all (inferred from the row count, not measured). The vintage bytes and the cutoff rebuild each one.
- `audit` lists each breach with the latest cutoff or release time it names.
- `reconciliation` is the `reconcile_series` record of the reference case.
- `returns` holds each month's holding and the strategy and baseline returns as exact fractions from `plain`, the series [D054](#d054-backtest-allocations-with-a-plain-reference-and-a-vectorbt-engine) left for P04 to choose.
- `engine-agreement` holds vectorbt's worst month and its exact difference from `plain` against the 1e-12 tolerance. It does not store vectorbt's floats.
- `pilot-evaluation` holds the evaluation result with its procedure version and parameters. `encode_evaluation` and `decode_evaluation` own its format, and decoding then encoding returns the same bytes. The role name differs from replay's `evaluation`, which holds labels and pairs.

One data exposure event covers each dataset, over the keys that a read at the cutoff supplied. A result event covers the evaluation when one exists. Coverage is `declared_inputs`. Two fresh runs on the same inputs give identical bytes in every result artifact and the same specification ID, and saving an opened package again gives the same digest.

The status follows the first check that decides it:

| Finding | Status |
| --- | --- |
| An audit breach, a known-answer mismatch, or an undecided first month | rejected |
| Engines that differ by more than 1e-12 in a month | failed |
| An interval above the minimum effect with every subperiod positive | successful |
| An interval below the minimum effect | null |
| An interval that spans the minimum effect, a nonpositive subperiod, or zero variance | inconclusive |

A refused read returns the adapter's `Refused`, and the command exits 1 without a package. The frozen window rules out the evaluation's other invalid inputs.

The reference case reconciles `SAHM` with `SAHMREALTIME` at the cutoff through `reconcile_series` and saves the record and the `SAHMREALTIME` vintage. The package therefore carries the baseline outputs that [Q003](open-questions.md#q003-what-independent-baseline-defines-agreement) names. An unexplained mismatch rejects the run. An invalidation of the `SAHMREALTIME` vintage withdraws the reference package, because its Phase 1 claim rests on that agreement. `reconcile package` still supports only the replay profile, so P02's box for choosing package metrics by profile stays open.

D053 left the 20 excluded months for P04 to accept or reject. They were accepted as the reconciliation window of the scoped Phase 1, each with its recorded reason. An agent accepted them under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026, and the researcher accepted them on 23 October 2026.

This amends [D023](#d023-render-research-reports-only-from-verified-packages). The report now reads the hash-verified bytes of each attempt's `pilot-evaluation` artifacts through `read_artifact`. It renders the procedure version, the parameters, the verdict and reason, the trials counted and confidence, the mean and incremental intervals with standard errors, and each subperiod estimate. Floats print with `repr`, so the text round-trips. A package without that role renders the same bytes as before. A malformed artifact makes `report` exit 1 before it writes any output.

The live check ran on 23 October 2026 on a fresh data root, pulled and ingested with the P01 commands. An agent validated and approved it for this check only, and its attestation says it is not the researcher's validation. `quant-research reference run` took 79 seconds for the four cases. All four packages verified, and each case ended as expected.

- `sahm-rule/v1` recorded inconclusive (`spans_minimum`). The incremental return over buy-and-hold was 0.00115 a month, with a standard error of 0.00075 and an interval from -0.00033 to 0.00263 at confidence 0.95. The mean return was 0.00775 a month. The subperiod estimates were -0.00063, -0.00040, 0.00498, and -0.00064. The strategy switched 26 times and held T-bills in 176 of 797 months. vectorbt agreed with `plain` within 3.5e-16, in December 1979. The audit found no breach. The reconciliation matched 780 of 800 months, excluded 20 with recorded reasons, and found no mismatch. A separate `reconcile series` run at the wall-clock time gave the same counts.
- `sahm-rule-current-vintage/v1` was rejected with 797 of 797 decisions in breach.
- `sahm-rule-fixed-lag/v1` was rejected with 2 breaches, November 2025 (read at 21 November) and December 2025 (read at 17 December).
- `never-bills/v1` recorded null with an incremental estimate of exactly 0.

An inconclusive verdict on the real data is an accepted outcome, as the [pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#accept-the-local-pilot-as-a-scoped-phase-1) says. An agent accepted the scoped Phase 1 result under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026. The researcher reviewed this decision and accepted the frozen file, the 20 excluded months, and the scoped Phase 1 result on 23 October 2026. That acceptance completes Phase 1 for the local pilot on public data and vectorbt and closes [Q003](open-questions.md#q003-what-independent-baseline-defines-agreement). It does not accept the firm frameworks, per [D075](#d075-run-the-local-pilot-track-ahead-of-the-master-plan-phase-order).

These limits are deliberate:

- The intervals assume independent, identically distributed errors, so the inference stays provisional, as D015 says.
- All history is development data that the researcher and the models have seen.
- The audit trusts adapter-made read records.
- `run` saves a one-trial package. P15 adds the history that a search's trial count needs.
- The implementation digest covers the source files at run time, so a later code change gives a new specification.

## D056. Make the workflow the single accounting owner with durable call receipts

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P05 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts-p05). It removes two limits of [D051](#d051-own-the-research-loop-and-call-subscriptions-as-tool-disabled-completions). The campaign runner and the provider gateway kept separate ledgers, and the ledger had no way to record usage that a provider left unreported.

The [campaign runner's](../src/quant_research/execution.py) workflow is now the only writer of a campaign's resource ledger, as [D042](#d042-run-each-campaign-as-one-temporal-workflow-that-owns-its-logs) requires. The [gateway](../src/quant_research/providers.py) writes only its call log. `LedgerStore`, `Ledger.is_open`, the gateway's charge, its reconciliation at open, and `ChargeRefused` are deleted, because the gateway was the store's only caller. The event codec stays in [accounting](../src/quant_research/accounting.py) as `dump_ledger` and `load_ledger`. A dump holds the budgets and every event as canonical JSON. A load replays each event through the ledger's own methods and requires the same bytes back. A closed run's `Record.ledger` carries the dump, read in parts like the acceptance and steering logs, per [D073](#d073-bound-the-campaign-runners-per-event-cost-and-payloads). `Summary` still carries only the compute seconds.

A `Job` declares `dispatches`, the number of models one attempt may send content to, which is its role's chain with fallbacks. Before an attempt's activity runs, the workflow mints one call ID per model with `workflow.uuid4()` and reserves the attempt's estimate. Beside the declared compute seconds, the estimate now holds `model_calls` equal to `dispatches` and 600 `wall_seconds`. The IDs form a `Lease` with the attempt ID and a fence, which is the number of the activity that holds the lease. A body receives a dispatch function as its third argument. It calls the gateway under that lease and takes no lease of its own, so a body cannot send under another attempt's IDs. The gateway sends model i only under call ID i and mints none, so an activity that outlives its timeout can spend only what its attempt reserved. A chain longer than its lease raises before any adapter runs. A body must build its requests only from its revision and argument, never from the lease, because reuse requires the same request.

The call log is now at schema version 2 and holds three kinds of entry. A claim is stored before its adapter runs. It names the call ID, the attempt, the fence, the role, the model record, the policy version, and a SHA-256 digest of the request's texts, schema, and derived restrictions, so a reused reply keeps the restrictions of the request it answered. Each claim also stores whether the adapter that sends it bills by subscription, and a token minted for it, so no two claims hold the same bytes. The shared write-once helper reports success when a file already holds identical bytes, and the token stops two writers from both owning one claim. The adapter runs only when the log stored this holder's claim first. The call's record follows with its outcome, its usage, and the reply text of a completed or malformed reply. `Closed` ends an attempt's lease, and no later claim may name the attempt. `CallLog.append` returns the entry its key already holds instead of raising, so every writer learns which entry won, and the first record of a call ID is final. A record unlike its claim, a refused record of a claimed call, and a claim after its attempt closed still raise.

The gateway decides each call ID from a fresh read of the log, and reads again after each write:

- A stored record is reused, and nothing is sent. A record for another attempt, role, model, or request raises.
- A claim without a record under a later fence means that a later activity holds the lease. One under the same fence means that another call of this activity is sending it. Both raise.
- A claim without a record under an earlier fence belongs to an activity that will never use its reply. The gateway seals it as `unknown` and never sends it again.
- An unclaimed call ID of a closed attempt raises.
- Otherwise the policy check runs. A denied model is recorded as refused, and an admitted one is claimed and sent.

A model attempt whose activity times out resumes as the same attempt under the same lease, in a new activity `<job>/<n+1>`. Each activity of a model attempt runs with the rest of the attempt's 10 minutes, floored to whole seconds, as its schedule-to-close timeout. A crash before the claim sends the call once on resume. A crash while the adapter runs, or before its record is stored, leaves a claim without a record, which the resume seals. A stored record is reused without a request. After the attempt's last activity, a `close` activity, which Temporal retries until it succeeds, stores `Closed`, seals the lease's claims that have no record, and returns one receipt per sent call as canonical text. The workflow then charges the attempt's wall time and each receipt under its call ID, and only then releases the attempt. No reserved model call or wall time is freed before every dispatch of its attempt is settled. Compute jobs keep D042. A lost report releases the attempt as failed, and the next attempt is fresh. An interrupted model attempt therefore shows as several activity links for one acceptance attempt, and the timed-out link stays visible.

`Usage.unreported` names the fields that a provider left unreported, from input tokens, output tokens, and cost. Each named field holds the reported lower bound, or zero when nothing was reported, so the undercounted subscription tokens of D051 fit as well. Sums add the amounts and join the names, so limit checks and exact arithmetic work on the known part. Each `CostLine` counts, per field, the charges that left it unreported. A sealed call is charged one model call with all three fields unreported. When its claim was sent on a subscription route, its cost is known to be zero, whichever worker seals it. The gateway records one model call per sent call, whatever the adapter reports. It records no cash cost when the route's adapter declares `subscription`, so an API-equivalent price never becomes cash spent. `ScriptedProvider(usd_per_token=None)` scripts such a route.

`ResourceLimits` gains `max_model_calls`, `max_wall_seconds`, `max_input_tokens`, and `max_output_tokens`. Model calls and wall time are hard limits. Each model attempt reserves them before its activity runs, and the ledger refuses a reservation that would pass either. Token limits are observed. Nothing reserves tokens, so the same refusal rule refuses the next attempt only once the known sum passes a limit, and one attempt can overshoot it. Wall time is a model attempt's capped occupancy of its 10-minute deadline. It is measured in workflow time as the lesser of 600 seconds and the time from the attempt's first schedule to its last settle. It includes workflow processing delay below the cap, and it excludes an adapter that keeps running after Temporal times out its activity. Compute jobs reserve and charge no wall time. `start` refuses a job whose `dispatches` exceed `max_model_calls`, and an undeclared limit counts as zero, so a revision saved before P05 cannot dispatch. It also refuses a job that dispatches more than 16 models, because the close activity returns every receipt of an attempt in one payload, an external job that dispatches, and a dispatching campaign whose `max_wall_seconds` is below 600. With a limit of three model calls, a campaign of four one-model jobs ends `budget_exhausted` after three provider requests and three charged calls, and the fourth job's activity is never scheduled.

An adapter reports a usage cap as `RateLimited`, which the gateway records with the outcome `rate_limited`. The gateway then moves to the next fallback, as after a provider error, because a cap on one subscription says nothing about another provider. An attempt is rate-limited when one of its receipts is `rate_limited` and none is `completed`. The runner records a reported rate-limited attempt as `inconclusive`, with the detail `rate_limited: ` followed by the body's detail. It releases the attempt as `inconclusive` and never accepts it. Under D016, an inconclusive release holds no failure slot. A rate-limited attempt whose report was lost records no completion, as D042 keeps for any interrupted attempt, and the runner still releases it as `inconclusive`. The next attempt dispatches under new call IDs. Pausing on a rate limit belongs to P12.

A revision file uses schema version 2 exactly when the revision sets one of the added limits, and it omits each added limit that is unset. Any other revision keeps version 1 and main's bytes, so its revision ID and its children's parent links hold. `parse_file` accepts both versions. It requires that the parsed revision's stored form equals the file's bytes. That one check gives each revision one byte string, and it replaces the check that `generated_code` is stored only as true. A version 2 child of a version 1 parent lists `limits` as its change. The runner still refuses a steer that changes limits.

When it charges a receipt, the workflow catches only `InexactUsage`, the ledger's error for arithmetic that cannot stay exact. It then charges the call again with a cost of zero marked unreported, and the call log keeps the reported amount. Temporal cannot refuse an activity result, and a raise in workflow code fails every workflow task, which is the case that D042's report handling already covers. Every other ledger error still raises. An agent decided on this defensive branch under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026.

Two independent architecture sketches, a cross-judge, and two Codex reviews shaped this design. Four alternatives were rejected:

- A lease as a second ledger reservation without a failure slot, with its own settle event. It adds an event kind and lets a charge name a lease instead of an attempt.
- Receipts in the attempt's report, with a recovery activity only after a lost report. Receipts would then reach the workflow by two paths.
- An unknown value in each usage field. A sum with an unknown value loses the known lower bound, so an observed token limit could never refuse.
- A new acceptance attempt that inherits the lost attempt's lease. Its charges would land on an attempt that did not reserve them, and the lease's owner would stay open while later attempts run.

[The ledger tests](../tests/test_accounting.py) cover unreported sums and counts, an observed token limit, `InexactUsage`, and the dump round trip with 14 kinds of damaged bytes. [The gateway tests](../tests/test_providers.py) cover a crash before the claim, after the claim, after the record, and after the body returned. They also cover a late activity after a resume and after close, two threads on one fence that make one adapter call, a request that differs from its claim, a chain longer than its lease, a subscription route, and a rate limit that continues the chain. [The campaign tests](../tests/test_campaigns.py) open a revision graph written by main's code under its original IDs and round-trip every subset of the added limits. [The runner tests](../tests/test_execution.py) cover the dispatch limit, a worker killed after a stored reply, a lost report whose claim has no record, a rate-limited attempt with a `max_attempts` of one, wall time, and the `start` refusals.

The live check on 22 October 2026 ran a local dev server and a campaign with one one-model job and `ScriptedProvider`. The job's body kept its activity open after the reply. A separate worker process stored the reply and was killed with SIGKILL. A restarted worker in another process opened the same call log. The ledger charged the call once, the restarted provider received no request, and the log held one claim. The attempt shows two activity links under one attempt ID, `q/1` timed out and `q/2` completed.

This changes four earlier records. [D016](#d016-account-for-resources-in-an-append-only-ledger) loses `LedgerStore` and `is_open`, and its usage gains wall seconds and unreported fields. In [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy), the gateway no longer charges a ledger or checks that an attempt is open before each model, and a closed lease takes over that check. Its call log keeps reply texts and stores no refused charge. In [D042](#d042-run-each-campaign-as-one-temporal-workflow-that-owns-its-logs), bodies take a dispatch argument, model attempts resume, a close activity follows each model attempt, and the record carries the ledger. This entry resolves the D051 limits on one accounting owner and on unreported usage.

These limits are deliberate:

- Every worker that polls a dispatching campaign's queue must open its gateway on the same call log directory, because a resume or a close can run on any of them. Keeping that pairing is the operator's job, as D041 already leaves it. A worker without a gateway stalls such a campaign at its close activity.
- A worker that crashes again and again can open many resume activities within one attempt's 10 minutes, and each one grows history toward the D073 limit.
- An adapter that is still sending when a resume seals its claim finishes its send. The log refuses its late record, and that call stays charged as unreported. No second send happens.
- A body must call `dispatch` off the event loop, for example through `asyncio.to_thread`, or its heartbeat stops.
- Cancelling an attempt does not reach an adapter that a body runs in a thread, so the adapter can keep sending after its call is sealed. [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters) bridges Temporal cancellation to the adapter's event for model calls that `ask_model` runs, and `CampaignRun` bodies keep this limit.
- The call log keeps reply texts under the restrictions of their requests, with no retention rule. [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters) moves sent and reply texts into content-addressed transcripts, under the same limit.
- Workflow histories started before this change do not replay under the new workflow code. No campaign ran outside tests.
- Every gateway decision reads the whole call log again, so one call costs several full replays.

## D057. Call Claude Code as a tool-disabled completion behind a local first-turn gate

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P06 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#call-claude-code-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-p06). [quant_research.claude_adapter](../src/quant_research/claude_adapter.py) implements the [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy) `Adapter` over the installed, unmodified `claude` executable under the researcher's own sign-in, as [D051](#d051-own-the-research-loop-and-call-subscriptions-as-tool-disabled-completions) selects. It accepts only Claude Code 2.1.301, the installed version. Its route is the provider `claude-subscription`. Its policy admits only material with no restriction labels, and it records that the provider retains content. The adapter declares `subscription`, so the gateway records no cash cost for its calls, per [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts).

Each call runs `claude auth status` first and requires a `claude.ai` sign-in to the first-party API. It then starts one stream-json session in a new empty directory and in its own process group. The session runs with `-p`, `--tools ""`, `--safe-mode`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--setting-sources ""`, `--disable-slash-commands`, `--no-session-persistence`, a fixed system prompt, and the model that the request's model record names. Both processes receive only `HOME`, `PATH`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, and `TMPDIR` from the caller's environment, so every variable that quarry blanks is gone. Eight values are forced. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1` stops telemetry and surveys. `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES=2` bounds the CLI's own retries. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_REFUSAL_FALLBACK=1` and `CLAUDE_CODE_NO_MODEL_FALLBACK=1` are the switches that the binary reads to stop rerunning a turn on another model. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_MENTIONS=1` stops the CLI from reading a host file that an `@path` in the request names. `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1`, and `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1` are the values that the binary sets for its own isolated children.

The adapter first sends the control request `get_hooks_listing`, which the CLI answers without a user turn, and requires an empty hook list. The CLI reports its tool and MCP inventory only in a `system/init` event, and only once a user turn arrives. The adapter's first turn is therefore the fixed text `/cost`. With slash commands disabled, the CLI answers it locally as unavailable and reports zero model turns, so no model sees it. The adapter compares the whole inventory in every `system/init` with the expected one. It writes the request only when they are equal and the first turn stayed local. The expected inventory has no tools, MCP servers, or skills. It has only the binary's two built-in plugins, `cc-plugin-agents-md` and `cc-plugin-plugin-authoring`, each with the path `builtin` and the source `<name>@builtin`, so a user plugin with the same name differs. It has no API key source, the output style `default`, version 2.1.301, and the requested model. Any difference fails the call before the request is written. The real turn's own `system/init` is checked the same way, which detects a change after the write but cannot prevent it. A `system` event in the real turn with the subtype `model_fallback`, `model_refusal_fallback`, or `model_consent_fallback` also fails the call, so a reply never comes from a model other than the requested one.

Probes at 2.1.301 on 23 October 2026 shaped the session. None of them made a model call.

- `--json-schema` adds a `StructuredOutput` tool to the inventory. That contradicts D051's rule that every tool is disabled and P06's pass condition that the inventory lists no tools. The adapter does not pass it, which amends the flag list in the plan. For a request with a schema, it appends the schema's keys and JSON types to the system prompt. The gateway's exact parse stays the only judge of the reply.
- Without `--disable-slash-commands`, a user message whose text block starts with `/` runs a CLI command, even after another text block. With it, every slash command answers locally as unavailable. A real turn that the CLI answers locally reports zero model turns and fails, so a request that the CLI reads as a command is never sent.
- Under these flags, a user-level MCP server in a temporary home's `.claude.json` does not reach `system/init`. `--setting-sources ""` alone also keeps it out, and without the three isolation flags it appears with the source `user`. `--setting-sources ""` also removes the user's installed plugins, which `--safe-mode` alone still lists.
- Because the server never loads under these flags, the plan's live MCP check cannot see a refusal. The check therefore also runs the adapter through a wrapper that drops `--strict-mcp-config`, `--safe-mode`, and `--setting-sources`. The gate must then refuse before the request is written.
- `--safe-mode` also keeps out a server that `--mcp-config` names.
- `get_hooks_listing` answers before any user turn. Under these flags it lists no hooks. Without them, a hook in the user's settings appears with the source `userSettings`. No managed settings exist on this machine, and pointing the CLI's environment at a managed settings file did not load one, so no probe has shown a managed hook in the listing.
- Every `system/init` under these flags reports the output style `default`. For the same reason, no probe has shown a managed output style, which would add instructions to every request.

The adapter maps the real turn's events to the gateway's replies. An assistant error of `rate_limit` returns `RateLimited`. Any other assistant error, a result marked as an error, a turn the CLI answered locally, a malformed or truncated stream, the deadline, or a failed sign-in check returns `Failed`. A cancel returns `Cancelled`. Otherwise the result text is the reply, and the gateway judges it against the schema. Reported tokens, with cache reads and writes counted as input, are lower bounds marked unreported, as D051 and D056 require. The adapter does not retry, because a second send under one call ID would be a second model call that the gateway records as one. The CLI's own retries are bounded at two, the call's deadline defaults to 300 seconds, and the gateway's fallback chain retries across models. A cancel or the deadline kills the process group at once. After a reply, the adapter closes stdin, waits up to 2 seconds for the CLI to exit, and then kills the group.

Anthropic's [data usage page](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/data-usage), read on 23 October 2026, says that consumer plans keep Claude Code data for 5 years when the account allows model improvement and for 30 days when it does not. The provider retains content under either setting, so `retains` is true without reading the account's setting. The setting decides only whether the content trains future models.

The plan gates P06's merge on the researcher's review of the isolation evidence. An agent decided this gate under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, and P06 merged before the researcher's approval. The agent accepted the gate, the exact inventory comparison, and the flag and environment evidence above as isolation, because no request content reaches the CLI before the gate passes. After the merge, the researcher approved the isolation and this decision's amendments to the plan on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation. The amendments drop `--json-schema`, add `--setting-sources ""` and `--disable-slash-commands`, and rewrite the MCP check.

The MCP part of the live check ran on 23 October 2026 against the real CLI with only `claude auth status` replaced by a stub, because the CLI's sign-in on this machine had expired. With the user-level MCP server in a temporary home, the adapter under its own flags saw no MCP server in either `system/init` and wrote the request, which the CLI then failed at the expired sign-in before any model request. Through the wrapper that drops the three isolation flags, `system/init` listed the server with the source `user`, and the adapter failed the call with only `/cost` written. Every process group was gone after each call. A rerun after the hooks check was added gave the same results. Under the adapter's flags, the real CLI answered `get_hooks_listing` with no hooks, and the stripped run wrote only that request and `/cost`. With the sign-in restored, both runs that used a temporary home stopped at the sign-in check before any session started, because the CLI has no sign-in under a temporary home. The platform must not copy the sign-in into one, so the stubbed runs stay the only evidence for a user-level server. Two signed-in runs with the real home then tested a server that `--mcp-config` adds. Under the adapter's flags, both `system/init` events listed no MCP server, and the call completed. Through a wrapper that dropped only `--safe-mode`, `system/init` listed the server with the source `dynamic`, and the adapter failed the call with only the hooks request and `/cost` written. These runs spent 2 more calls, so the live check spent 9 of the plan's 10.

The rest of the live check ran on 23 October 2026, after `claude auth login` restored the sign-in, and P06 is a go. `claude auth status` reported a `claude.ai` sign-in to the first-party API. The run spent 7 of its 8 budgeted calls: two bare `claude -p` calls and five adapter calls, one of which stopped at the sign-in check. In each of the four adapter sessions, the CLI answered the hooks listing with no hooks, and both `system/init` events listed no tools, MCP servers, or skills, version 2.1.301, and `claude-opus-5-5`. Two text calls completed with the reply `OK`. They recorded 609 and 611 input tokens and 4 output tokens each, as lower bounds. A structured call asked for a string, a float, and a boolean, and the gateway accepted the one JSON object it returned, `{"city": "Paris", "population_millions": 2.15, "certain": false}`. Its second material began with `/`, and the model still answered it. Its record names the runtime `claude 2.1.301`, the resolved model `claude-opus-5-5`, and 401 input and 133 output tokens as lower bounds. A cancel 4 seconds into a call, while the model turn was streaming, returned `Cancelled`, and that call's process group was gone. Every other process group was also gone after its call. The two text calls took 2.35 and 2.93 seconds, against bare calls of 4.57 and 2.81 seconds. Model time varies too much for two pairs to bound the adapter's overhead. A separate run therefore timed the adapter's own work before the request ten times under the live sign-in, with no model call. The sign-in check, the session's start, the hooks listing, and the `/cost` turn took a median of 0.57 seconds and at most 0.91 seconds. Closing the session took at most 0.11 seconds. That work includes the CLI's own start, which a bare call also pays, so it is an upper bound on the added latency, and it stays under the 2-second limit. P11 may count P06, and [D062](#d062-resolve-a-campaign-revision-into-its-run-authority) now accepts `claude-subscription`.

These limits are deliberate:

- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) adds fixed-token reasons to adapter failures and schema 4 call records. Historical records keep an unknown reason.
- Under D061, a record names the runtime `claude 2.1.301`. A completed or malformed reply also names, as its resolved model, the model that the role requested, because the adapter refuses any session whose `system/init` reports another model and any turn that reports a model fallback. A failed, cancelled, or rate-limited call names no resolved model, because its turn ended before the result could rule out a fallback.
- The adapter reads the version from the session's own `system/init` before it writes the request, not from a separate `claude --version` run.
- The gateway records one model call for a call that the adapter refused before writing the request, although no model saw it.
- Without `--json-schema`, the CLI does not enforce the reply format. A reply that a model wraps in prose or a code fence is `malformed_output`.
- After a reply, the adapter does not read stdout while it waits for the CLI to exit. A CLI that writes more than a pipe buffer after its result blocks until the 2-second wait ends.
- The binary's built-in agents stay listed in `system/init`. They run only through the Agent tool, which the session lacks.
- The unit tests drive a fake executable that replays event shapes taken from the CLI's real stream. The live check observed successful and cancelled model turns, but no usage cap.
- The fallback switches, the file mention switch, and the fallback events come from the binary's code. No probe has seen a fallback or a file mention.
- With the file mention switch on, the CLI does not read a file that an `@path` in the request names, but it still adds a note that names the path. The model has no tool to follow it.
- The gateway records no cash cost for a subscription call, but Anthropic bills some Claude Code calls at API rates. A paid plan with [usage credits](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-manage-usage-credits-for-paid-claude-plans) turned on pays for calls after an included limit runs out, and a [usage-based Enterprise plan](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11845131-use-claude-code-with-your-team-or-enterprise-plan) pays for every call. The CLI's `rate_limit_event` carries `isUsingOverage` and `overageStatus`, but only after the call is served. `claude auth status` reports a `subscriptionType` whose values no probe has seen. The gateway owns subscription billing, and P10 owns that contract, so [Q005](open-questions.md#q005-which-infrastructure-and-provider-assumptions-are-correct) holds the answer instead of an adapter check. On 23 October 2026 the researcher decided not to buy usage credits and confirmed that auto-reload is off, so this route's calls cannot be billed while the balance stays empty and auto-reload stays off.
- The adapter marks the tokens unreported once the request is queued, before its first byte reaches the CLI. A cancel in that window reports tokens for content that no model saw, which overstates rather than understates the call.

## D058. Call Codex through a fresh app-server in an isolated home

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P07 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#call-codex-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-p07). It gives the pilot its Codex route under [D051](#d051-own-the-research-loop-and-call-subscriptions-as-tool-disabled-completions). The live check passed, so the Codex route is a go, and P11 may activate it.

[The Codex adapter](../src/quant_research/codex_adapter.py) implements the [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy) `Adapter` over `codex app-server`. It accepts only `codex-cli 0.162.1`, the installed version and the plan's pin, which `initialize` reports. Each call starts one app-server, sends one turn, and stops it. The adapter declares `subscription`, so the gateway records no cash cost.

The user's own Codex home is unsafe for a completion. On this machine its configuration approves every command, grants full disk access, turns on live web search, and enables hooks, about 40 plugins, and 3 MCP servers. Codex also adds the home's own `AGENTS.md` to every prompt, and no setting found at 0.162.1 turns that off. Each call therefore runs in a temporary `CODEX_HOME` whose only entry is `auth.json`, a symbolic link to the user's sign-in file. The adapter never opens that file. Codex 0.162.1 saves `auth.json` by truncating and rewriting it in place, so a token refresh writes through the link, and a logout removes only the link. A test on a scratch file confirmed both. The keyring and automatic credential stores delete `auth.json` after each save, so the adapter forces the file store. The process also loses every environment variable whose name starts with `OPENAI_` or `CODEX_`.

The adapter disables quarry's 32 features, among them the shell, browser use, computer use, apps, plugins, standalone web search, and multi-agent tools. It also turns off web search, project documents, and Codex's permission, apps, collaboration, environment, and skills instructions. A one-model catalog entry for the requested model sets no shell, direct tool mode, and no extra tools. Its system prompt is the adapter's own, in place of Codex's agent prompt. The thread is ephemeral, with no environments, no dynamic tools, a read-only sandbox, and no model fallback.

Before it sends any material, the adapter runs these checks in order. Any miss ends the call as `Failed` before `turn/start`.

- `initialize` reports Codex 0.162.1.
- `account/read` reports a ChatGPT account.
- `config/read` echoes each override, and it reports no OpenAI base URL, no `openai` provider entry, no MCP server, and the default ChatGPT backend. It does not echo the two `tools` switches, so those go unchecked.
- `experimentalFeature/list` reports none of the disabled features as enabled.
- `thread/start` confirms the requested model, the `openai` provider, no environments, and no instruction files.
- `mcpServerStatus/list` lists no server at all.

No app-server method reports the tools a model receives. The live check therefore sent one synthetic turn, with the adapter's exact flags and catalog, to a local mock Responses server under a dummy key. Codex's request held no tool, in `tools` or in an `additional_tools` input item, and its input was only the system prompt and the user's text. The same turn without the adapter's flags and catalog carried 13 tools, among them `exec`, `spawn_agent`, `clock`, and `sleep`, and six instruction messages. `experimentalFeature/list` still reports `unified_exec` as enabled, so the shell's absence rests on the disabled shell feature and the catalog entry, which the capture covers.

The turn sends each cleared text as one input. With a schema, it adds an `outputSchema`, which Codex sends as a strict JSON schema. The adapter maps the turn's end as follows.

- A completed turn with a final message is `Replied`, with the message's text unchanged.
- A string `usageLimitExceeded` or `rateLimitExceeded`, from the turn, an unretried error, or a request's error, is `RateLimited`. An object-shaped error keeps its existing `Failed` classification ([D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority)).
- Cancellation is `Cancelled`.
- Everything else is `Failed`. This covers a failed check, `unauthorized`, a failed turn, a turn with no message, any item other than a message or reasoning, any request from the server, a `model/rerouted` notice, and a turn past its deadline, 600 seconds by default.

The last reported token totals are recorded as lower bounds, with input and output tokens marked unreported, because Codex retries inside a turn and subscription usage need not count every inference. A call that ends before `turn/start` reports no usage. The adapter logs each unreplied call. [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) also preserves a fixed-token reason in the call record.

A call ends by closing the app-server's input. The app-server then exits on its own, within 25 milliseconds in a probe and about 0.4 seconds after a turn. Only a server that still runs after 2 seconds gets SIGTERM, and SIGKILL 1 second later, sent to its process group, because the `codex` command is a Node shim whose child is the native binary. A signal never ends a normal call, because a process killed while Codex rewrites `auth.json` could leave the user's sign-in file empty.

`policy(retains=...)` gives the `codex-subscription` provider an empty label ceiling, so only unlabeled material passes. App-server does not report the account's data controls, so the campaign setup states `retains`. On 23 October 2026 the researcher said that the ChatGPT account does not train on what it receives. That answers training, not retention, so the pilot keeps `retains=True`.

OpenAI's [Codex authentication page](https://developers.openai.com/codex/auth) recommends API key authentication for programmatic Codex CLI workflows, such as CI/CD jobs, and calls API keys the recommended default for automation. The pilot keeps the ChatGPT sign-in, as D051 chose, and the researcher confirmed that choice on 23 October 2026. A switch to API keys bills per token and needs the researcher's plan details and consent, as D051 leaves open.

The live check ran on 23 October 2026 on this machine, under the user's signed-in ChatGPT Pro account, with `gpt-6.1-sol`. It spent 8 of its 10 permitted turns, one at a time, and sent only synthetic public text.

- The pre-turn checks passed on every call.
- Three structured calls each returned one JSON object that the gateway accepted. Each recorded 156 input tokens and 47 to 53 output tokens, marked unreported.
- A turn that asked the model to run a shell command, read a file, open a browser, and search the web produced only messages, with no tool item and no server request. The model answered that it could call no tools. The process group held only the Node shim and the native binary.
- A cancellation 5 seconds into a long turn returned `cancelled` 0.50 seconds later, and the process group was gone.
- The sign-in file was unchanged, and no process remained.

The rule for added latency over a bare app-server turn is a failure above 2 seconds per call. A bare turn here used the same isolation without the checks. Across three live pairs, the adapter added a median of 0.43 seconds. Ten alternating runs of the phase before the turn, with no turn, added a median of 0.38 seconds. Their paired differences ranged from -0.83 to 1.51 seconds, at a load average near 300 on 14 cores. `account/read` takes most of the added time, at a median of 0.30 seconds, and every other check takes 5 to 30 milliseconds. The end of a call costs the same on both sides, about 0.4 seconds.

The plan asks the researcher to review the isolation evidence before merge. An agent decided the isolation review under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026. The researcher accepted the Codex route's isolation later that day.

Two independent design sketches, a Codex second opinion, and the probes above shaped this design. Three alternatives were rejected:

- The user's own Codex home with every source disabled. It always injects the home's `AGENTS.md`, and it starts the user's MCP servers before any check can refuse.
- A copy of `auth.json` in the temporary home. A refresh through the copy would rotate the refresh token, and the user's own file would then hold a stale one.
- One long-lived app-server for many calls. It saves about 0.4 seconds per call, but every call would share one process's state.

[The adapter tests](../tests/test_codex_adapter.py) drive a fake app-server that restarts itself as a child, as the Node shim does. They cover success, malformed output, failed runs, cancellation, a deadline, a continuous stream of notices, rate limits, an expired sign-in, a nonempty inventory, a tool item, a server request, a rerouted model, usage that arrives before a request's error, the launch isolation, a refresh through the link, and concurrent calls. The live check's script and evidence are kept outside the repository with the session notes.

This resolves, for Codex at 0.162.1, D051's limit that a capability probe must show structured output with every tool disabled. Claude Code stays with P06.

These limits are deliberate:

- The link lets Codex rewrite the user's real sign-in file. A process killed during a rewrite could empty it, and the adapter signals only a server that ignored the end of its input for 2 seconds.
- Concurrent calls share the sign-in file, and two refreshes at once are untested.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) adds fixed-token failure reasons. Under [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters), it names the runtime `codex 0.162.1`. A completed or malformed reply also names, as its resolved model, the requested slug, because `thread/start` must report that slug and a `model/rerouted` notice fails the call. A failed, cancelled, or rate-limited call names no resolved model, because its turn ended before completion could rule out a reroute.
- Token totals are lower bounds, and the live check did not reach a usage cap or an expired sign-in. Those arrival paths are tested only against the fake.
- The gateway reads a float field only as a JSON number with a decimal point. The system prompt asks for one, and every live reply complied.
- Each call starts a process in a fresh home, which costs about 0.4 seconds before the turn.
- No repository content has reached the subscription yet.

## D059. Render each tool call as gateway material under the restrictions of what it read

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P08 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#give-tool-results-the-restrictions-the-gateway-checks-p08). It builds on [D025](#d025-carry-restrictions-with-material-and-authority-with-the-run), the gateway of [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy), and the agent tools of [D049](#d049-expose-evidence-to-agents-through-typed-tools-bound-to-the-run-context). The design was decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026, after two design candidates, a cross-judge, and a second opinion. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation.

`Call.material` returns the `Material` that the gateway checks: the canonical JSON text a model reads and the call's restrictions. `Toolset.call` and `replay` set the restrictions once. They derive over every readable item the tool read, returned or not, and over a floor. The floor has no labels, approval for the run's own providers, and availability at the run's cutoff. Every tool answers as of the cutoff, so every result is available at it.

| Tool | Restrictions derive over |
| --- | --- |
| `search_corpus` | The source declaration of every readable corpus member that holds a chunk, whatever the query returns |
| `read_dataset` | The dataset's documented access labels and provider approvals, also for a read with no rows |
| `lookup_location` | The source declaration of the located element's occurrence |
