| `read_lessons` | Each returned lesson's restrictions in the bound review log |
| `run_signal` | The dataset read the run ran over, whatever the run's outcome |
| Any refusal | Nothing, so the floor alone |

The floor defines the case that `derive` refuses, a result that read no material. It keeps every allow and deny decision. A provider the run permits gets the same denials with or without it, and a provider the run does not permit is already refused as `provider_not_permitted`, with `provider_not_approved` possibly added beside it. Approval for no provider was rejected, because a model could then never receive a refusal or an empty result.

A search derives over everything it searched, because identifier membership and BM25 statistics read every eligible unit. Restrictions of the returned units alone would let a route learn whether a unit it may not receive matched its query, from whether the result reached it. The plan asked for each returned unit's occurrence restrictions, and this rule covers them and every unit the answer depended on. One readable member that a route is not approved for therefore makes every search unsendable to that route, matching or not. An operator pins a corpus per destination. `retrieval.eligible_restrictions` reads `corpus_members` as `eligible_units` does, and `retrieve`, its records, and its decoder do not change.

`datasets.Supplied` carries the read's restrictions: the documentation's access labels and provider approvals, available at the cutoff. `read` builds them once and also uses them for its clearance check. Its signature, its refusals, and the `ReadRecord` bytes do not change, so `signals.decide`, `worker.run`, and `reconcile` are unchanged. A refusal carries the floor, as every refusal does, so the adapter's catalog-state refusals still reach the model.

`run_signal` now reads the dataset before it starts a container, and a refused read is a refusal before any container starts. The run takes the read's restrictions. Its output digest and every failure reason are functions of the rows, so success and failure carry the same restrictions. Replay uses that read instead of reading again. `worker.run` still repeats the read inside, against the same catalog value and the same hash-checked vintage.

The text names no label, approval, availability, cutoff, context identity, vintage, catalog digest, or memory pin. A read renders exactly the object that `worker.run` writes to `/job/slice.json`, so code a model writes against what it read matches what `run_signal` gives it. A search renders its units, a lookup its ref, occurrence, and location, and lessons their record IDs and fields. A run renders its ID with the signal digest, or with the worker's reason and message. A refusal renders its reason and message. A search returns at most 20 units, the first in the mode's order, and `retrieve` stays the uncapped baseline of [D040](#d040-retrieve-exactly-over-a-pinned-eligible-corpus). A text over 262,144 bytes of UTF-8 is replaced whole by a `result_too_large` notice, which keeps the full result's restrictions so its size cannot reach a route that the result could not. No partial rows are shown, because a clipped series would pass as a whole one.

No restrictions enter any record. The identities a record already holds pin them. A read record names the catalog digest, which covers the documentation's approvals. A unit names its occurrence, whose ID hashes its declaration. The context identity covers the run's providers and cutoff. Replay derives the restrictions again.

On 22 October 2026 the live check pulled and ingested the P01 datasets into a fresh root. It documented `alfred.unrate` and `alfred.sahmrealtime` for `firm-proxy` alone and `french.ff3_monthly` for `claude-subscription` and `firm-proxy`. Their validation attestations name this live check and are not the researcher's validation. It registered the pilot brief for both routes and a synthetic note for `firm-proxy` alone, and published one corpus of the brief and one of both. Under a context that permits both routes, the reads rendered 125,736, 107,744, and 209,720 bytes, each equal to the worker's slice bytes, under the approvals their documentation names. A lexical search of the brief corpus returned 7 units approved for both routes. The same search of the mixed corpus, and an identifier search that matched nothing there, carried `firm-proxy` alone. A refusal carried the floor. A real gateway with scripted adapters sent every material to `firm-proxy`, and it refused each `firm-proxy`-only material on the `claude-subscription` route as `provider_not_approved` without invoking that adapter.

These limits are deliberate:

- A result's restrictions cover what the tool read, not its arguments. A query text or a `run_signal` source is model output, so P12 must derive each tool result together with the reply that proposed the call, on every send and fallback.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) supersedes this increment's use of current declarations on pinned lessons. Admission and returned restrictions now use the same exact prefix.
- The unit cap is a replay input that the record does not carry, so a search record made before this change with more than 20 units no longer replays. No such record persists.
- A read near the byte cap is tens of thousands of tokens, which nothing has measured. P12's prompt bound decides how many fit. A dataset over the cap stays unreadable until a tool can narrow a read.
- P11 must declare the pilot brief and the dataset contracts for every route a session uses, or every search over that corpus is unsendable to the missing route.
- A refusal still shows whether a named dataset exists and is restricted, as D049 allows, until P11 refuses an uncleared dataset as unknown.
- The gateway test builds a real `Gateway`, and P05 changes its ledger in parallel, so whichever merges second adapts one test helper.

## D060. Let a package record no selected trial

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P09 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#let-a-package-record-no-selected-trial-p09). It amends [D006](#d006-keep-development-packages-self-contained), under which every package selected one trial. P17 will write one package when a campaign ends, and a campaign that is cancelled, fails, or finds nothing selects no trial and may hold none. The design was decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after two design candidates converged and a second opinion moved the registry migration into the import transaction. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation.

A package that selects no trial has `trial_id` `None`, and it may supply no trials. Its manifest uses schema version 3, which is version 2 with `"trial_id": null`. Saving writes version 2 when a trial is selected and version 3 only when none is. Every selected package therefore keeps its bytes and digest, and a reader built before this change refuses an unselected package by its version number, as D006 requires. Opening refuses version 3 with a trial ID and versions 1 and 2 without one, so each record has one encoding. `save_package` publishes an unselected package under `unselected/<digest>` in its destination, beside the directories named by trial ID. No trial ID reads `unselected`, so the names cannot collide.

`PackageRecord.selected_trial` raises `PackageError` with "The package selects no trial" when nothing is selected. A reader that can work without a selection branches on `trial_id`. A reader that needs one reads the property, and its command reports that reason.

| Command | A package that selects no trial |
| --- | --- |
| `verify` | Verifies it |
| `inspect` | Prints `lineage_id` and the record's `trial_id` as null |
| `report` | States "The package selects no trial." in the introduction and in place of the question, and lists every supplied trial. With no trials, the trials section says "No trials were supplied. This does not establish that no trial ran." |
| `compare` | Exits 1 with `Comparison failed: Package A at <path> selects no trial; compare needs a selected trial in each package`, naming side A or B |
| `reconcile package` | Exits 1 with `Reconciliation failed: The package selects no trial` |
| `replay --repeat` and `--retry` | Exit 1 with `Replay failed: The package selects no trial` and save nothing |
| `replay --new --history` | Carries the package's trials, exposures, and artifacts into the new package and links the new trial to none of them. When no history package selects a trial, the new trial starts a new lineage. |

The campaign runner's replay job always selects its new trial, so `execution.py` did not change.

The registry's `package.trial` field is now `option<record<trial>>`. An unselected package's row omits the field, as a first attempt's row omits its predecessor. A `None` value would read back absent, and the next import of the same package would refuse it as a changed row. `DEFINE FIELD IF NOT EXISTS` keeps the required type on a database created before this change. The import transaction therefore also runs `IF (INFO FOR TABLE package).fields.trial CONTAINS 'TYPE record<trial>' { ALTER FIELD trial ON package TYPE option<record<trial>> }`. A probe on SurrealDB 3.3.4 measured how it behaves. It migrates an old database, and a row without the field then inserts in the same transaction. It creates nothing on an empty database. A cancelled transaction rolls the change back. Once the field is migrated, it writes nothing, so it adds no commit conflict. An unconditional `ALTER FIELD` or `DEFINE FIELD OVERWRITE` failed a concurrent transaction's commit, so neither is used.

Only the import transaction runs the statement, which every import of a package, an invalidation, a review log, or a run log shares. `load_ledger`, `load_run_log`, and cleanup never migrate, because the last two run outside the retried transaction and a migration there could fail one call. Two first imports into an old database conflict once and retry like any other commit conflict.

The live check on 23 October 2026 created a registry with the registry code of `b68b6bb`, from before this change, and imported P04's reference package `e5087796` with it. The field then read `TYPE record<trial>`. The code at `538b0c9` imported a version 3 copy of the same record that selects no trial, `5065005c`, into the same database, and the field then read `TYPE none | record<trial>`. Importing P04's package again changed nothing. Both packages exported byte-identical, and `quant-research verify` passed on each export. `report` rendered the unselected export, and `compare` refused it with its reason.

These limits are deliberate:

- `evaluation.evaluate` divides by the record's trial count, so it raises on a record with no trials. No caller passes one. P17 must not evaluate a campaign package with no trials.
- `replay --new --history` records its `--reason` nowhere when no history package selects a trial, because no link carries it.
- When a database created before this change refuses an import, the transaction cancels the migration too, so the field stays required until an import commits.
- The migration matches the definition text that SurrealDB 3.3.4 reports. A server that reported the old field differently would skip it, and an unselected import would then fail with a type error rather than store a row.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) refuses a selected trial without attempts through `ReconcileError`, separately from the no-selection refusal.

## D061. Run model calls and tool calls as activities that stop their adapters

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P10 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-runner-activities-p10). It builds on the leases and receipts of [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts) and the rendered tool material of [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read). An agent decided the design under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after two design candidates, a cross-judge, and two second opinions. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation, including the two Codex findings declined on PR 133.

[quant_research.activities](../src/quant_research/activities.py) holds both ends of the activity contract. A worker registers `Activities(gateway, resolve).definitions`, the `model`, `tool`, and `close` activities. `resolve` maps the campaign revision that an activity's input names to the run's `Toolset`, and a model call sends under that toolset's run context, because a `RunContext` cannot cross a Temporal payload. P11 supplies the real resolver and rebuilds the authority after a steer. A resumed activity resolves the revision its own input names, never a later one.

Workflow code calls two functions and schedules these activities no other way. `ask_model` runs one model attempt from its lease to its close. The caller reserves the chain's model calls and the attempt's wall time first. `ask_model` mints one call ID per model, runs activity n under fence n with the rest of the attempt's time as its schedule-to-close timeout, sets a 1-second heartbeat timeout, turns server retries off, and waits for a cancelled activity to finish. It resumes a timed-out activity under the next fence while a second remains. It then closes the lease under `asyncio.shield`, so a workflow cancel that arrives during the close still returns the receipts. It returns `Asked`, which holds the result, the receipts, and the elapsed wall time, and the caller charges both. A stop condition or a workflow cancel makes the result `cancelled`. `call_tool` runs one tool call and returns its material or `failed`.

The model activity runs `Gateway.call` on a daemon thread and heartbeats every 0.2 seconds. The SDK sends a heartbeat at most about every 0.8 heartbeat timeouts, so a cancel reached the activity about 0.8 seconds after the workflow requested it. The activity sets the adapter's `threading.Event` on any cancel. It also sets it at its own deadline, the earliest Temporal deadline minus a 5-second grace, because heartbeats cannot deliver a cancel while the server is unreachable. A probe on SDK 1.34 saw a timeout arrive as a cancel about 0.4 seconds after the deadline, with `not_found` set and `timed_out` unset, so the activity reads no cancellation detail. After setting the event, it waits up to the grace for the adapter to return. A live worker therefore stores the call's `cancelled` record before Temporal times the activity out, and that call ends its attempt instead of resuming. A resume now happens only when the worker died. The outcome set has no timeout, so `Asked` tells a deadline from a provider's cancel only by its elapsed time. An adapter that outlives the grace is logged twice, when it is abandoned and when it ends, and D056's first record still wins. A set event also ends the fallback chain, so an abandoned call that fails late never claims the next model's call ID, and an activity that starts with less than the grace left sets the event before its adapter runs.

Neither activity relays a backing exception. Every exception except a cancel becomes a non-retryable `ApplicationError` with a fixed message and type, `ModelFailed`, `ModelDeadline`, or `ToolFailed`, raised after its handler, so it has no cause or context. The original goes only to the worker log. A test passes each failure through Temporal's failure converter and finds no backing text. P12 must not turn a failure into model material. A floor-only refusal notice was rejected because an exception after a read would carry less than the restrictions of what the tool read.

Each dispatch keeps content-addressed transcripts in the directory `<call log>.transcripts` beside the call log, because the log directory refuses any file that is not a numbered entry. A file is named by the SHA-256 digest of its bytes and written once. The sent transcript is `Request.to_bytes()`, the bytes the request digest already hashes, so the claim's `request_digest` names it and no second digest exists. It is written before the claim. The reply transcript is the reply as one ASCII JSON string, so any text encodes, and it is written before the record that names it. `CallRecord.text` is replaced by `reply_digest`. Every completed reply, sent now or reused, is read back from its transcript and checked against its digest, so a missing or damaged transcript raises and is never sent again. A refused call stores no transcript.

Each record names its provenance. The configured model is the claim's model record, as before. An adapter declares `runtime_version`, the exact runtime it accepts, which the claim stores before the adapter runs. Each reply may report `resolved_model`, the model the provider says it served. A sent record carries both as `Served`, a sealed record carries the claim's runtime and no resolved model, and a refused record carries neither. A record that names another runtime than its claim fails to open. This increment moved the call log to schema version 3 and refused version 2. [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) writes version 4 and reads both versions 3 and 4 without rewriting old entries.

Requests, results, and materials cross payloads as text, as `execution.py` passes text. `Request.from_bytes` reads what `to_bytes` wrote and requires the same bytes back, so a decoded request gives the claim's digest. Each decoded material carries the request's derived restrictions, which is all the gateway reads. `load_result` takes the request the workflow holds, derives an answer's restrictions and values from it, and refuses a record of another request. Materials cross through `dump_material` and `load_material`, because the workflow sandbox cannot decode a native `datetime`.

The live check on 23 October 2026 ran a local Temporal dev server, a workflow that reserves, calls `ask_model`, and charges a ledger, and `ScriptedProvider`. A completed call left its sent and reply transcripts under the digests its record names, and its record named the runtime `scripted` and the resolved model `model-a`. A call that blocked was cancelled from the client. It recorded `cancelled` 0.84 seconds after the cancel, its receipt said `cancelled`, and the ledger charged it under its call ID. A worker subprocess was killed with SIGKILL while a second call blocked mid-send, which left a claim without a record. A restarted worker resumed the attempt as `model/<attempt>/2` after `model/<attempt>/1` timed out on its heartbeat, sealed the claim as `unknown` without sending, and the ledger charged one model call from its receipt with tokens and cost unreported. The restarted provider received no request.

Activity overhead per model call with `ScriptedProvider` was a median of 85 milliseconds, from 75 to 96, over 7 interleaved runs of 20 calls each, against the plan's rule of 300 milliseconds. Each run compared `ask_model` through a dev server with direct `Gateway.call` and `close` pairs in process, which took 13 to 17 milliseconds a call, and every one of the 280 calls on each side completed. Two activity round trips per call, the model call and the close, set the cost. The activities themselves ran for about 57 milliseconds a call. Other sessions held the machine at a load average of about 200 on 14 cores, so the figure is an upper bound.

Two design candidates shaped this entry. The cross-judge preferred thin wrappers that leave lease minting, fences, resume, and the close to each workflow. They were rejected so that the live check and the tests run the code P12 will call, and so that the heartbeat timeout a cancel depends on lives in one place. Also rejected:

- `asyncio.to_thread` for the call, because the default executor joins its threads at shutdown and an abandoned adapter would hang the worker.
- A margin after the Temporal deadline, because a resume would then race the adapter's record.
- Separate `runtime_version` and `resolved_model` fields on the record, because `Served` makes a served refusal unrepresentable.
- Claim-check payloads that pass digests instead of texts, because they would move prompt construction out of workflow code.

This changes four earlier records. In [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts), a model activity on this surface stops its adapter at its deadline and resumes only after its worker died, the call log keeps reply digests, and reply texts move to transcripts. In [D041](#d041-check-outbound-provider-content-against-the-run-and-a-versioned-provider-policy), each call record also names its runtime and resolved model. In [D057](#d057-call-claude-code-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-behind-a-local-first-turn-gate) and [D058](#d058-call-codex-through-a-fresh-app-server-in-an-isolated-home), each adapter declares its pinned runtime and reports the requested model as the resolved one on a finished reply, because its gate fails a call that another model served.

These limits are deliberate:

- `CampaignRun` bodies that dispatch inside the `attempt` activity still do not bridge cancellation to their adapters. P12's workflow uses `ask_model` instead.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) shares one decorated `close` implementation between both workers under the existing name and contract.
- Setting the event is cooperative. An adapter that ignores it keeps sending after its activity gives up. By their own records, the Claude Code adapter kills its process group at once on a cancel, and the Codex adapter signals an app-server that outlives its closed input within 3 seconds, both inside the 5-second grace. Neither has run under these activities.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) adds the fixed-token failure grammar requested by D057 and D058 and preserves this increment's logs and activity results.
- Cancelling a tool call does not stop it, because `Toolset.call` takes no event. A cancelled `run_signal` container finishes in the background.
- The local deadline compares the worker's clock with the server's. A skew above the grace stops a call early or lets Temporal time it out first.
- A request whose materials pass Temporal's 2 MiB payload limit cannot cross, and P12 bounds prompt bytes. No rule caps a stored reply's size. A reply past the limit cannot reach the workflow, but its record is already stored and a resume reuses it unsent, so the close still charges the call once. No test has sent one.
- Transcripts have no retention rule, as D056 already says of reply texts. [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) consolidates their content-addressed write mechanism with sources and public data.
- `tests/test_records.py` and `tests/test_execution.py` changed only where the version 3 records forced it, and the Temporal dev-server fixture moved to `tests/conftest.py` as `temporal`.

## D062. Resolve a campaign revision into its run authority

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P11 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#resolve-a-campaign-into-its-run-authority-p11). It builds on the run context of [D025](#d025-carry-restrictions-with-material-and-authority-with-the-run), the dataset adapter of [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter), the agent tools of [D049](#d049-expose-evidence-to-agents-through-typed-tools-bound-to-the-run-context) and [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read), and the provider verdicts of [D057](#d057-call-claude-code-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-behind-a-local-first-turn-gate) and [D058](#d058-call-codex-through-a-fresh-app-server-in-an-isolated-home). The design was decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after two design candidates, a cross-judge, a Codex second opinion, and an advisor review. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation.

`campaigns.resolve(revision, setup)` returns an `Authority`. The revision supplies the permitted datasets with their intervals, the sources, the model roles, and whether generated code runs. A `Setup` supplies what only the operator may say: the source and dataset stores, the catalog, the review log, the clearances, the cutoff, the provider policies, the memory pin, and the generated-code run log. The authority holds the revision ID, the permissions, a run context, the published corpus, a role version, the policies of the providers that its active roles use, each active role's prompt as gateway `Material`, and a `Toolset` bound to the context. `Authority.record` returns canonical JSON of all of it, with each prompt named by the SHA-256 of its text. Resolution reads files and writes only the write-once corpus record, so it belongs in an activity, never in workflow code. A repeat returns an equal authority. After a steer, resolving the steered revision rebuilds the authority, and nothing is cached.

The run context gains `scope`, the dataset names that the revision permits. `None` leaves a context unscoped, which keeps every context issued before this change, such as those of `reference run` and `reconcile`. The identity includes the sorted scope only when it is not `None`, as it includes the memory pin only when one is set, so every earlier identity holds. `narrow` keeps the scope. Enforcing it in `datasets.read` scopes every reader at once, the tools, `worker.run`, and `signals.decide`, the last of which narrows a campaign context per decision.

`datasets.read` now checks visibility before catalog state. A name that is not a logical name, a name outside the scope, a name outside the catalog, and a dataset whose access labels the run is not cleared for all return `unknown_dataset`, with the message "No dataset this run may read has this name". Only a visible dataset can then return `documented`, `validated`, `revoked`, or `other_adapter`. The `uncleared` reason is deleted. This amends D045, whose uncleared refusal named no label but still showed that a restricted dataset existed. One private gate decides visibility for `read`, `listing`, and `describe`, so the three cannot disagree, and none of them opens a file before the gate passes.

Two agent tools join the five of D049:

| Tool | Returns | Restrictions derive over |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `list_datasets` | Each dataset that `read_dataset` would supply, in name order, with its owner and fields | Each listed dataset's documented labels and approvals |
| `describe_dataset` | The contract units of one visible dataset that the run's corpus holds and its cutoff admits, in order of availability, then of part | The dataset's documented labels and approvals and each returned part's declaration |

`list_datasets` takes no argument and never refuses. `describe_dataset` refuses exactly as `read_dataset` does. Neither rendering names a label, approval, availability, cutoff, context, or catalog digest. A listing record holds the catalog digest and the listed names. A description record holds the catalog digest, the dataset, and its units with their text, as a retrieval record does. Both replay by running again and comparing bytes.

Each contract is package data at `src/quant_research/contracts/<dataset>.json`. It holds ordered parts, each with its own availability, availability evidence, received time, and Markdown text. `datasets.publish_contract` registers each part as its own source occurrence and publishes its text generation. The location `quant_research.datasets/v1/contract/<dataset>` links the occurrence to its dataset. Each part takes the labels and provider approvals that the dataset's documentation in force declares, so a contract reaches exactly the runs and routes that its data reaches. Part 0 holds the structural facts in the plan's areas, and it is available from the earliest availability of any row of the dataset. That declared policy lets every run that can read a row read the dataset's structure. Each later part is one conclusion, available from the date that it became known. A conclusion with no cited earlier date takes the date this project recorded it, which can only hide it from earlier cutoffs. `describe` filters the run's readable corpus members by location, so a conclusion that the cutoff or the clearances hide is simply absent. An agent wrote contracts for `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.sahmrealtime`, `alfred.payems`, `alfred.indpro`, `alfred.awhman`, and `french.ff3_monthly` under the user's autonomy grant, from FRED series metadata and ALFRED and Ken French downloads retrieved on 23 October 2026 and from this record. The researcher approved publishing them on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation. That approval does not review their fields, so each text still says that its review status is unknown. Before the approval, the agent checked that no part's text names a year after the part's availability date, apart from the 2026 retrieval date in its evidence note. Fields that nothing establishes say `unknown`, and each names the operation it blocks.

A campaign's sources are now pinned published generations. A source's locator is the ID of a published text generation, and its digest is that generation's manifest digest, as a corpus pins both. `campaigns.register` registers bytes, publishes their generation, and returns that source. A source whose role is `prompt` holds the instructions of the model role that its name names. Every other source is a corpus member. `campaigns.contract_sources` publishes the contracts of the permitted datasets and returns one `contract` source per part, so a revision pins every contract part that its runs read, and nothing lists them by hand. A frozen revision therefore fixes its corpus. An edited contract file changes no existing revision's corpus ID, so the tool records of a revision keep replaying under a later commit. A steer that changes the permitted datasets runs `contract_sources` again. Resolution refuses a source, prompt or not, that is the contract of a dataset the run cannot read now, or whose restrictions are looser than those the dataset's documentation now declares, because a search would otherwise show that contract. It also refuses a source whose generation's manifest digest differs from the source's digest, so a frozen revision fixes its corpus in any store.

A campaign role's status may now be `active`. Stored revision files already hold the status, so no revision changes its bytes or ID. A role's model and fallbacks are `<provider>/<model>` text, each a key of `model_provenance.MODELS`. That table holds two records, `codex-subscription/gpt-6.1-sol` and `claude-subscription/claude-opus-5-5`, the models of the D058 and D057 live checks. Each has an unknown version and knowledge cutoff, because no published statement is cited. `model_provenance.ACCEPTED` names the provider routes that a live check's go has activated, which are `codex-subscription` and `claude-subscription`. A role version may now be `active`, and its constructor refuses an active version that names a provider outside `ACCEPTED`. Inactive versions keep their version IDs, and `revise` keeps the activation. Resolution skips inactive roles without looking up their models. It builds each active role's assignment from `MODELS`, and it refuses an unknown model or an unaccepted provider. D057 recorded the Claude route's go on 23 October 2026, and a later PR activated the route after the researcher approved it. [Q005](open-questions.md#q005-which-infrastructure-and-provider-assumptions-are-correct) records why its calls cost nothing.

A resolved role version names the campaign, no previous version, the active assignments, and a fixed reason. Equal role sets therefore have one version ID across revisions, and the revision chain is the lineage of role changes, because a child names its parent and lists `model_roles` among its changes. This amends [D012](#d012-record-model-provenance-and-role-versions-as-inactive-data), under which each change creates a version linked to the version it replaces. The context's providers are those of the active chains. The authority's policies are the setup's policies for exactly those providers, and a provider with no policy or two fails resolution.

A prompt template is a source with the role `prompt`, named after its model role. Its whole extracted text becomes the role's `Material` under its declared restrictions, and P12 sends it as the first material of each request to that role. Resolution refuses an active role without a prompt, a prompt whose extracted text does not match the text digest that its generation pins, and a prompt that the run cannot read under its own context, for example one declared available after the cutoff. Prompt sources of inactive roles are ignored, so a steer can activate a role later without touching its sources. The revision ID pins each prompt's generation, and the authority record names each prompt's text digest.

Resolution also refuses a permitted dataset that the catalog does not hold and a source that names no published generation. It refuses a source at a contract location whose revision does not name its part, so every contract a run describes has a part order. It refuses a revision that enables generated code when the setup supplies no `Generated`, and the authority record names the bound container limits. It does not check that each corpus member, listed dataset, and prompt is approved for each active provider. The gateway refuses each such send and records its reason, and the live check verifies the pilot's corpus against the Codex policy instead.

At P11, the permitted intervals rode on the authority and its record, and no read applied them. Those dataset-only semantics remain for frozen legacy contracts. [D081](#d081-enforce-permitted-months-through-pinned-dataset-contracts) adds read-time intervals under pinned monthly mappings. Treating an interval's end as a cutoff would conflate the period that data covers with the time it became available.

On 23 October 2026 the live check pulled and ingested the six contracted datasets into a fresh data root outside the repository for `codex-subscription`. Their validation attestations name this live check and are not the researcher's validation. The check registered the pilot brief for `codex-subscription` at its last commit time and a researcher prompt. It resolved a campaign that permits `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.sahmrealtime`, and `french.ff3_monthly` from 1960 to 2026, with an active Codex researcher role and an inactive Claude critic role, under setup policies for both routes. The printed authority named exactly those three datasets with their intervals. Its corpus held eight generations, the brief and the seven contract parts of the three datasets, and not the prompt. It named one active role, `researcher`, on `codex-subscription/gpt-6.1-sol`, and one policy, Codex's. `list_datasets` named the three datasets. A read of the approved but unpermitted `alfred.payems` rendered the same text as a read of a name that does not exist. Under a 2015 cutoff, `describe_dataset` of `alfred.unrate` omitted the October 2025 conclusion and kept the structure. The Codex policy admitted the listing, the description, a search, a read, and the prompt. A repeat resolve gave identical record bytes. A steered child that swapped `french.ff3_monthly` for `alfred.payems` resolved to the new scope and refused `french.ff3_monthly`, and the parent still resolved to its own record. The script and its output are kept outside the repository with the session notes.

This changes six earlier records. [D012](#d012-record-model-provenance-and-role-versions-as-inactive-data) role versions may be active and link no predecessor when resolved. [D013](#d013-version-campaign-specifications-as-content-addressed-revisions) roles may be active, and a resolvable revision's source locators and digests name published generations and their manifests. [D025](#d025-carry-restrictions-with-material-and-authority-with-the-run) run contexts gain a scope. [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter) checks visibility before status and loses `uncleared`. [D049](#d049-expose-evidence-to-agents-through-typed-tools-bound-to-the-run-context) has seven tools. [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read) resolves its limit that a refusal shows whether a named dataset exists and is restricted.

These limits are deliberate:

- Legacy contracts retain dataset-only authority. [D081](#d081-enforce-permitted-months-through-pinned-dataset-contracts) enforces intervals for mapped contracts.
- A corpus member or dataset that an active route is not approved for makes that route refuse every search or listing at send time, not at resolution. A contract part copies its dataset's approvals when it is published, so a later revocation or stricter documentation makes resolution refuse until a steer pins new parts.
- A permitted dataset whose contract parts the revision does not pin has an empty description.
- A tool-call record that holds the old `uncleared` refusal no longer replays. Nothing calls the tools outside tests, so no such record persists. Worker run logs never stored a refused read.
- A later correction to a `MODELS` record changes the role version ID that the same revision resolves to.
- A caller can still build a `Toolset` from an unscoped context. P12 must build its tools from the authority.
- The bodies of the campaign runner in `execution.py` still receive a context fixed when the worker starts. P12's loop resolves the revision each attempt carries.
- The pilot brief's declaration lives in the live-check script, because the module that owns public registrations is outside P11's files. P13's campaign start owns it.
- The contracts have no review status, and the researcher's approval to publish them does not give them one. A person must review them before a reviewed claim enters a contract version, as the data discovery plan requires.

## D063. Run the platform-owned research loop as one Temporal workflow per campaign

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P12 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-p12). It joins the leases and receipts of [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts), the activities of [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters), and the run authority of [D062](#d062-resolve-a-campaign-revision-into-its-run-authority) into the loop that [D051](#d051-own-the-research-loop-and-call-subscriptions-as-tool-disabled-completions) describes. An agent decided the design under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after three explorers, two design candidates, a cross-judge, an advisor review, and a Codex review. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation.

[quant_research.research](../src/quant_research/research.py) holds `ResearchLoop`, one Temporal workflow per campaign. Its workflow ID is the campaign ID, as `CampaignRun`'s is, so one campaign has one runner of either kind. `start` stores the revision ID and the frozen evaluation rules digest in the memo instead of a job list. A repeated start joins the run only when both match, and a start after the campaign's workflow closed is refused. The workflow is the single writer of its ledger, steering log, step records, and transcript, which it keeps in memory and Temporal rebuilds by replay, as [D042](#d042-run-each-campaign-as-one-temporal-workflow-that-owns-its-logs) requires. It runs on a task queue of its own, because `execution.worker` serves only `CampaignRun` and both modules register an activity named `close`.

The loop's one activity of its own, `opening`, resolves a revision once and returns text: the researcher role's prompt, the role's assignment, the digest of `Authority.record()`, a floor for declared steering text, and a protocol that describes the reply schema and every tool in `agent_tools.TOOLS`. The protocol and the tool names come from history, so a tool added to `agent_tools` cannot change a request that an open campaign rebuilds on replay. The activity refuses a role prompt whose fixed part passes 64 KiB with a second fixed error, so an oversized prompt never crosses a payload. Any other failure becomes one fixed error with no cause.

Each step asks the researcher role for one reply with three text fields: `action`, `arguments` as JSON text, and `rationale`. The reply parses to a tool call, an experiment request, a conclusion, or a stop. Any action other than `experiment`, `conclude`, or `stop` is a tool call, and `Toolset.call` renders an unknown tool or bad arguments as refusal material, so `agent_tools` alone decides which tools exist. A tool call runs under the revision that its ask ran under. An experiment request is refused with fixed text until P15 replaces the one method that refuses it. A conclusion or a stop ends the loop with the reply as the result's restricted material.

A request holds the role prompt first, as D062 requires, then the protocol, then the step entries of the revision it runs under, oldest first. A step's entry is its reply and its result as one material under the restrictions derived over both. A declared steering note is an entry under its declared restrictions. Runner text, the protocol included, takes the role prompt's restrictions, which every request already holds, so the workflow needs neither the cutoff nor the providers. A failed, timed-out, cancelled, or rate-limited ask adds no entry, so no failure reaches a model, as D061 requires. A request's bytes stay within 384 KiB of `Request.to_bytes()`, and the fixed part must fit in 64 KiB, so a request holds a dataset read of the tools' 256 KiB maximum. A result whose escaping costs more, such as text dense with quotes, is withheld. The loop drops the oldest entries for good until a request fits, and an entry too large for the window is replaced by a fixed notice under its own restrictions. When the restrictions of a revision's entries alone pass the bound, no request can fit, and the loop ends inconclusive, naming `prompt`, with no closing ask. Once anything is dropped, each request carries a fixed notice under the restrictions of the prompt and of everything dropped, so a request's restrictions never fall below those of an entry that an earlier request showed. Each revision has its own entries, so text read under a parent's authority never reaches a request under its child.

The loop keeps a conclusion's budget in the ledger. Every ordinary ask is one attempt on one shared run of one trial, and it reserves the chain's model calls and 600 seconds of wall time before it runs. `max_attempts` therefore bounds failed asks across the whole loop. Before the first ask, a standing attempt on a second run of the same trial reserves one more chain and 600 seconds. Ordinary reservations are refused around it, and the closing ask runs under it, so no limit can refuse the closing ask. A refused reservation, the cap of 100 ordinary asks, or 32 MiB of history makes one closing ask, which asks the model to conclude or stop. The loop then ends inconclusive and keeps a conclusion or stop as the closing reply, because the budget ended the session, not the model. Each ask charges its elapsed wall time and each receipt once, under the receipt's call ID, with the `InexactUsage` fallback of D056. Tool compute is observed, like tokens. Nothing reserves it, a tool step charges its measured seconds, and the next reservation is refused once the sum passes `max_compute_seconds`. `start` refuses a revision with no active researcher role, a `max_model_calls` that is undeclared or below two chains, and a `max_wall_seconds` below 1,200.

[Steering](../src/quant_research/steering.py) events may now declare the restrictions of their text. An event stores a `restrictions` key only when it declares them, so every log written before keeps its bytes and schema version 1, and `execution.py`'s calls are unchanged. The key uses the material codec of the provider gateway, and a stored declaration must be in that codec's canonical form. `declared` returns only the events that declared, and it is the loop's only route from steering text to a prompt, so undeclared text never becomes model material. The workflow dates every event itself, at the later of its time and the last event, so no client clock can make an event arrive dated before another. `control` refuses the actor `runner` and the loop never records a resume, so only a researcher ends a runner pause. A declared note must fit the head revision's floor: it must be available by the run's cutoff, approved for every provider of the chain, and labelled only with labels that every chain provider's policy admits within the run's clearances. Otherwise every later request would be refused. A note is refused until the first opening returns. A researcher's cancel or the loop's end abandons an opening in flight, so a hung opening holds neither the loop nor a steer, and the run closes when the loop ends. An actor, a reason, or a declaration holds at most 4 KiB, and a campaign takes at most 256 researcher commands, because updates while paused add history that no step check sees.

A revision's `interventions` now declare checkpoints in two forms. `checkpoint every N steps` pauses the loop as the runner before the ask that follows every Nth ask. `checkpoint before conclude` holds a conclusion or a stop before it ends the loop. A researcher's resume ends the loop, unless a steer or a declared note arrived during the hold, in which case the reply is set aside and the model is asked again. A `rate_limited` ask, one with a `rate_limited` receipt and none `completed`, pauses the loop as the runner, and a resume asks again under a new attempt. A Temporal workflow cancel is recorded in the steering log as a runner cancel, and the loop ends cancelled.

A steer is an update that runs the child's opening before it is applied, so a child that does not resolve is refused at the update instead of ending the loop later. It also refuses a child that changes `limits` or `evaluation`, one whose chain is longer than the held conclusion's, and a steer before the first opening returns or once the closing ask has begun. Later asks run under the child.

`status` returns a `Report` while the loop runs and after it closes: its state, who holds the pause, the head revision and its authority digest, every step record, the limits, and the spend. A step record holds no model text. `record` reads the same report with the steering log and the ledger, in parts after the loop closes, as `execution.record` does. The workflow's result is an `Ending` with the outcome, the reason, the step count, and the final reply.

The workflow templates in `workflows.py` stay unwired. The loop reads none, P19 wraps the loop rather than a template, and their tool names cannot become the `agent_tools` names without a template name rule that admits underscores, which would change every template ID.

[The research tests](../tests/test_research.py) cover each move, the unknown tool, each limit through its closing ask, observed tool compute, the step cap and the history guard with a replay, both checkpoints and the set-aside, the rate-limit pause, the restrictions of every material and the withheld undeclared text, the floor, refused commands and steers that fail no workflow task, a steer under the child alone with a replay, both cancels, a cancel during a hung opening, a steer whose opening is abandoned when the loop ends, a request that cannot fit, a role prompt past a payload, and the start refusals. They also run the scripted campaign of the plan's "You see" box. It searches and reads, pauses at a checkpoint, resumes, and stops at its dispatch limit with an inconclusive result. [The steering tests](../tests/test_steering.py) pin main's bytes for an undeclared log and cover declared round trips and damaged declarations.

The live check on 23 October 2026 pulled `alfred.sahmrealtime` and `french.ff3_monthly` into a fresh data root outside the repository and approved them for `codex-subscription`. The approvals carry an attestation that names this PR's live check and are not the researcher's validation. A `ScriptedProvider` on the Codex route answered, so no subscription call was made. A worker process stored step 2's reply and then hung before reporting it, as a worker that dies at that moment would, and it was killed with SIGKILL. A worker in another process resumed. Step 2's model activity resumed as `model/<attempt>/2` after `model/<attempt>/1` timed out, and it reused the stored reply. Each worker's adapter answered two requests, and the call log held four claims and four records under four call IDs. The campaign concluded after four steps, 11 seconds after the kill. The script and its output are kept outside the repository with the session notes.

The perf check ran five interleaved runs on each side under a load average of about 5 on 14 cores. A light run listed the datasets each step, reached the cap at 101 asks, and left 1,823 history events and 3.8 MB of history. A heavy run read `french.ff3_monthly` each step at the prompt bound, and the history guard ended it at 52 asks with 33.9 MB. The longest workflow task of a live run took 10 to 15 milliseconds. A fresh worker that answered a status query had to replay the whole history in one workflow task, which took 0.29 to 0.31 seconds for a light run and 0.48 to 0.50 seconds for a heavy one, against the plan's rule of 2 seconds. A `Replayer` replay used as much CPU time as wall time, 0.25 and 0.35 seconds, so one core bounds it. The overhead per step was a median of 82 to 86 milliseconds for a light run and 75 to 79 milliseconds for a heavy one, with a maximum of 122 milliseconds, against the rule of 1 second. Of that, 52 to 72 milliseconds is the dev server's wait before each of the three workflow tasks that a step takes. At the cap with a one-model chain, the status payload was 30,512 bytes and the full record 144,464 bytes. A status holds no logs, so its size does not depend on the chain.

Alternatives were rejected:

- An effects port that lets tests drive the workflow class without a server. Its fake activities would check a stand-in for Temporal, and every reader of the loop would learn the port.
- Reserving a step and a conclusion together at each ask. The reserve would not hold against observed limits or a longer steered chain.
- Counting a conclusion from the closing ask as concluded. The plan ends budget exhaustion inconclusive.
- Refusing a runner's resume in the steering fold, which would make earlier valid logs unreadable.
- Reserving tool compute before every ask, which refuses a campaign that could conclude without a tool.
- One read type that carries both logs, because the steering log is not bounded by the step cap.
- Continuing as new, for the reason D073 gives.

This changes five earlier records. [D013](#d013-version-campaign-specifications-as-content-addressed-revisions) intervention points now declare the loop's checkpoints. [D017](#d017-define-research-workflows-as-validated-versioned-templates) templates stay unwired by decision. [D031](#d031-record-campaign-steering-in-an-append-only-log) events may declare restrictions, and a runner records pauses and a cancel. [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters) activities now have a production caller. [D073](#d073-bound-the-campaign-runners-per-event-cost-and-payloads) said the steering log grows only with researcher commands, and in the research loop it also grows with runner pauses, within the step cap and the command limit.

These limits are deliberate:

- The request text, the protocol rendering, the notices, the 384 KiB bound, and the drop rule are code. Changing any of them while a campaign is open fails its replay forever. `activities` decodes a model result before the lease closes, so that failure also leaves the attempt's receipts uncollected. Open campaigns must be drained before such a deploy.
- [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) completes the shared close implementation and makes `execution.start` refuse an open research loop through its existing plan comparison.
- The loop asks one active role, `researcher`. A critic role needs a second assignment.
- A failed or malformed reply adds nothing to the next request, so a model can repeat it until `max_attempts` ends the loop. A rate-limited closing ask ends the loop inconclusive without a pause.
- One resume ends every pause in effect. The report names the actor and reason of the latest.
- A rate-limited ask counts as a step, so the resume after its pause can land on an every-N checkpoint, and the researcher resumes twice.
- A steer during a held conclusion leaves the set-aside entry with the parent revision.
- An opening that hangs instead of failing retries every minute until it returns, a researcher cancels, or the loop ends, and a steer waits for it.
- A steer's revision text has no byte limit, and each steer's opening adds the role prompt to history. Steers while paused are bounded by the command limit and the payload limit, not by the history guard.
- Tool compute is observed, so one tool call can pass `max_compute_seconds` by at most its 5-minute timeout.
- `research.control` takes no time argument, unlike `execution.control`.
- The live and perf checks used `ScriptedProvider`. No subscription has served a research loop yet.

## D064. Search signal programs in a bounded grammar over a chosen ALFRED universe

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P14 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#define-the-search-grammar-and-its-universe-p14). It extends the operators of [D053](#d053-evaluate-signal-programs-per-decision-and-reconcile-the-sahm-series-with-fred). An agent chose the search universe below under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026, and the researcher approved it on 23 October 2026. The grammar's parameter ranges, its size limit, and its three leak definitions, including the vintage axis that lets a program express a read past its decision's vintage, were also decided by an agent. The researcher approved them on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation, and in a direct reply to the P14 agent.

[quant_research.signals](../src/quant_research/signals.py) adds `Lag`, `Ratio`, `Not`, `And`, and `Or` to D053's `Series`, `TrailingMean`, `TrailingMin`, `Subtract`, and `Threshold`, and gives `Series` a `vintage` field. A program is a number or a condition. A field that takes a number refuses a condition, and the reverse, so a boolean never feeds arithmetic. `Lag` takes its source's value a number of months before the evaluated month. `Ratio` divides one number by another, and `Not`, `And`, and `Or` combine conditions. A difference over time is `Subtract(x, Lag(x, k))`. A comparison of two numbers is `Threshold(Subtract(a, b), 0)`, and strictly below is `Not(Threshold(...))`. The grammar therefore has no separate difference, comparison, or maximum operator.

Each new operator follows D053's null convention. `Lag` is null when the earlier month is null. `Ratio` is null when either side is null or the denominator is zero. `Not`, `And`, and `Or` are null when any operand is null, so false and null give null, not false. `observe` still returns the latest month whose whole reach is present and whose value is not null. A null at the latest month therefore falls back to an earlier month, and each decision records its observation month, so a search can see how old its value is.

`Series(dataset, vintage=k)` reads the vintage that the decision k months earlier read. `decide` narrows the run context to 00:00 UTC on the first day of the decision month, as before. For a read at vintage k, it narrows that context again to the first day of the month k months earlier. At k = 0 this is D053's read, so `SAHM` reads, evaluates, and records exactly what it did before. At k = 1, `Subtract(Series(d), Series(d, vintage=1))` is the revision between this decision's vintage and the previous decision's. A program that reads two vintages takes the latest month both vintages hold, which is normally one release older than a single-vintage read gives. A read's key is the dataset name at vintage 0 and `dataset@k` otherwise, so a history keyed by dataset name still evaluates `SAHM`. `Decision.reads` holds one read record per dataset and vintage, in that sorted order.

Every parameter has a declared range:

| Parameter | Range | A value below the floor reports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Series.vintage` | 0 to 12 | `reads_past_vintage` |
| `Lag.months` | 0 to 24 | `negative_lag` |
| `TrailingMean.months` and `TrailingMin.months` | 1 to 24 | `out_of_range` |
| `Threshold.level` | -1,000,000 to 1,000,000, in steps of 1/10,000 | `out_of_range` |

Every program has a size limit. It may take at most 256 evaluations per month, read at most 4 distinct dataset vintages, and nest at most 32 operators deep. A window costs one plus its length times its source's cost, so `SAHM` costs 54. An operator count would not bound the work, because three nested windows are four operators. The read limit bounds the cost of parsing a stored file for each read at each decision. A program over any limit is `too_large`. The constructors check every range and limit, so a program built in Python and one parsed from JSON pass the same checks. `SAHM` and `SAHM_RULE` construct unchanged.

Three rejections are the leaky-candidate test that the master plan's Phase 4 gate requires:

- `reads_past_vintage` rejects a negative vintage, which would read a vintage released after the decision. For a historical decision the run context alone would still allow that read, because the later cutoff precedes the run's own. `decide` narrows each read from the decision's context, which `RunContext.narrow` cannot widen, so such a read fails at run time with an `AccessError`. This rule names the leak when the program is proposed, before anything runs.
- `negative_lag` rejects a negative lag, which would use an observation later than the one the decision reports.
- `unapproved_dataset` rejects a series whose dataset is outside the search universe, is not approved in the catalog, or is not a logical name. This check runs when a program is proposed, before any read. The dataset adapter still refuses an unapproved read if approval changes before `decide` runs.

Other rejections are named too: `malformed_json`, `unknown_operator`, `missing_field`, `extra_field`, `wrong_type`, `out_of_range`, `too_large`, and `unknown_field`. The catalog's documented fields decide `unknown_field`. `parse(data, catalog, universe)` is the single entry point for an untrusted program, such as a model's experiment request in P15. It decodes strict JSON, builds each operator through its constructor, and returns the program or a `Rejected` value with one reason. A Python caller gets `ProgramError` with the same reason.

`canonical(program)` encodes each operator as an object whose `op` is its class name. It leaves out fields at their default, writes levels as fraction text, and sorts keys with `records.canonical_bytes`, so `"0.5"` and `"1/2"` give one program. `digest(program)` is the SHA-256 of those bytes, and P04 or P15 records it in the trial's specification. The digest of `SAHM_RULE` is `89d9afc2e436146ee1a924fabff1cc38b78152299840f22794108ff4ef27215c`.

The search universe is `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.payems`, `alfred.indpro`, and `alfred.awhman`, which `public_data.SEARCH_UNIVERSE` names. The plan asked the researcher to choose it. Under the user's autonomy grant an agent chose it instead, and the researcher approved it on 23 October 2026. The researcher can revise it by changing that tuple and this entry. These reasons decided it:

- Each is a monthly ALFRED series with a long real-time history. On 22 October 2026, FRED reported 800 `UNRATE` vintages from 15 March 1960, 860 `PAYEMS` vintages from 6 May 1955, 1,224 `INDPRO` vintages from 26 January 1927, and 779 `AWHMAN` vintages from 3 November 1961.
- `UNRATE` keeps the Sahm program in the search as its baseline. `PAYEMS` adds payroll employment, `INDPRO` adds industrial activity, and `AWHMAN` adds hours worked in manufacturing, a labor-market measure distinct from employment counts.
- P01's `alfred.<series>` source serves each one unchanged, and each full real-time download converts.

These candidates were excluded:

- Initial claims, `ICSA`, is weekly, and its ALFRED vintages start on 28 May 2009. Windows and lags count months, so claims would first need a monthly aggregation and its timing convention.
- Household employment, `CE16OV`, does not convert. The real-time periods of its July 1947 observation are not contiguous, so P01's check refuses the download.
- `W875RX1`, `CMRMTSPL`, `CIVPART`, and `TCU` have vintages only from 2010, 2013, 1997, and 1996.
- Manufacturing employment, `MANEMP`, is part of `PAYEMS`.

Each new series' catalog documentation names its units. Payrolls are thousands of persons, and hours are hours per week. `INDPRO` is an index whose base year changes across vintages. Its January 1990 value reads 140.9 when first released, 107.2 from April 1990, and 61.6440 by March 2023. Its levels therefore compare only within one vintage, and a ratio of two months of one vintage does not depend on the base. The documentation of `alfred.unrate` is unchanged, so re-ingesting it keeps its approval.

Every decision parses each read's whole stored file, as D053 records, so cost grows with the decisions, the file sizes, and the reads. Other sessions loaded the 14-core machine during these measurements, so each names its load average:

| Dataset | Stored file | Median time per read, 60 decisions, 3 runs, load 162 to 202 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `alfred.unrate` | 0.29 MB | 33 ms |
| `alfred.awhman` | 0.55 MB | 57 ms |
| `alfred.payems` | 1.86 MB | 195 ms |
| `alfred.indpro` | 5.38 MB | 531 ms |

In the live check, at a load near 52, 48 decisions took 3.1 s for a program that reads `AWHMAN` and `UNRATE`, 13.1 s for the payroll revision, which reads `PAYEMS` at two vintages, and 20.1 s for a program that reads `INDPRO`. `SAHM` took a median of 1.04 s over three runs, and `SAHM` with one more `UNRATE` read at vintage 1 took 2.31 s. P15 fails a candidate above 30 seconds on the search window, so at these rates a candidate that reads `INDPRO` fails after about 70 decisions. The stored vintages are content-addressed, so a parse cached by vintage digest in `datasets.py` would remove most of this cost. Other plan PRs own that file, and P14 does not change it. On 23 October 2026 the researcher left the choice between that cache and dropping `INDPRO` to an agent. The agent kept `INDPRO` and chose the cache, which P15 is adding.

The live check ran on 22 October 2026 in a fresh data root. An agent pulled, ingested, validated, and approved `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.sahmrealtime`, and the three new series, with an attestation that names it as P14's live check and not the researcher's validation. Three hand-written programs parsed and computed a value at every decision from January 2007 to December 2010. They were the payroll revision, industrial production below its level a year earlier, and the three-month mean of manufacturing hours below its value a year earlier while the Sahm value was at least 0.30. The three leaky programs were rejected with `reads_past_vintage`, `negative_lag`, and `unapproved_dataset`. `quant-research reconcile series` on that root printed the same record as before this change apart from its cutoff, with 780 of 800 months matched and the same 20 excluded, as in D053's live check.

On 23 October 2026, `decide` evaluated a bare `Series` of each universe dataset in the researcher's data root at every decision month from a year before its first vintage through October 2026. The earliest computable decision is February 1927 for `INDPRO`, June 1955 for `PAYEMS`, April 1960 for `UNRATE`, and December 1961 for `AWHMAN`, so all four compute from December 1961. Every later decision computes a value for each series. Those first vintages hold history back to January 1919, January 1951, January 1948, and January 1932, which is 95, 51, 145, and 357 months before their first observations. So a program that reaches back no further than that finds every month it reads present from the same decision, and a program that reads at vintage 1 finds them one month later. Whether such a program computes a value there still depends on its own nulls, such as a zero denominator, which this bare-series check does not cover.

These limits are deliberate:

- The grammar has no generated code and no estimator. The D047 worker and P22's probit stay outside it.
- A read record lists every supplied row's identity, so a program with more reads keeps more of them.

## D065. Run experiments inside the loop under a frozen search method

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P15 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#run-experiments-inside-the-loop-under-a-frozen-search-method-p15). It joins the reference pipeline of [D055](#d055-run-the-reference-experiment-through-one-audited-pipeline-against-a-frozen-experiment-file), the research loop of [D063](#d063-run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-as-one-temporal-workflow-per-campaign), and the search grammar of [D064](#d064-search-signal-programs-in-a-bounded-grammar-over-a-chosen-alfred-universe) under the limit that [D015](#d015-evaluate-pilot-signals-against-their-recorded-trials) puts on outcome-dependent search. The plan asked the researcher to accept the repeated-search method before merge. The method, the windows, and the design were decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after three explorers, two design candidates, a cross-judge, an advisor review, and a Codex review; the researcher has not reviewed them.

### The method

History splits once, before any search, into two windows. The search window's decisions run from January 1963 to December 2004, 504 months, under an information cutoff of 00:00 UTC on 1 January 2005. The held-back window's decisions run from January 2005 to August 2026, 260 months, under the cutoff of 00:00 UTC on 23 October 2026 that [reference.json](../src/quant_research/reference.json) uses. Both use a minimum effect of 0.001 a month, alpha 0.05, the buy-and-hold market baseline, and `pilot-evaluation/v1`.

- Every tool read and every experiment of the search runs under the search cutoff, so none reads data available after 1 January 2005.
- Each experiment's search-window verdict returns to the model. It counts the trials the campaign's lineage holds so far, this one included, as D015 counts a record. These verdicts guide the search and support no claim.
- Only the final evaluation, P16's, reads the held-back window, under a separate authority. It adjusts for the campaign's declared `limits.max_trials`, whatever number of candidates ran, so the count does not depend on when the search stopped. `evaluation.py` is unchanged here, so P16 decides how `evaluate` receives that count. One option scales alpha by the recorded count over `max_trials`, which records a scaled alpha in the artifact.
- The split controls what this campaign's tools and experiments can read. It does not make the held-back period unknown. The researcher and the models know post-2005 history. The role prompt and declared steering notes are trusted inputs that the wide authority sends, and they could carry held-back facts. D015's assumptions still apply. Held-back verdicts are therefore development evidence, and no family-wise error guarantee is claimed beyond D015's.
- No embargo separates the windows. The first held-back decision reads vintages that already hold December 2004.

These reasons fixed the windows:

- January 1963 is the first decision whose read of a vintage 12 months older finds a vintage for every universe series. `alfred.awhman`'s first vintage is dated 3 November 1961. A candidate is then never rejected only because its first month has no value.
- Each of the search window's four subperiods holds at least one NBER recession: 1969 to 1970, 1973 to 1975 and 1980, 1990 to 1991, and 2001.
- The held-back window uses two subperiods, which hold the 2008 and 2020 recessions. Four parts would leave two parts without a recession, whose estimates for a recession-timing strategy are exactly zero, so no such strategy could be supported.
- The 260 held-back months keep 130 observations in each part, well above the profile's minimum.

### The rules file

The rules file is canonical JSON with exactly the keys `held_back`, `schema_version`, and `search`. Each window is one document in `reference.json`'s exact format, which `load_experiment` parses unchanged. `load_rules` also requires the held-back window to start the month after the search window ends, and the search cutoff to be midnight at that start. The pilot's file is [rules/6387e6b8c71e4b825295ab7c92cbca7d3230afabc564788fea32b62b4bb3b8b0.json](../src/quant_research/rules/6387e6b8c71e4b825295ab7c92cbca7d3230afabc564788fea32b62b4bb3b8b0.json), named by its SHA-256. Commit `a5c27e6` added it alone at 12:12 UTC on 23 October 2026, before any code that runs a candidate existed. A campaign's `evaluation.digest` names a rules file, and its `evaluation.profile` must be `pilot-evaluation/v1`.

`Setup.rules` is a directory of rules files named by their digests, and it defaults to the package's `rules` directory. `resolve` reads `<digest>.json`, checks that the bytes hash to the name, and parses it. It raises `RulesError` when the file is missing, does not match, or does not parse, when the profile differs, or when `Setup.cutoff` is before the held-back cutoff. The last check stops a campaign from spending its trials when its final evaluation could never run. The opening turns `RulesError` into its own fixed error, and the loop ends inconclusive with the reason `rules` before its first ask or ledger event.

### Two authorities

`resolve` still issues the run context at `Setup.cutoff` and narrows it once to the search cutoff.

| Consumer | Context |
| --- | --- |
| The gateway's send check, the role prompt's read check, the floor for steering notes, and P16's final evaluation | `Authority.context`, at `Setup.cutoff` |
| Every tool, through `Authority.toolset`, and every experiment, through `Authority.search` | the search context, at the search cutoff |

`activities.Activities` now takes a resolver that returns the whole `Authority`. The `model` activity sends under `Authority.context`, and the `tool` activity calls `Authority.toolset`. Before this change the resolver returned the Toolset, so its context also authorized sends, and a Toolset narrowed to 2005 would have refused the 2026 role prompt. Narrowing the one context instead would have forced every prompt and steering note to declare an availability before 2005, a false record. `Authority.record()` moves to schema version 2 and names the search context and the rules digest. Tool results take the search cutoff as their availability through D059's floor, so they pass the wide send check.

The Toolset's corpus search and dataset descriptions narrow with it. A corpus member declared after 2005, such as the pilot brief, is hidden from the model's tools during a search, so what the model must know belongs in the role prompt.

### Experiments and the lineage

An experiment request's `arguments` is one program document in the D064 grammar. The protocol names the search window, its cutoff, the universe the campaign permits, and `limits.max_trials`, with the Sahm rule's canonical JSON as an example. The loop refuses an experiment once the campaign's trials reach `limits.max_trials`, with fixed text and no activity. Otherwise it mints the trial's ID and schedules the `experiment` activity, which resolves the revision and calls `reference.extend`.

- `extend` parses the program with `signals.parse` over the search universe that a read under the search context would supply. A parse failure, or a program that is a number rather than a condition, is a rejected trial. A number would otherwise make `allocate` raise.
- A parsed condition runs through P04's pipeline on the search window under the search authority, exactly as `reference.run` does. The pipeline reads the factors after the signal's reads, and a refusal there would drop the records of reads already made. So when the search cannot read `french.ff3_monthly`, the candidate fails before any read, and no read of a trial is refused.
- The trial joins the campaign's one lineage: its lineage ID is the campaign ID, and it links the previous trial with the relation `follows`.
- `extend` opens the head package by its digest and saves a new head that holds every earlier trial and artifact plus the new one. The head selects no trial, because P16 selects.
- The workflow keeps only the head's digest, and one function in `reference` owns the `<packages>/<campaign>/unselected/<digest>` layout.

Every candidate is a trial: evaluated, rejected at parse, rejected by the pipeline, or failed. When the `experiment` activity raises or times out after 5 minutes, the workflow at once runs `forfeit`, which appends the candidate as a failed trial whose detail says nothing was evaluated. The forfeited run may have read data that no exposure records, so from that head on the lineage's disclosure coverage is unknown. A retry of `forfeit` saves another immutable head, and the workflow records the one it receives. When `forfeit` also fails three times, the loop ends inconclusive with the reason `lineage`, because a search whose record cannot stay complete must stop, unless the researcher cancelled during the forfeit, when it ends cancelled. When a cancel ends the loop during an experiment, the loop ends cancelled. In either ending, unless a result arrived with the cancel, the report carries that one candidate's revision, trial ID, program, and times for P17. A timed-out thread can still save a head that nothing opens, and that orphan can disagree with the forfeited trial of the same ID. A result that arrives with a cancel is kept as the step's result before the loop ends cancelled.

A search trial's `signal_profile` is `search/v1` followed by the program's digest, `signals.digest` for a parsed program and the SHA-256 of the request's bytes otherwise. A `program` artifact holds those bytes, so two programs give two specification IDs. `reference.run` keeps its output byte for byte, so P04's packages and specifications are unchanged. The contract artifact of a search trial is the search window's document.

The model reads canonical JSON with exactly `trial`, `status`, `detail`, and `evaluation`, the decoded pilot-evaluation artifact or null. It holds no package bytes, and package inputs hold whole vintage files, held-back rows included. Its restrictions derive over D059's floor at the search cutoff and the restrictions of every dataset the run read. The step's result is `rejected` for a rejected trial and `ran` otherwise. `StepRecord` names the trial, and the report names the head.

Experiment compute is observed and charged under the role `experiments`, as tool compute is. An activity failure releases the attempt as failed, so `max_attempts` bounds failed experiments as it bounds failed asks.

### The held-back read and the leaky experiment

`read_dataset` names only a dataset, and the adapter omits a key with no revision by the cutoff without a reason, by the design of [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter) and [D062](#d062-resolve-a-campaign-revision-into-its-run-authority). A read therefore cannot be refused a held-back month. The plan's "refused" read passes here when no supplied row is available after the search cutoff and no key after the search window appears. That rewording was decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant. An experiment that reaches past its vintage or lag is rejected by `parse` as `reads_past_vintage` or `negative_lag`. Behind both, `signals.decide` raises `AccessError` for any month past the search cutoff, because a narrowed context cannot widen.

### Read cost

The perf rule fails a candidate above 30 seconds on the search window. Before this change each read parsed its whole vintage file and built `Restrictions` for every row. On 23 October 2026, at load averages of 6 to 8 on 14 cores, `reference.run` on the search window took 6.5 seconds for the Sahm rule on a second run, after 15.1 seconds on a first run that included the engine import. Over two runs each, it took a median of 92.4 seconds for the payroll revision and 140.4 seconds for industrial production below its level a year earlier.

`datasets.read` now caches parsed rows by file, dataset, and vintage, and checks the file's SHA-256 on every read before it uses them, so a hit still checks the requested dataset, and a missing or altered file still fails. A row's values refuse change, so one caller cannot change what a later read returns. The refusal covers the values object itself, not a nested list or object inside it. Every ingested row holds flat text values, and no reader changes a value. The row loop compares each row's availability with the cutoff directly, because the admission gate already refused a dataset whose labels the run is not cleared for. Read records are identical with and without the cache. This edits `datasets.py`, which is outside P15's file list, as is the one constant that `backtest.py` gains for the factors' dataset name. No other open PR owns it, and the change closes the to do that D064 left.

After the change, on 23 October 2026, five program shapes ran five times each, interleaved, through `reference.extend` on one growing lineage over the live check's data root, after one discarded warm-up candidate. Other sessions loaded the 14-core machine to load averages of 65 to 115.

| Program | Median | Range |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The Sahm rule | 0.99 s | 0.88 to 1.03 s |
| The payroll revision, two `PAYEMS` vintages | 5.18 s | 4.21 to 5.41 s |
| Industrial production below its level a year earlier | 6.42 s | 5.81 to 6.90 s |
| Four `INDPRO` vintages, the most reads of one file | 21.67 s | 20.80 to 21.86 s |
| Four universe series at vintage 0, the most distinct reads | 10.69 s | 10.49 to 11.53 s |

Every candidate stayed under 30 seconds, and each was inconclusive. The three programs measured before the change fell from 6.5, 92.4, and 140.4 seconds to 1.0, 5.2, and 6.4 seconds. The four-vintage program spends most of its time reading and hashing the 5.4 MB `INDPRO` file at each of its 2,016 reads, about 4 ms of each 5 ms read at a load near 5.

### Checks

The unit tests run offline on synthetic data. `tests/test_research.py` runs a scripted search whose three candidates become three trials of one lineage. The leaky one is rejected, and the search's read of `alfred.unrate` supplies no row after the search cutoff, although the store holds four more years.
