It also covers the trial limit, a rules file that cannot be resolved, a failed experiment that is forfeited as a failed trial, a failed forfeit, a cancel during a forfeit that fails, and a cancel during an experiment. `tests/test_reference.py` pins the rules file's digest and covers `extend`, `forfeit`, program identity, and the summary's restrictions. `tests/test_campaigns.py`, `tests/test_activities.py`, and `tests/test_datasets.py` cover the rules lookup, the two authorities, and the cache. On 23 October 2026, after the fixes from Codex's review, these five files with `tests/test_backtest.py` and `tests/test_campaign_cli.py` passed 474 tests in 119 seconds, at a load average near 115.

The live check ran on 23 October 2026 in a fresh data root. An agent pulled, ingested, validated, and approved `alfred.unrate`, `alfred.payems`, `alfred.indpro`, `alfred.awhman`, and `french.ff3_monthly`, with an attestation that names it as this PR's live check and not the researcher's validation. A scripted provider on the Codex route, which makes no subscription call, drove one campaign that names the pilot rules file on a local Temporal dev server. Its script read `alfred.unrate`, ran the Sahm rule and the payroll revision, tried a program that reads a later vintage and one that lags three months ahead, and concluded.

- The campaign concluded after six steps. Its four candidates became four trials of one lineage whose ID is the campaign ID, each following the one before, and the head selects no trial.
- The Sahm rule and the payroll revision were inconclusive on the search window, counting one and two trials.
- The two reaching programs were rejected with `reads_past_vintage` and `negative_lag`.
- The read supplied no row available after 1 January 2005. Its last key was November 2004, and the same read under the wide authority reached September 2026.
- `signals.decide` under the search authority raised `AccessError` for February 2005.
- The model saw four summaries, each with exactly the four keys.

### Changes to earlier records

This changes eight earlier records. [D013](#d013-version-campaign-specifications-as-content-addressed-revisions) evaluation digests now name a rules file that a revision needs to run. [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter) reads cache each parse, and a row's values are read-only. [D053](#d053-evaluate-signal-programs-per-decision-and-reconcile-the-sahm-series-with-fred) said each read parses the whole stored file, and now a process parses each vintage once. [D055](#d055-run-the-reference-experiment-through-one-audited-pipeline-against-a-frozen-experiment-file) `run` shares its pipeline with `extend`, which adds the trial history that D055 left to P15. [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters) activities take a resolver to the `Authority`, not the Toolset. [D062](#d062-resolve-a-campaign-revision-into-its-run-authority) authorities gain the search context and the rules, their record moves to schema version 2, and their Toolset reads under the search cutoff. [D063](#d063-run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-as-one-temporal-workflow-per-campaign) experiment requests now run, steps may be `rejected`, the loop may end with the reason `rules` or `lineage`, and the report names the lineage head and any unrecorded candidate. [D064](#d064-search-signal-programs-in-a-bounded-grammar-over-a-chosen-alfred-universe) read costs no longer hold, because the parse it proposed to cache is cached.

### Limits

- The researcher has not reviewed the method, the windows, the rules file, or the rewording of the refused read.
- The held-back window is known to the researcher and the models, so held-back verdicts are development evidence.
- Every lineage head is kept, and nothing prunes superseded heads or orphans. In the perf run, 26 trials left 26 heads that took 277 MB together.
- Early search verdicts face a lenient Bonferroni count, because each counts the trials so far.
- The search Toolset hides corpus members declared after the search cutoff.
- `Setup` gains the required fields `packages` and `lock`, so every caller that builds one must pass them.
- A stuck experiment thread can hold up a worker's shutdown for up to its 5-minute timeout.
- Scheduling the new activities changes the history of an open campaign that takes an experiment step, so open campaigns must drain before deploy, as D063 says.

## D066. Interpret held-back results with a separate critique

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P16 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#interpret-results-under-critique-p16). An agent decided the protocol under the user's autonomy grant after an architectural explanation, two design candidates, a cross-judge, and a Codex advisor review. The researcher has not reviewed this decision. The earlier approvals in D057, D058, D063, and D064 retain their stated scope.

### Freeze the shortlist before the final read

The [research loop](../src/quant_research/research.py) treats `conclude` as a request to end search with an exact JSON object in `arguments`, `{"trials":["SEARCH_TRIAL_ID"]}`. The IDs must be unique recorded experiment IDs. The existing before-conclude checkpoint runs first. Once it accepts the selection, the workflow freezes the revision, search package head, source trial IDs, and proposing model. Steering cannot change that selection, and the loop cannot return to search after the held-back read. `stop` retains its meaning of no finding and records `no_result` without reading the held-back window.

One activity evaluates the entire frozen shortlist, with automatic retries disabled. It uses the wide campaign authority narrowed to the held-back cutoff in the [D065 rules file](../src/quant_research/rules/6387e6b8c71e4b825295ab7c92cbca7d3230afabc564788fea32b62b4bb3b8b0.json). The [reference pipeline](../src/quant_research/reference.py) recovers the selected programs from the frozen package. Each held-back measurement has a new workflow-minted trial ID and its own specification, linked to the source search trial. The new immutable package preserves the search trials and adds the held-back contracts, decisions, audits, returns, engine checks, evaluations, inputs, implementation, lock, and exposure records. No trial or program is reconstructed from a later model reply.

The [evaluation function](../src/quant_research/evaluation.py) accepts an explicit positive `adjustment_trials`. Its default retains the recorded-history count for existing callers. Every held-back evaluation passes the campaign's declared `limits.max_trials`, regardless of early search stopping, shortlist size, or the count of appended held-back measurement records. `Evaluation.trials` records that adjustment count, and its profile retains frozen alpha 0.05. Repeated measurement records do not create new search candidates. This implements D065's adjustment without changing the frozen profile or inventing trials.

### Keep interpretation separate from evidence

After evaluation, the loop asks the model that actually supplied the accepted shortlist for a fresh trial-citing conclusion. It pins that model from `Answer.record`, including when a fallback supplied the shortlist. It then asks a different authorized active provider for a separate critique. An active critic role can supply that model and prompt. Otherwise the loop uses its authorized researcher models. It checks each candidate against the full critique request and its individual provider policy, prefers another provider, and preserves a configured same-provider critic before the researcher fallback. A same-provider critique is a fresh completion marked as not independent. Unrelated active roles supply no critic candidates, and each role prompt crosses the opening payload once. Both final completions have separate attempts and call IDs.

The final completions receive exact JSON-format instructions and the complete required evidence. The workflow parses their raw texts so it can preserve a malformed or refused claim in the assessment. The critic also receives the conclusion. Material restrictions derive over what each request contains. Numerical artifacts stay in the package. A required request that cannot fit the prompt bound ends inconclusive rather than silently omitting evidence. Status omits the raw final texts, and the closed multipart record keeps them. The assessment retains both restricted texts, their call provenance, the frozen source IDs, evaluated IDs, and package heads independently of the rolling search transcript, for P17.

The campaign class is `supported`, `null`, `inconclusive`, or `no_result`.

- `supported` requires a cited supported held-back evaluation and an accepted valid critique.
- A campaign-wide `null` requires a nonempty shortlist whose evaluations are all null, valid citations, and an accepted valid critique.
- Unsupported positive claims, foreign citations, invalid evaluations, failed calls, and rejected or incomplete nonempty shortlists are inconclusive. Critic rejection is inconclusive and never null.
- An explicit stop or valid empty shortlist can be `no_result`. A positive claim after an empty shortlist is refused and is inconclusive even if the critic accepts it.

Only executable held-back verdicts can supply support or null evidence. The models and researcher already know post-2005 history, and D015's assumptions remain unchecked, so every result is provisional development evidence. This does not complete the [Phase 4 gate](master-plan.md#phase-4-complete-a-bounded-autonomous-campaign), which still requires an independent held-out capability comparison.

### Preserve resource and cancellation ownership

The workflow remains the sole ledger writer under [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts). Before search it reserves the closing shortlist chain and two single-model final completions on separate runs. Starting therefore needs at least `2 * researcher_chain + 2` model calls and, when declared, four model timeouts of wall time. A normal conclusion releases the unused closing selection reservation. Budget or history termination uses that selection reservation, then the two final reservations. It retains its original inconclusive outcome and reason even when an individual held-back trial has a supported or null verdict.

The one evaluation activity receives five minutes per frozen candidate, with a minimum of five minutes for an empty shortlist. Every selected source and program is validated before the first measurement. Held-back compute is observed and charged. Each model lease closes before its receipts are charged and its reservation is released. A researcher pause holds the next final stage, and resume does not repeat evaluation. Cancellation preserves delivered evidence and texts, settles the running model lease, releases unused holds, and starts no later interpretation or recovery activity.

Before evaluation starts, the assessment records a recovery reference derived from the frozen lineage, rules, search head, ordered source and measurement IDs, and trial budget. Each completed measurement package contains the accumulated evidence, exact restrictions, and frozen request in the same atomic publication as its numerical artifacts. The measurement component owns a read-only recovery operation. It scans manifest metadata for the receipt, fully verifies matching packages, opens their frozen base once, and returns the longest completed prefix for that reference without reading datasets or repeating a measurement. This extra durable artifact is necessary because relocating return handling cannot recover a head after the activity loses its return.

A noncancelled activity failure takes one read-only recovery snapshot, retains unfinished shortlist entries as pending, and ends inconclusive without interpretation calls. Cancellation retains the recovery reference and schedules no recovery activity. A thread can publish after cancellation or timeout, so a snapshot describes only its read time. P17 must retain the original workflow report and follow its recovery reference to collect any later completed package. The workflow's recorded head and this reference together identify the authoritative handoff. Neither recovery nor a late publication changes the original outcome or ledger, and neither permits evaluation to run again.

Search rate limits still pause under D063. A rate limit during finalization ends inconclusive without an automatic retry or resume, consistent with the earlier closing-ask behavior. Changing this protocol changes workflow history, so existing campaigns must drain before deployment.

### Checks and retained evidence

The final targeted offline checks passed 121 tests in `tests/test_research.py`, 60 in `tests/test_reference.py`, 54 in `tests/test_evaluation.py`, and all 44 campaign CLI tests, including its service case. They cover evidence-based classification, both provider-selection paths, raw final text, fixed multiplicity, budgets, pause and cancellation, replay, rate limits, and prompt bounds.

A numerical failure probe saved candidate A and then failed candidate B. The correction recovered A's exact head without another measurement. Tests also cover a lost return after atomic publication, a lost final activity result, exact saved restrictions with the dataset store unavailable, and a later B publication after control cancellation, Temporal cancellation, or timeout. Workflow histories schedule evaluation once with retries disabled and retain settled holds. Separate cases lose each final call's record and verify its unknown-usage receipt, earlier numerical evidence, and any delivered conclusion text. Recovery rejects altered request, summary, source provenance, and conflicting heads. Snapshot failure or absence and cancellation during recovery have no dedicated injected-fault tests.

The CLI's reservation-count assertion changes from three to five for the two extra holds. Its service case passed after full execution access was enabled. Ruff and `git diff --check` passed. The full suite runs inside `make merge` on the commit that lands, per D074.

A compatibility probe against `origin/main` caught an invalid-input regression while adding the explicit adjustment. Without an override, an empty history and empty returns still return `Invalid(too_few_observations)`. Only a supplied adjustment is validated before the existing data checks. A regression test preserves that behavior.

A real-data numerical probe ran the Sahm program on the frozen search and held-back windows using the fresh ALFRED UNRATE and Ken French factors. The saved package reopened with its original search trial and a new `held_back_of` measurement. Its declared candidate budget was five despite only one searched candidate and two total measurement records. The evaluation retained alpha 0.05 and confidence 0.99. Its held-back verdict was inconclusive, with an incremental estimate of 0.00170755 a month and an interval from -0.00172472 to 0.00513981. One subperiod estimate was negative. `numeric-summary.json`, `numeric-final.log`, `numeric_check.py`, `numeric.env`, `source-snapshot-4`, and `numeric-packages` retain the evidence outside the repository. This probe made no workflow or model call and does not pass the P16 subscription live check.

On 23 October 2026, after full execution access was enabled, a real subscription campaign passed with Claude proposing and Codex critiquing. Campaign `71b64a2d-8d44-4ab7-b76b-4095e6afce86` used 4 recorded calls, with no duplicate dispatch. It passed after merging D077 and applying the local review fixes. The three live runs consumed 12 of the 20 allowed subscription dispatches in total. It ran the real-time Sahm rule, froze its search trial, recorded a distinct linked held-back measurement, and obtained a fresh conclusion and an independent critique.

Both final texts cited the saved evidence and agreed with the executable inconclusive verdict. The live campaign declared one candidate, so its adjustment used one trial, alpha 0.05, and confidence 0.95. The incremental estimate was 0.00170755 a month, with an interval from -0.00089699 to 0.00431208 and one negative subperiod estimate.

This passes the P16 development live check and does not establish researcher acceptance, confirmation, or an investment claim. The original report, Temporal history, summary, call receipts and transcripts, source implementations, inputs, and lineage packages remain under `runs/71b64a2d-8d44-4ab7-b76b-4095e6afce86`, `calls`, and `packages` in the external artifact root. Earlier permission-denial logs are retained as historical evidence.

The live artifacts and service setup script have a durable external copy at `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p16-live-20261023`. Its archive index verifies the original inputs, packages, histories, receipts, and reports by SHA-256; the original temporary evidence is also retained. `live_check.py` starts and stops only its own Temporal server, writes the campaign revision, call log, workflow history, status, and closed report, and caps subscription dispatches at 20 across invocations. `runtime.env` sets this worktree's source path and keeps runtime caches and temporary files under the durable artifact root. From this worktree, the reproducible campaign command is `uv run --offline --env-file ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p16-live-20261023/runtime.env python ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p16-live-20261023/live_check.py`. Repeating it creates a new campaign and consumes the remaining dispatch budget. Original data pulls, catalog attestations, and packages stay under that external root for P17. These attestations name the agent's P16 live check and are not researcher validation.

The system Codex CLI had changed to 0.164.0. The live check therefore uses the already-installed, qualified `@openai/codex@0.162.1` under its external `codex-runtime` directory and passes that executable to the unchanged adapter. Claude Code remains at the qualified 2.1.301. No API-key fallback or paid usage credits are enabled.

The P16 file list also expands to `reference.py`, `evaluation.py`, and their tests, plus the campaign CLI test's reservation-count assertion. The existing reference API could evaluate only search candidates, and the evaluation API derived its adjustment only from observed history, so the two additions are required to run the specified held-back evaluation with the declared trial budget. The design replaces immediate model-text acceptance with an evidence-based terminal phase.

## D067. Archive each research ending as a complete campaign snapshot

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P17 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#save-a-campaign-as-one-complete-research-package-p17), after [D060](#d060-let-a-package-record-no-selected-trial), [D066](#d066-interpret-held-back-results-with-a-separate-critique), and [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority) merged. An agent chose the design under the user's autonomy grant after two design candidates, an independent cross-judge, and an advisor review. The researcher has not reviewed this decision. The campaign remains development research and confirmation remains unavailable.

### Publish at the ending

[The research loop](../src/quant_research/research.py) fixes its original ending, closes command admission, and drains admitted handlers before freezing the full record and every admitted revision. A terminal activity reads the frozen bytes through bounded queries against that exact workflow run. It publishes before the workflow returns, including conclusions, stops, exhausted budgets, cancellations, handled failures, opening failures, and unexpected implementation failures. The latter keep an operational failure token and exception class, retain an absent research ending, and then fail the workflow. Backing exception text enters neither the artifact nor the terminal workflow failure. Only an implementation exception caught inside the run becomes a fixed non-retryable application failure after archival. Temporal replay and sandbox errors keep the SDK workflow-task failure behavior so a worker fix can recover them.

`CampaignArchive` binds an archive root and call-log directory independently of the authority resolver. Successful opening pins the exact authority, catalog, wide and search contexts, rules, lock, model roles, prompts, all resolved source files, and permitted dataset vintage bytes before returning. Opening failure can therefore publish a zero-trial package without trying the failed resolver again. The writer never calls the evaluation function for a zero-trial package. `worker` and `run_worker` now require an explicit `archive` binding. Every worker on that queue must share the archive root, call-log directory, and numerical publication storage. Composition binds `Gateway(CallLog(calls), ...)` and `CampaignArchive(root, calls)` to one call-log path. `Authorized.definitions` includes the terminal archive activity. The research, activity, and campaign CLI test callers migrate with that interface. The resolver exposes its complete immutable generation map on `Authority` so the archive retains active and inactive prompt sources excluded from the retrieval corpus. This small resolver extension is necessary because prompt text alone cannot reconstruct a revision's source locators. Readable locator publications for those extraction IDs retain the original manifest, checksum, and publication metadata. Vintage artifact names use the existing content digest, while the exact vintage bytes retain the complete dataset name. No provider, adapter, or call-log schema changes are part of P17.

The activity retains the original workflow report and ending independently of its new package metadata. It saves a finite Temporal history prefix ending before the first archive activity was scheduled, which retains tool results even when no later prompt saw them or the rolling prompt discarded them. All ledger-reserved attempt IDs scope the original call-log entries, including final conclusion and critique calls without ordinary step records. Sent request and reply objects retain their exact bytes and digests, and version 3 and version 4 log entries are not rewritten. The package also contains the steering log, ledger, reconciled receipts, acceptance evidence, original shortlist, evidence, conclusion and critique, every admitted revision, trial and exposure records, exact vintages, frozen contracts, implementation snapshots, and locks.

The research loop has no acceptance-log owner. Its default acceptance artifact explicitly records that absence. A historical export may supply an actual acceptance log. Neither a successful trial nor critic agreement invents a human acceptance event. The report lists recorded steering actors without inferring human review and retains the predecessor named by every superseding acceptance decision.

### Keep retries and later evidence distinct

The first publication for an explicit export key materializes the evidence bytes and commits one immutable selection. Retries reuse that selection and its published digest even if the original stores become unavailable or a later measurement appears. An optional importer receives that exact verified package, so an import failure cannot rerun the research or change its selected snapshot. The existing registry import/export stores every artifact without a campaign schema change. Original trial, specification, attempt, and exposure rows remain unchanged, allowing a campaign package to import after its numerical package.

Following D066's recovery reference takes one read-only snapshot of the longest published measurement prefix. The archive keeps the original workflow head and the recovered head separately. A new explicit export key can include a later completed measurement while retaining the exact original report, outcome, ledger, and interpretation. Pending and unrecorded candidates remain explicit. One snapshot does not claim to contain future publications.

The [report](../src/quant_research/report.py) renders the campaign question, stopping reason, original conclusion and class, critique, recorded decisions, and each model's `model_provenance.CutoffCheck`, alongside every trial and its saved evaluation. Each cutoff check uses the models and rules of its own pinned revision, including after a steer changes model assignments. Unknown model versions and cutoffs stay unknown. The campaign reader uses only standard-library and existing portable-package dependencies, so the standalone report consumer does not acquire a Temporal dependency.

### Checks and retained evidence

The targeted offline checks passed. The required research, report, and registry command passed 206 tests. The two migrated worker caller checks and 28 focused campaign resolver checks passed. A source-restoration regression moved the original sources away, restored every archived source locator including an inactive prompt, and reproduced exact authority bytes and digests with otherwise identical operator configuration. The same regression publishes 47- and 128-character dataset names and restores exact locator files, corpus bytes, and `Located` results. This tests source restoration, not reconstruction of all operator configuration. They exercise automatic publication without a record reader, frozen multipart bytes, cancellation during final publication and handler drain, a lost archive reply, idempotent registry import, zero-trial opening failure, sanitized unexpected failure with its actual reserved ledger, revision-scoped cutoff checks, superseded acceptance decisions, configured-importer retry, original-store removal after selection, late immutable recovery snapshots, artifact contents, portable report sections, and registry byte preservation. The baseline before P17 passed all 197 research, report, and registry tests. Normal hooks and the final `make merge` gate remain in place.

The P16 campaign `71b64a2d-8d44-4ab7-b76b-4095e6afce86` exported to campaign package `a19a831c0d918dc64694ba14f9a5fe654e36dc3a78d405ab943642b35e6c4cc8`. Reconstructing its saved authority from the original revision, catalog, source generations, and packaged lock reproduced the exact authority digest in its workflow report. Its recovery head matched the recorded numerical head. The live check imported the original numerical package first, then imported the campaign package and exported it byte-identical, preserving both original trial rows and exact report bytes. Repeating the same export key returned the same digest.

The rerun subprocess's macOS sandbox denied reads of the original durable and temporary data and transcript stores, both repository source directories, and all network access. It restored the two exact dataset vintages, contexts, frozen rules, lock, and 45 Python source files from the exported package. Its search and held-back trials reproduced all 10 numerical artifacts byte for byte, including decisions, audits, returns, engine agreement, and pilot evaluations. Both executable verdicts and the original campaign interpretation remain inconclusive. The check made zero new research model calls. It did not establish researcher data validation, confirmation, investment usefulness, or an independent capability result.

Evidence stays at `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p17-live-20261023`. `export-summary-final-v5.json`, `reproduction-summary-final-v5.json`, `report.md`, `reproduction.sb`, the exact exported package, and the logs retain the measured results. From this worktree, `PYTHONPATH=src OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 uv run --offline python ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p17-live-20261023/export_p16.py --snapshot final-v5` repeats the idempotent historical export using its original stores. `PYTHONPATH=src OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 uv run --offline python ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p17-live-20261023/reproduce_package.py` reruns the exported package under the recorded deny policy. These scripts read no FRED key and enable no paid model fallback. The P16 original stores and dispatch counter remain unchanged.

These limits remain:

- External Temporal termination cannot execute terminal cleanup. The same historical export API is the operational recovery path for retained evidence. Permanent loss of every usable evidence store cannot be repaired by a package writer.
- A terminal package describes its pinned snapshot. It cannot promise eventual inclusion of a thread that publishes after that snapshot. A later export uses a new key and preserves the original outcome.
- Archive storage or configured importer failures leave publication retrying rather than claiming workflow completion. Existing workflow histories must drain before deploying this workflow-history change. The archived P16 campaign was exported offline rather than restarted.
- Model cutoff sentences qualify historical evidence. The package's scope stays development and its confirmation field stays unavailable.
- The [master plan's Phase 4 gate](master-plan.md#phase-4-complete-a-bounded-autonomous-campaign) stays open until an independent held-out capability comparison exists.

## D068. Register campaign procedures before later released outcomes

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P18 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#register-the-frozen-procedure-for-prospective-observation-p18), after [D067](#d067-archive-each-research-ending-as-a-complete-campaign-snapshot) merged. An agent chose the design under the user's autonomy grant after an architectural explanation, two design candidates, a cross-judge, and an advisor review. The researcher has not reviewed this decision. Registration records a procedure and an eligibility boundary. It supplies no confirmation, investment claim, or held-out capability result.

### Register at campaign publication

[CampaignArchive.publish](../src/quant_research/research.py) registers its verified terminal package after publishing its immutable receipt and before running its optional importer. Automatic completion and offline package registration call the same prospective operation. The append-only local file log retains the first registration for a campaign revision and procedure across retries and export keys. Its boundary is the actual registration time, never the historical campaign ending or the package's data cutoff.

The procedure preserves the frozen source candidates, their specification identities, pinned evaluation rules and model records, and a one-calendar-month outcome horizon. Candidate specification identities already bind their programs, inputs, implementation, and environment. The existing shortlist's preprocessing, training, portfolio, and evaluation references name the pinned rules, campaign configuration, held-back contract, and evaluation rules respectively. These references do not claim a separate model training run.

The horizon follows the existing reference pipeline's monthly return convention. A result for a decision month matures at the start of the next calendar month. The held-back sample's historical duration is not an outcome horizon.

Unknown model versions and cutoffs stay unknown. A deliberately empty frozen shortlist remains distinct from an ending that never froze a valid procedure. Missing authority or invalid selection evidence stays explicit rather than inventing a valid empty freeze. A valid source selection stays frozen when a held-back measurement is pending or failed. The registration preserves that state without claiming the historical measurement finished.

### Separate initialization from scored releases

[The prospective registry](../src/quant_research/prospective.py) retains exact historical initialization separately from each observation's scored row evidence. A new observation must name the same procedure, supply at least one scored row for its declared dataset and window, and wait for every input and the outcome horizon to mature. Every scored row's original publication must be strictly after registration. Its original availability must be no later than observation. At registration, only rows already available can become initialization, even when a pinned vintage contains future rows. The full original vintage stays available for later eligible observations. `available_at` is the earliest availability among scored rows, so a recent row cannot hide an older scored outcome. A scored window names its first and last decision months as UTC month starts, inclusively.

An ancillary input can reuse exact frozen initialization or use a genuinely later published row. An outcome row in the scored dataset and window cannot be exempted as initialization. Calendar periods may precede registration when their data was genuinely first published later. Repeated dataset, key, and revision identities must retain their values and both original dates across registrations and observations. Downloading again cannot refresh eligibility. Loading the durable log replays its rules and verifies the retained vintage bytes.

This amends [D032](#d032-register-frozen-procedures-before-observing-them-prospectively) for new evidence-bearing records. Legacy schema-1 bytes, procedure identities, registration identities, and validation semantics remain unchanged. New registrations require evidence-bearing observations, including direct registry appends. The CLI records and validates a caller-produced evaluation. As in D032, the caller remains trusted to report the inputs and execute the frozen numerical procedure. The local log is neither signed nor immutable against a consistent manual rewrite.

### Checks and retained evidence

The prospective suite passed 55 tests covering legacy identities, deliberate empty and unfinished procedures, original vintage verification, historical initialization, later releases, mixed old and new scored rows, changed dates, future initialization, publication races, and the CLI. Focused archive and CLI checks passed. The combined prospective, research, and campaign CLI run passed 225 cases and failed one experiment heartbeat before terminal registration. An exact retained session excerpt of the Temporal timeout event identifies the heartbeat failure, and that case passed an isolated rerun without changing the heartbeat protocol. Routine pytest retention removed the original temporary archive, so the retained excerpt is not a complete workflow history. The PR records the commands and final verification. The full suite runs only in `make merge` on the commit that lands.

The live check registered the actual P16 campaign `71b64a2d-8d44-4ab7-b76b-4095e6afce86` from the verified P17 package `a19a831c0d918dc64694ba14f9a5fe654e36dc3a78d405ab943642b35e6c4cc8`. The stored log reopens and verifies its shortlist, actual model records, monthly horizon, and original initialization dates. P16 remains inconclusive. The live check makes zero new research model calls and records zero real prospective observations. A separate synthetic fixture demonstrates one eligible later release. The first real observation waits for a future monthly release and is outside this merge gate.

Evidence stays at `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p18-live-20261023`. `package-preflight.json` inventories the 108-artifact input package and 3,401 initialization rows. `live-summary.json`, `p16-registry.json`, and `p16-registration` retain the verified real registration, and `synthetic-fixture` retains the separate CLI example. `heartbeat-history-excerpt.json` retains the earlier diagnostic event with its active-session provenance. From this checkout, `PYTHONPATH=src TMPDIR=~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p18-live-20261023/tmp uv run --offline python ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p18-live-20261023/live_check.py` repeats the idempotent check without a data pull or research model call. The [master plan's Phase 4 gate](master-plan.md#phase-4-complete-a-bounded-autonomous-campaign) stays open without independent held-out capability evidence.

## D069. Record a reusable development baseline through the research loop

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P19 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#record-the-single-branch-baseline-on-development-cases-p19), after [D067](#d067-archive-each-research-ending-as-a-complete-campaign-snapshot) merged. An agent chose the design and acceptance rule under the user's autonomy grant after an architectural explanation, two design candidates, an independent cross-judge, and advisor reviews. The researcher has not reviewed this decision.

The tasks and their answers are agent-authored development material. They do not establish the master plan's independent held-out capability comparison or researcher acceptance.

### Reuse development tasks without restoring held-out eligibility

[Capability evaluation](../src/quant_research/capability.py) keeps the historical `Comparison`, configuration identities, and schema 1 freeze and disclosure bytes unchanged. An additive `Development` envelope commits the complete comparison and its known-answer success share. The same task-set version, definition, and event directory support another execution with a fresh identity. The async runner records each execution before delivering task inputs.

That event excludes every admitted input digest from future held-out comparisons and invalidates overlapping held-out comparisons frozen earlier, including when the development execution crashes. Renaming an exposed task does not restore eligibility. The shared ledger retains answer-key and disclosed configuration-name bindings. Each complete execution discloses once and retains every case's score for every repeat.

`ResearchProcedure` evaluates exactly one supplied candidate through a fresh real research workflow, worker, gateway, numerical store, and `CampaignArchive`. Its gateway and archive share the call-log path. Each cell preserves the revision, workflow history, report, numerical package, terminal campaign package, calls, and elapsed time. The procedure receives task inputs only.

Answers and kinds enter disclosure after execution. Rejection requires the supplied candidate's actual saved program and executable rejection, followed by an empty shortlist and an accepted no-result interpretation. A repaired or unrelated candidate does not qualify. Support requires cited supported held-back numerical evidence, and estimates come from that evidence.

An unsupported raw positive claim cannot pass a null case merely because the platform classified it inconclusive. Operational failures, exhausted limits, cancellation, missing evidence, malformed final replies, and critic rejection score as failed. An honest completed numerical inconclusive remains eligible for null scoring.

### Freeze the configuration before subscription calls

The immutable live manifest is `17aa0d3af3b7a590598e473baa364a27975c406999d0fc0aaa7bb9cb095f6408`, frozen at `2026-10-23T21:10:53.516905+00:00` from implementation commit `ec2cd37914a3f5b1dcec8a327098bf46b6d213b6`. It seals source, fixture, driver, runtime, and lock hashes, the separate answer key, three repeats, empty starting memory, and the supplied-candidate steering protocol. The researcher role uses `claude-subscription/claude-opus-5-5`, and the critic uses `codex-subscription/gpt-6.1-sol`, without fallbacks. Both model versions and knowledge cutoffs are explicitly unknown.

Runtime checks recorded Claude Code 2.1.301 and Codex CLI 0.162.1. Each cell starts a fresh findings ledger and isolated stores. Only the frozen candidate instructions steer it, and the prompt requires the candidate unchanged once, an explicit shortlist or empty shortlist after rejection, and fresh evidence-based interpretation and critique.

The acceptance rule was fixed before the first live dispatch. Each known-answer case needs at least two successes in three repeats. Every flawed case must be rejected in every repeat, and every null repeat must avoid a positive claim.

A durable counter admits each subscription completion before dispatch and includes retries, conclusions, and critiques. The limits are 150 dispatches across the entire live effort, five per campaign, and fifteen per case across all three repeats. A rate limit stops all later dispatches and preserves its retry time as unknown when the adapter supplies none. No API-key fallback, API billing, or Claude usage credits are enabled.

The measured wall limit is 1,200 seconds per case across all three repeats, including setup, archive publication, and worker cleanup. The driver passes each repeat its remaining allowance, cancels at the deadline, and fails performance acceptance if cleanup exceeds it. Workflow admission is separately 3,600 seconds, with one trial.

A 2,400-second admission left no headroom for the first call's measured wall time before the next reservation. An initial inference that a second trial was needed was disproved by the advisor and a real-worker probe with one trial and the larger wall allowance. The one-trial cap remains. Cancellation is not a hard termination bound for archive retries.

### Check the numerical answer independently

[The pilot inputs](../tests/fixtures/capability/pilot/tasks.json) generate causal synthetic monthly data with separate 30-month search and final windows. They reuse the existing dataset adapter, grammar, backtest engines, evaluation profile, and archived research loop. The positive case alternates market returns of minus and plus five percent. Each decision uses the latest predictor observation available then, from two months earlier. The rule holds bills in negative months and the market otherwise.

The exact net incremental intercept is `2881/120000`, about 0.024008333333333333 a month, with tolerance `1e-12`. This includes twenty-nine switches charged at ten basis points and no initial allocation charge. Independent rational arithmetic and the real numerical pipeline agree.

The never-bills case is exactly the market baseline and records null. The two flawed cases request a negative lag and a vintage after the decision. Their executable reasons are `negative_lag` and `reads_past_vintage`. These are narrow grammar-error cases, not independent scientific flaw families or evidence of open-ended discovery.

### Retain every repeat and its resource use

The targeted offline command `PYTHONPATH=src uv run --offline python -m pytest tests/test_capability.py` passed 94 tests, including all 52 historical cases and 16 real Temporal and scripted-provider scenarios. They cover development reruns and reopening, crashes before dispatch, pending held-out exclusion, legacy identities, committed answers, exact grids, per-case success shares, honest inconclusive results, both flawed candidates, repaired and unrelated candidates, unsupported positive claims, malformed replies, critic rejection, exhausted budgets, rate limits, worker failure, and deadline cancellation. Ruff and diff checks passed. The full suite runs only in `make merge`.

The complete external driver also passed all twelve scripted cells with 48 scripted dispatches, matching admissions, claims, receipts, and charged calls. Separate negative checks refused incomplete, mismatched, unknown-usage, rate-limited, and over-limit summaries before live execution.

The real subscription study completed three repeats for each of four cases. All twelve scores passed, the known-answer share was three of three, all six flawed repeats were rejected, and all three null repeats recorded null. The live effort used 48 dispatches, twelve per case and four per campaign, with every receipt reconciled.

Both roles used the signed-in subscriptions, and their separate-provider critiques were recorded independently. The table reports cumulative measured seconds per case across all three repeats.

| Case | Kind | Repeat 1 | Repeat 2 | Repeat 3 | Calls | Seconds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| case-1 | known_answer | successful, pass | successful, pass | successful, pass | 12 | 80.11 |
| case-2 | null | null, pass | null, pass | null, pass | 12 | 64.59 |
| case-3 | flawed | rejected, pass | rejected, pass | rejected, pass | 12 | 55.17 |
| case-4 | flawed | rejected, pass | rejected, pass | rejected, pass | 12 | 53.00 |

Evidence remains at `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p19-live-20261023`. The frozen manifest is under `freezes/17aa0d3af3b7a590598e473baa364a27975c406999d0fc0aaa7bb9cb095f6408.json`, the shared exposure events under `exposure-ledger`, and all cumulative admissions under `subscription-dispatches`. Execution `282707a8-fc14-4639-b1f1-02e486322abb` is retained at `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p19-live-20261023/executions/dfb64939-ebfd-4e23-98a9-c27557b293e0` with `results.json`, `disclosure.json`, `summary.json`, the Temporal database and logs, and each cell's complete evidence.

`case-wall.jsonl` preserves cumulative timing across invocations. The `preparation` directory keeps the task list, design comparison, advisor result, independent oracle, local review, and targeted test log. The driver and offline probes remain beside these artifacts.

To repeat verification, check out the recorded implementation commit in this original worktree so all sealed hashes and the source path match, then run `PYTHONPATH=src uv run --offline python ~/.codex/artifacts/quant-p19-live-20261023/live_check.py verify 17aa0d3af3b7a590598e473baa364a27975c406999d0fc0aaa7bb9cb095f6408`. The same command with `run` instead of `verify` starts another execution, retains the earlier one, and consumes the existing cumulative budgets. It does not reset them.

The remaining per-case allowance is three calls, so it cannot complete another four-call repeat under this freeze. Do not rerun merely to read the baseline.

Read `disclosure.json` and the campaign packages instead. No P16 campaign was rerun.

The ledger remains a local event store, without registry import. Checksums protect integrity, not adversarial mutation of local files. Internal network retries inside a subscription CLI are not separately observable adapter dispatches.

The supplied-candidate baseline measures controlled execution, numerical verification, and interpretation on known synthetic tasks. It does not show autonomous signal discovery, investment usefulness, firm-framework acceptance, or independently authored held-out capability. The [Phase 4 gate](master-plan.md#phase-4-complete-a-bounded-autonomous-campaign) stays open.

## D070. Register the Estrella and Mishkin working paper and its FRED series

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P21 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#register-the-paper-case-p21). It registers the [Phase 3 paper case](phase0-pilot.md#define-the-phase-3-paper-case) in the source store of [D018](#d018-publish-complete-source-generations-from-a-file-store), locates its elements with the locator of [D043](#d043-locate-paper-elements-in-published-extractions), and ingests its series through the commands of [D052](#d052-ingest-the-pilots-public-data-into-a-vintage-addressed-store). The paper version, the recession series, and the declaration were decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026. On 23 October 2026 the researcher approved the working-paper version, `USRECQ`, the declaration, and the cover's citation notice, accepting the agent's recommendations, and kept the paper unapproved for providers.

The published article is Arturo Estrella and Frederic S. Mishkin, "Predicting U.S. Recessions: Financial Variables as Leading Indicators", The Review of Economics and Statistics 80(1), pages 45 to 61, February 1998, DOI 10.1162/003465398557320. Crossref's record confirms the title, authors, volume, issue, pages, and date. The article is not free. OpenAlex lists it as closed access with no open copy, and RePEc says its full text is restricted to subscribers. Two working-paper versions are free from their publishers:

- NBER Working Paper 5379, dated December 1995, 53 pages, from nber.org.
- Federal Reserve Bank of New York Research Paper 9609, dated May 1996 on its cover and April 1996 on its title page, 61 pages, from newyorkfed.org.

The platform registers Research Paper 9609 and cites it as that working paper, not as the 1998 article. Its abstract matches the published abstract in substance. Both say that stock prices help one to three quarters ahead and that the yield-curve slope is the best single predictor beyond one quarter. They differ only in wording. The working paper says "article" where the published abstract says "paper", writes numerals where it spells numbers out, and lists currencies among the financial variables. The abstract of Working Paper 5379 says one to two quarters and beyond two quarters, so its results differ from the published ones, and it was rejected for that reason. Research Paper 9609's cover says that the paper is preliminary and not for citation or quotation without the authors' permission, and the declaration's rights field records that notice. The published article was not obtained, so differences in its tables, samples, and estimates are unknown. Research Paper 9609 estimates its equations on quarterly data from the first quarter of 1959 to the first quarter of 1995.

Both copies are image scans without a usable text layer. The conversion read every page with Tesseract and macOS Vision and resolved each disagreement between them against the page image. It writes each table as one pipe table, each equation as display math, and each chart as its `Figure N:` caption with a note that the chart is not reproduced. The conversion's correction log records a check of every cell of Tables 1 to 5 in the body against its page image. The P21 agent then checked every cell of Tables 2 and 5 against the page images again, and all of them matched. At conversion time, the appendix checks resolved OCR disagreements and inspected overlay sheets for shared misreadings, without claiming cell-by-cell verification. The conversion's scripts and its correction log stay beside the Markdown.

On 24 October 2026 UTC, a visual inspection compared all 1,984 horizon entries in A1 to A8 with the pinned PDF's page images. Of these, 1,982 matched, including signs and significance markers, and two remain inconclusive because of scan damage. The unresolved readings are the NAPMC `t-stat` numeral at `k=1` in A2 and the BOND `t-stat sp` sign at `k=4` in A4. The rendered values are `-3.99**` and `5.69**`, respectively. Labels, headers, titles, equations, and notes were checked separately and matched. Original-page crops resolved the previously uncertain A1 SP500 stars and the faded A5 M0 and A7 SP500 rows. The inspection established no transcription defect, and conversion 1 and its digest remain unchanged. The reports, source images, overlays, and input digests are preserved in `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-appendix-verification-20261024/`, whose `manifest.json` records every file's hash.

`public_data.Paper` pairs a source declaration with the SHA-256 of the converted Markdown it names, and `public_data.ESTRELLA_MISHKIN` is the registered paper. `register_paper(root, paper, markdown)` refuses bytes with another digest, registers the Markdown in `<root>/sources`, and publishes a chunk generation with `TEXT_PIPELINE` and a locator generation with `MARKDOWN_LOCATOR_V2`. A repeated call returns the same generations. The digest check exists because an occurrence's identity hashes its declaration and not its bytes. Without the check, a fresh store would register any bytes under the paper's provenance. A corrected conversion therefore needs a new revision in the declaration and a new digest.

The declaration records these values:

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `location` | The Research Paper 9609 PDF on newyorkfed.org |
| `revision` | The authors, the title, Research Paper 9609 of May 1996 as the working paper of the 1998 article, and Markdown conversion 1 |
| `acquisition` | The PDF's SHA-256, its download time, and the OCR conversion |
| `rights` | The authors' copyright, the New York Fed's free distribution, and the cover's notice |
| `labels` and `approved_for` | None. D052 leaves provider approvals to the researcher, who kept them empty on 23 October 2026 |
| `published_at` | None, because the cover and the title page give only a month, and they disagree |
| `source_available_at` | 1 June 1996 at 00:00 UTC, the end of the cover's month |
| `received_at` | 23 October 2026 at 02:17:42 UTC, when the PDF was downloaded |

`received_at` is fixed, so the constant describes one acquisition, and a repeated registration lands on the same occurrence. A run whose cutoff is before 1 June 1996 reads neither generation.

The PDF and the Markdown stay outside the repository, because the paper is copyrighted and its cover restricts quotation. The PDF is in `~/quant-research-data/p21-paper-download`, and the Markdown, its scripts, and its correction log are in `~/quant-research-data/p21-paper-conversion`. The repository keeps only their digests:

| File | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| Research Paper 9609 PDF | `529284d889247958197453552b01561f6f0deb738793362efe743e554fbcc02e` |
| Markdown conversion 1 | `317a49b5bf0ac60d1fb3663ff090efa0c33307c639d2a07900b1ae2b3d3943a5` |

`public_data.PAPER_CASE` names the paper case's series. Each one is an `alfred.<series>` dataset that P01's commands pull and ingest unchanged:

| Dataset | FRED series | Vintages begin | Comparison with the paper |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `alfred.gs10` | 10-year constant-maturity Treasury yield, monthly average | 3 December 1996 | The paper's BOND is the 10-year Treasury bond yield |
| `alfred.tb3ms` | 3-month Treasury bill secondary-market rate, discount basis, monthly average | 3 December 1996 | The paper's BILL is a bond-equivalent market yield, so `TB3MS` is adapted data |
| `alfred.usrecq` | NBER-based recession indicator, quarterly | 3 November 2011 | The paper codes recessions by quarter from the standard NBER dates |

`USRECQ` is FRED's interpretation of NBER's quarterly turning points. Its value is 1 from the quarter after a peak through the trough, so the 1990 peak and the 1991 trough mark the fourth quarter of 1990 and the first quarter of 1991. Its vintages change when NBER announces a turning point. The first quarter of 2020 read 0 in vintages before 8 June 2020, and the four quarters from July 2020 read 1 in vintages before 21 July 2021. The monthly `USREC` and NBER's own chronology were not ingested, because the paper works by quarter and NBER's chronology would need a new converter. Every vintage of the three series starts after the paper's 1959 to 1995 sample, so a cutoff inside the sample reads none of them, and P22 reads them at a later cutoff as adapted data. Each series' catalog documentation states its units and its first vintage, and the documentation of the other series is unchanged.

On 22 October 2026 the live check ran in a fresh data root, `~/quant-research-data/p21-live-20261022`. Two pulls and two ingests of the three series gave the same download digests, vintages, and catalog digest. An agent validated and approved them with an attestation that names this PR's live check and not the researcher's validation. A read at a 1 December 1996 cutoff supplied no row of any series. A read at a 22 October 2026 cutoff supplied 882 months of `GS10`, 1,113 months of `TB3MS`, and 688 quarters of `USRECQ`. `register_paper` registered conversion 1 twice and returned the same generations. The chunk generation holds 256 chunks, and the locator generation holds 13 tables, 4 figures, 17 equations, and 58 claims. A run with a 31 May 1996 cutoff read neither generation. The ref of Table 2, which gives in-sample fits of single-variable models, looked up text equal to its span of the Markdown. macOS Vision then read page 15 again from a fresh render of the PDF, and the digits of all 126 decimal cells matched the located text. Vision missed the minus sign of 13 cells and stars of 3 cells, so an agent checked every cell, sign, and star of the table against the page image. All matched.

These limits are deliberate:

- The registered paper is the 1996 working paper. A table or estimate may differ from the 1998 article, and nothing here checks one against the other.
- Two appendix entries remain inconclusive because of scan damage. The later inspection did not verify prose, whose conversion checks resolved OCR disagreements and included the additional image and spot checks recorded in the correction log. Shared OCR misreadings can remain. A match to the printed scan does not establish numerical or econometric correctness.
- The charts are not reproduced, so a figure location holds only its caption.
- Provider approvals are empty by the researcher's decision, so no subscription route may receive the paper's text. Approving one changes the declaration, and an occurrence's identity hashes its declaration. The paper's chunk and locator generations then get new IDs, and P22's specification pins the current ones.
- The researcher's data root holds the paper and its three series, validated and approved on 23 October 2026 ([pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#pin-the-data)).

## D071. Reproduce Table 2's SPREAD probit on adapted FRED data

Selected on 22 October 2026 for P22 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#specify-and-run-the-paper-reproduction-p22). It applies the rules of [D020](#d020-fix-the-reproduction-comparison-before-results) to the paper case that [D070](#d070-register-the-estrella-and-mishkin-working-paper-and-its-fred-series) registered, and it changes none of them. The table, the model, the comparison basis, every implementation choice, and the tolerances were decided by an agent under the user's autonomy grant on 22 October 2026. The researcher approved them on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation. That keeps the frozen 1959Q1 sample start, takes the pseudo R² formula from Research Paper 9716, leaves the eight t-statistics inconclusive until a source states the Newey-West lag, and keeps Table 2's 16 printed numbers in the code. The plan asks the researcher to review the comparison basis before the run. That gate did not pass, because the approval came after the run.

The reproduction targets Research Paper 9609, the May 1996 working paper, and not the 1998 article. Its Table 2 gives in-sample fits of probit models with one explanatory variable over horizons of 1 to 8 quarters. The row chosen is SPREAD, the 10-year Treasury yield minus the 3-month bill rate, because the paper's conclusions rest on it. Each horizon has two printed cells, a pseudo R² and the slope's t-statistic, so the specification has 16 targets. Its comparison basis is adapted data, because every input differs from the paper's in some way:

- The ALFRED vintages of `GS10`, `TB3MS`, and `USRECQ` all begin after the paper's sample, so the run reads values revised since 1996.
- The paper's bill rate is a market yield on a bond-equivalent basis from an internal New York Fed source. `TB3MS` is a monthly average on a discount basis, and the run converts it with an approximation.
- `USRECQ` is FRED's quarterly coding of the NBER dates, and the paper does not say how it codes a peak quarter.

[quant_research.probit](../src/quant_research/probit.py) holds the frozen `SPECIFICATION`. It pins the D070 locator generation `6a49b4eb7b1aab159a3b26c1a797cfb5257454696bf89fc46a156f37f4530a2f` with digest `09013031e8c0d9337b7c7298a777b887ceaa0a70b6f3f42b63fa0d517e88bcb9`, and its identity is `391795111a3e1d743a8a19f7ddd46372df77f8d96d490f326d5337ada4639899`. Its data field names the vintages read, the dataset adapter, and the cutoff of 22 October 2026 at 00:00 UTC. The equation, table, and claim locators resolve against the pinned generation when the specification freezes. Passage locators name spans of the D070 chunk generation `ec99feef…` in the same form, and they stay opaque, as D020 says. The module holds the 16 printed numbers and no other text from the paper.

| Choice | Value | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `model` | `P(R_{t+k} = 1) = Phi(a0 + a1 SPREAD_t)`, fit by maximum likelihood, one equation for each horizon k from 1 to 8 | Equation 1, the likelihood, and Table 2's model line |
| `sample` | SPREAD in quarters 1959Q1 through 1995Q1 minus k, paired with R k quarters later, so n is 145 minus k | The sample paragraph |
| `spread` | The quarter's mean of monthly `GS10` minus the bond-equivalent `TB3MS` yield, in percentage points | Table 1 and Appendix B |
| `bill` | Each monthly discount rate d becomes `365 d / (360 - 91 d)` in decimal terms, a fixed 91-day maturity applied to the monthly average | Appendix B, which gives the bond-equivalent basis |
| `recession` | `USRECQ`, which is 1 from the quarter after an NBER peak through the trough | The recession coding equation and the NBER dates paragraph |
| `pseudo_r2` | Estrella's measure `1 - (ln Lu / ln Lc)^(-(2/n) ln Lc)`, with Lc the likelihood of a constant-only model | The paper's citation of Estrella (1995), and page 8 of Estrella's Research Paper 9716 |
| `t_statistic` | The slope over its Newey-West standard error, with Bartlett weights, lag k minus 1, and the sandwich of the probit scores around the observed Hessian | The paper's t-statistic sentence and its footnote 3 |

The open question `newey_west_lag` is of kind `missing`. The paper says that its t-statistics apply Newey-West to the probit first-order conditions, but it names no lag, kernel, or Hessian form. Estrella and Rodrigues later published the method as Federal Reserve Bank of New York Staff Report 39, dated April 1998. Its simulations compare the quasi-maximum-likelihood estimator, Newey-West with one lag, Hansen with one lag, and Newey-West with Andrews' optimal lag, and they do not say which one Research Paper 9609 used. Every t-statistic target depends on the `t_statistic` choice, so all eight are inconclusive, and the run still records their observed values.

The paper cites the pseudo R² as Estrella (1995), "Measures of Fit with Dichotomous Dependent Variables: Critical Review and a New Proposal", a New York Fed research paper. A search found no copy of that version. The formula above is from Research Paper 9716, "A New Measure of Fit for Equations with Dichotomous Dependent Variables", dated May 1997 and forthcoming in the Journal of Business and Economic Statistics, where it appeared in volume 16, issue 2, pages 198 to 205, in 1998. The agent read the formula on its page 8 and checked the cover's date and title. The specification treats this measure as the one the paper used, because the author and the measure are the same. The PDFs of both New York Fed papers stay outside the repository:

| File | Location | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Research Paper 9716 | `~/quant-research-data/p22-estrella-fit/9716.pdf` | `bea3994336c69d6a6099a3d2816c622f36ab5a6c6b80f1a28fccd50990b62053` |
| Staff Report 39 | `~/quant-research-data/p22-estrella-rodrigues/sr39.pdf` | `3e0fe9f9965d80f3b8af88227292e57059400162fbd96aadc61cfbf51b0d2d03` |

Each tolerance is half a unit in the last printed digit, 0.0005 for a pseudo R² and 0.005 for a t-statistic, with no relative part. A verdict of reproduced therefore means that the run gives the printed value after rounding. No source supports a wider allowance for adapted data, so the specification declares none. Read the distances below together with the verdicts.

These alternatives were rejected:

- A tolerance of 10 percent of the printed value or 0.01. Nothing in the paper or the data supports either number.
- An open question on the sample window. It would have made all 16 targets inconclusive. The paper says that it chose its start date because comparable data for all series were available from then. The agent read that sentence as fixing the explanatory variable's first quarter at 1959Q1, so the run keeps every value inside the paper's window. Codex's review before the run disputed that reading, and the researcher's review should settle it.
- `TB3MS` on its discount basis. Appendix B gives the bill rate on a bond-equivalent basis, so the conversion moves the input closer to the paper.

`probit.fit` estimates a probit by Newton's method with the observed Hessian, in plain Python. It stops after a step that moves no fitted index by more than 1e-6, so a regressor's units do not change where it stops. It computes the normal tails with `math.erfc`, so the repository gains no dependency. numpy and scipy are installed only as dependencies of vectorbt. The covariance is the Bartlett-weighted Newey-West sandwich of the scores at the requested lag, without a degrees-of-freedom correction. `fit` refuses data that it cannot estimate. Those cases are mismatched or ragged input, outcomes that are not all 0 or 1 or that are all equal, regressors that are not finite, too few observations, negative lags, a fit that does not converge in 100 iterations, a singular Hessian, and a fitted probability that rounds to 0 or 1. Its tests compare a saturated probit with one binary regressor, whose estimates have a closed form, and a constant-only model with alternating outcomes, whose Newey-West variance can be worked out by hand. `probit.observe` reads the three series through the dataset adapter and refuses a missing month or quarter. It fits the eight horizons and returns their observations with the adapter's read records.

The specification was committed in `e828ee6` at 04:02:14 UTC on 23 October 2026, before any probit ran on the real series. The live check froze it at 04:05:11 UTC and then estimated. It ran in a fresh data root, `~/quant-research-data/p22-live-20261022`. The agent pulled and ingested the three series there at 03:40 UTC, and their vintages equal the ones D070 ingested. It validated and approved them with an attestation that names this PR's live check and not the researcher's validation. `register_paper` published the same chunk and locator generations as in D070. The adapter supplied every month and quarter from 1959Q1 through 1995Q1. The harness and its JSON record are in `~/quant-research-data/p22-live-check`, and the record keeps the canonical specification, the read records, the observations, and every verdict with its distance. Codex's review of this PR found that the first version stopped when no coefficient moved by more than 1e-6, which depends on the regressors' units. After the fix in `1ff5881`, a rerun of the same frozen specification, with the same identity, changed no observation by more than 5.1e-13 and no verdict. Its record is `p22-live-check-2.json` in the same directory.

| k | Pseudo R², printed | Pseudo R², run | Verdict | t, printed | t, run | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 0.071 | 0.0611 | not reproduced | -2.71 | -2.869 | inconclusive |
| 2 | 0.211 | 0.1966 | not reproduced | -4.21 | -4.271 | inconclusive |
| 3 | 0.271 | 0.2692 | not reproduced | -4.71 | -5.083 | inconclusive |
| 4 | 0.296 | 0.2866 | not reproduced | -4.57 | -4.722 | inconclusive |
| 5 | 0.256 | 0.2338 | not reproduced | -3.87 | -4.028 | inconclusive |
| 6 | 0.149 | 0.1356 | not reproduced | -4.13 | -3.862 | inconclusive |
| 7 | 0.078 | 0.0747 | not reproduced | -3.02 | -2.930 | inconclusive |
| 8 | 0.031 | 0.0311 | reproduced | -1.63 | -1.584 | inconclusive |

Every verdict is on adapted data. The live check passed, because each of the 16 targets has a classification with its tolerance. The reproduction's outcome is `not_reproduced_on_adapted_data`. One pseudo R² reproduces, and the other seven miss by 0.0018 to 0.0222, all of them below the printed value. The run's pseudo R² rises to its largest value at four quarters and then falls, as the printed row does. Every run t-statistic has the printed sign, and only the eight-quarter one is below 2 in size, as in the paper. These are observations, not classifications.

These limits are deliberate:

- The causes of the gaps are not established. The bill conversion's fixed maturity and its use on monthly averages, revisions since 1996, and the recession coding are candidates, and none was tested. No variant ran after the results, so this specification stays the record. A later reproduction that changes a choice after seeing these numbers needs a new specification that says so.
- The t-statistics stay inconclusive until a source states the Newey-West lag that the paper used.
- The 1998 article was not obtained, so its tables may differ from the working paper's.
- The data root is the agent's. The researcher's data root has held the paper and its series since 23 October 2026, with the same vintages ([pilot brief](phase0-pilot.md#pin-the-data)).

## D072. Skip gate tests whose exact inputs already passed

Chosen on 22 October 2026, when the user asked to shorten verification again. This changes the runner that AGENTS.md calls finished. One full run took 393 s at a load average of 46 to 57 on 14 cores, and `tests/test_repo_gates.py` took 1,275 of the 1,447 summed test seconds. Those tests check the repository runner, and few PRs change what they read. With this change, four full runs that skipped them took 43 to 90 s at load averages of 64 to 242, and two runs that included them took 189 and 357 s.

Full and smoke suites hash the gate inputs with the prepared identity and `git --version`. The inputs are `tools/`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`, `examples/`, `tests/test_repo_gates.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/fixtures/fake_gh.py`, and `tests/fixtures/wheel_consumer.py`. The prepared identity already covers `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `.python-version`, the uv version, and the Python runtime. When a full suite passes, wheel checks included, it writes an empty receipt named by that digest under `prepared/gate-passes` in the Git common directory. A later full or smoke suite with the same digest passes `--ignore=tests/test_repo_gates.py` to pytest, and the full suite passes it to the collection count and every shard. Either suite prints `Skipped tests/test_repo_gates.py: a full suite already passed the same gate inputs` and the receipt prefix just before its `Passed` line. Smoke therefore drops its three gate checks from [D044](#d044-shard-the-full-suite-and-keep-the-smoke-suite-small) in commit and push hooks once a full suite has passed them. With them skipped, `make smoke-index` took 4 to 5 s, against 18 to 33 s for the commit hooks that ran them while this change was made. Only a passing full suite writes a receipt, and a failed one writes none. A snapshot without `tests/test_repo_gates.py`, such as a gate fixture repository, runs every test and writes nothing. Coverage never skips. Linked worktrees share the receipts, so each new set of gate inputs runs the gate tests once per machine. Deleting `gate-passes` forces a rerun. The manual hosted workflow starts with no receipts, so it always runs the gate tests.

A content key was chosen over a diff against `origin/main`. Nothing verifies merged `main` as a whole, so a skip based on the merge base could pass over a combination of gate changes that no run tested. The digest names the exact bytes that passed.

These costs are accepted:

- The key leaves out `src/`. Eight gate tests copy `src/` into a fixture to exercise the wheel build, `make run`, and `make example`. A change in `src/` that breaks only those tests, such as a new top-level import that the fixture's small environment lacks, appears on the next run whose gate inputs change. The application's own tests run the CLI and the authoring example, and every full run still builds and exercises the real wheel.
- `tests/conftest.py` is a gate input, so each change to it makes the next full run on the machine run the gate tests once, even in a PR that changes no tooling.
- A receipt records a pass on this machine. A change outside the key, such as the host's Git configuration or file system, can change the result without changing the digest.
- `make verify` and the hooks no longer always run the gate tests, so a PR body that cites a run must report the skip line when it appears.

## D073. Bound the campaign runner's per-event cost and payloads

Selected on 22 October 2026 to close three limits of [D042](#d042-run-each-campaign-as-one-temporal-workflow-that-owns-its-logs). Each let a long or unusual run stall: a failure report with undecodable details, folds that re-read every recorded event, and a record larger than one Temporal payload.

Temporal converts a reported failure, including its error details, before workflow code runs. A detail in an unknown encoding therefore raised during conversion and failed every workflow task. `execution.worker` now gives the worker a client whose failure converter leaves error details as raw payloads. Workflow code reads only a failure's type, so an undecodable detail ends its attempt as `failed`, like any other reported failure, and the next attempt runs.

The [ledger](#d016-account-for-resources-in-an-append-only-ledger) and the [acceptance log](#d030-accept-results-through-explicit-events) now keep folds that advance by each new event, as those entries describe. Before, every call re-folded every recorded event, so the runner's work grew with the square of its attempts. The events, their stored bytes, and every refusal are unchanged. A differential harness drove main and this change with the same random ledger and acceptance traffic over 200 seeds. The traffic covered every refusal limit, exactness errors, identity clashes, repeats, and retries. Both versions printed byte-identical transcripts, including the stored ledger bytes and the dumped acceptance log.

Other sessions loaded the 14-core development machine during these measurements, so each names its load average:

- An offline fold of the runner's per-attempt events for 1,000 attempts took 16.5 to 18.5 seconds before this change and 0.3 to 0.6 seconds after it, under a load near 40.
- Under a load near 95, the same fold took 36 to 60 seconds before and 0.26 to 0.30 seconds after at 1,000 attempts, and 213 to 215 seconds before and 1.3 to 4.1 seconds after at 2,000 attempts.
- Replaying one 1,000-attempt history with the SDK's `Replayer` took 28.4 to 30.0 seconds of CPU with main's code and 3.2 to 3.4 seconds with this change. Under a load near 50, its wall time was 42 to 47 seconds and 7.4 to 9.0 seconds.

The SDK refuses a payload over 2 MiB and warns over 512 KiB. In a test before this change, it refused a 2.4 MB record, so the run could not complete. The workflow now returns a `Summary` of its status and spend, and the `summary` query returns the same while the run is open. `execution.record` waits for the run to close, reads the `Record` through a `part` query 256 KiB at a time, and decodes the joined bytes. A closed run cannot change, so its parts never mix two states. No other payload grows with the attempt count. The trial limit bounds the plan, the revision bounds each activity input, the body bounds each report, and its arguments bound each update.

Two alternatives were rejected. Continue-as-new bounds history, but its input would carry the same growing logs and meet the same payload limit. Reading the parts of an open run could mix states, because the run can advance between parts.

[The runner tests](../tests/test_execution.py) fail a report with undecodable details and read back a 2.4 MB record. [The ledger tests](../tests/test_accounting.py) and [the acceptance tests](../tests/test_acceptance.py) bound the CPU time of 1,000 and 4,000 retries of one run. A ledger test shows that a refused reservation and a failed charge leave later calls unaffected.

These limits remain:

- Replay still costs the SDK about 3 milliseconds of CPU per attempt. A run of a few thousand attempts can therefore exceed the 10-second workflow task timeout after a worker restart.
- History grows with every attempt. Temporal limits a run's history to 51,200 events or 50 MB, per [its limits page](https://docs.temporal.io/workflow-execution/limits). An attempt adds about six events, so a run past about 8,500 attempts would reach that limit. Continue-as-new waits until the logs live outside workflow state.
- Each part query of a closed run replays its history on a worker.
- Steering appends still re-parse the steering log, which grows only with researcher commands.
- Each report is still one payload, so bodies keep bulk output in packages or files, as replay and generated code do.

## D074. Run the full suite only inside make merge

Chosen on 22 October 2026, when one day's work ran 236 passing full suites across about 100 PRs, about 45 machine-hours, on top of a smoke suite on every commit and push. Sessions reran the full suite after fixes, review rounds, and merges of newer main, and nothing tied a passing run to the commit that landed.

`make merge PR=<n>` is now the only supported way to merge. It holds an advisory lock on `repo-merge.lock` in the Git common directory for the whole run, so merges from this clone's worktrees run one at a time. A second merge prints the holder's PR, worktree, and start time, then waits. The PR must be open, ready for review, and from a branch of this repository. The checkout must be on that branch, with no tracked changes, at the PR's head. When the fetched base is not an ancestor of the head, `make merge` merges it without the merge-commit hook and pushes the result. A conflict aborts that merge and asks for a manual one. A diff against the base that touches only `docs/**/*.md`, top-level `*.md` files, or `.claude/**` runs the smoke suite, and any other diff runs the full suite. Markdown under `tests/fixtures/` counts as code, because tests read it. If the base moves during the run, `make merge` merges it again and reruns. It fails if the PR head on GitHub moved or the PR was retargeted to another base. Then it posts the `Passed` line as a PR comment and runs `gh pr merge --merge --match-head-commit` with the verified commit. It does not wait for Codex review.

Each suite now runs all of its steps to the end, then prints one summary of every failed step. Before, a ruff failure stopped the run before any test. Each full-suite shard appends every failed test phase to its own file, and the parent prints new lines about twice a second, so fixing can start before the run ends. No shard stops at its first failure.

The push hook is deleted. The commit hook still runs the smoke suite on the staged tree. A `pre-merge-commit` hook runs it on a merge result, and a `post-rewrite` hook runs it on HEAD after a rebase. A Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook in `.claude/settings.json` blocks Claude sessions from running `gh pr merge`, `make verify`, or `make coverage`. The manual hosted workflow is deleted.

Three alternatives were rejected:

- Parallel merges that rerun when main moves. Main could move under a passing run, and every move would cost another full run.
- A GitHub ruleset that requires a passing status. Rulesets on a private repository need a paid plan.
- Waiting for Codex review inside `make merge`. The lock would wait on an outside reviewer.

This changes three earlier records. The push check of outgoing branch tips that [D005](#d005-verify-disposable-snapshots-with-prepared-dependencies) describes is gone. In [D044](#d044-shard-the-full-suite-and-keep-the-smoke-suite-small), the smoke suite runs on commits, merge commits, and rebases instead of commits and pushes, and shards report failing tests as they fail instead of after every shard finishes. In [D072](#d072-skip-gate-tests-whose-exact-inputs-already-passed), the `make merge` output still shows the skip line, but its PR comment carries only the `Passed` line, and no hosted run exists.

These limits remain:

- A merge from the GitHub website, from another clone, or through `gh pr merge` outside Claude Code bypasses `make merge`.
