- Git ignores the exit status of `post-rewrite`, so a rebase that fails the smoke suite still completes. The hook only reports it.
- A push runs no suite, so a branch can reach GitHub with a commit that never passed smoke, for example after `git commit --no-verify`.
- The lock lives in one clone's Git common directory. Another clone on the same machine takes a different lock.
- The documentation rule trusts paths. A change under `.claude/`, including the merge hook itself, runs only the smoke suite at merge, so the hook's tests belong to the smoke suite.
- `make merge` runs from the installed runner, so each clone must run `make hooks` once after this change lands.
- The Claude Code hook reads simple commands only. A command run through `bash -c`, `env`, or a script passes.

## D075. Run the local pilot track ahead of the master-plan phase order

Approved on 22 October 2026 by the researcher, who holds every Phase 0 role under [D050](#d050-run-the-phase-0-pilot-locally-on-public-us-macro-data).

The master plan's [delivery sequence](master-plan.md#delivery-sequence) puts the Phase 2 workspace and Phase 3's provider acceptance before the Phase 4 autonomous pilot. The firm's systems are not available, and one person holds every role, so the [autonomous pilot PR plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md) builds a bounded autonomous session on one machine first. It skips the Phase 2 console, keeps campaign logs in Temporal history and the final package instead of SurrealDB, and leaves the Phase 3 paper case optional. The master plan records the track as an approved change to its order.

These limits are deliberate:

- The approval changes the order of the work, not any exit gate. Each phase completes only on the evidence its gate names.
- The local session does not complete Phase 4. Its gate needs a held-out capability comparison, whose answer keys must come from someone outside development.
- Local acceptance does not accept the firm frameworks. The firm port first reruns the frozen public inputs and must agree with the local results, per D050.
- The Phase 2 and Phase 3 gates stay open, including the parts the track skips.

## D076. Drive the research loop from the CLI and reconcile its receipts in status

Selected on 23 October 2026 for P13 of the [autonomous pilot plan](autonomous-pilot-prs.md#drive-campaigns-from-the-cli-p13). The plan assigns P13 no decision number, so this entry takes the next number above D075. An agent decided the design under the user's autonomy grant on 23 October 2026, after an explainer, two design candidates, a cross-judge, an advisor review, and a Codex review. The researcher approved it on 23 October 2026 by accepting the coordinating agent's recommendation.

[The CLI](../src/quant_research/__main__.py) adds `quant-research campaign` with six commands over the client of [D063](#d063-run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-as-one-temporal-workflow-per-campaign). `start` takes a revision file as `campaigns.save_revision` writes it and `--task-queue`, the queue a research worker polls. `steer` takes a child revision file, whose `reason` is the steer's text and whose `author` is its actor. `pause`, `resume`, and `cancel` take a campaign ID, `--actor`, and `--reason`. `status` takes a campaign ID and `--call-log`, the call log directory that every research worker of the queue writes. Every command takes `--address`, which defaults to the dev server's `127.0.0.1:7233`. Each command prints one JSON object. A failure prints `Campaign <command> failed: <reason>` and exits 1, and a usage error exits 2, as elsewhere in the CLI. A refused update prints the workflow's own refusal, not Temporal's wrapper text. Researchers still write revisions from Python, and the CLI only reads them.

Text reaches a model only when its restrictions are declared, per D063. `pause`, `resume`, `cancel`, and `steer` therefore take `--available-at`, a UTC time ending in `Z`, with repeatable `--label` and `--approved-for`. `--label` or `--approved-for` without `--available-at` is refused. The CLI has no default declaration, because a declaration is the researcher's claim about the text, and only the workflow knows the floor the declaration must fit. The CLI checks no rule that the workflow owns, such as the floor, the 4 KiB bound, a blank actor, or the reserved `runner` actor.

The unit tests run the CLI with `python -B -I -S`, where `temporalio` cannot load. Each command is therefore parsed into a frozen record, with its revision parsed, its declaration built, and its call log replayed, before the first Temporal import. Only one coroutine imports `temporalio` and `quant_research.research`, and nothing on the campaign path imports SurrealDB. A test runs every valid command under `-I -S` and requires it to stop only at the missing `temporalio`, and another test checks that importing the CLI loads neither Temporal, SurrealDB, the research module, nor the store.

`status` asks Temporal whether the loop's workflow still runs. A running loop answers through `research.status`, which carries no ledger, and a closed loop answers through `research.record`, which carries it. The output is the loop's report without the steering log, with `ledger` holding `Ledger.report()` cost lines once the loop closes and `null` before, and with `receipts`. The call log is read after the report, so it holds every call the report counts. A loop that failed, was terminated, or timed out makes `record` raise, and `status` prints the cause and exits 1.

The receipt reconciliation checks the workflow's ledger against the gateway's call log of [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts). The call log names no campaign, so the campaign's attempts select its entries from a log that other campaigns share. Every sent call record holds one model call, and the loop charges wall time and tool compute with none, so the ledger's charges with a model call are its receipt charges.

- After the loop closes, the attempts are the ledger's reservations. Each sent call record joins the charge under its call ID. A charge that repeats the call's attempt, role, and usage is matched. A charge that differs only by D056's fallback for a cost the ledger cannot hold exactly is `cost_unreported`. Any other charge `differs`. A sent call without a charge is `uncharged`, a charge without a sent call is `unrecorded`, and a claim without a record is `unsealed`.
- While the loop runs, P12's status carries no ledger, so the check counts. The attempts are those of the step records. The loop closes an ask's lease and charges its receipts before it records the step, so `pending`, the spent model calls minus the sent calls of those attempts, is never negative on the queue's own log. A held conclusion leaves it positive. `unclosed` names a recorded attempt without a `Closed` entry, and `unsealed` names its claims without a record, because `Gateway.close` stores `Closed` before it seals claims.

`agreed` is true when every list is empty and `pending` is not negative. `status` prints the JSON and exits 1 with `Receipt reconciliation found disagreements` when it is false, as `reconcile` exits 1 on a disagreement.

[The CLI tests](../tests/test_campaign_cli.py) cover each command's missing and foreign arguments, unreadable and damaged revision files, bad declarations, unreplayable call logs, the lazy imports, and every reconciliation bucket against a real gateway, call log, and ledger. An end-to-end test drives an in-process worker with `ScriptedProvider` on the session's Temporal dev server through every command in about 20 seconds. It also pins the server's answers. An update on a closed loop and a status of an unknown campaign fail with `NOT_FOUND` text, and a terminated loop's status fails with the server's termination text. A declared steer reason reaches a later request, and no undeclared reason reaches any request.

The live check on 23 October 2026 pulled `alfred.sahmrealtime` and `french.ff3_monthly` into a fresh data root outside the repository and approved them for `codex-subscription`. The approvals carry an attestation that names this PR's live check and are not the researcher's validation. A worker process answered with `ScriptedProvider` on the Codex route, so no subscription call was made. Each command ran as `python -m quant_research campaign`, and `status` showed each change. `status` failed for the campaign before `start`. `start` left the loop paused by the runner's checkpoint after step 2. `steer` changed the head revision to the child. `resume` made the loop run. `pause` made it pause with the researcher's actor and reason. A second `resume` made it run, and `cancel` ended it cancelled after 4 steps. The closed loop's status showed 2 cost lines and 4 receipts matched under 5 reservations, and the same status with an empty call log exited 1 with 4 `unrecorded` charges. The declared steer reason reached both requests made under the child, and neither undeclared reason reached any request. The first run of the check raced a `resume` against the runner's checkpoint after step 4. The checkpoint paused the loop again after the resume ended the researcher's pause, as D063 allows. The script and its output are kept outside the repository with the session notes.

Alternatives were rejected:

- Reading a running loop's ledger through the `part` query. D063 documents that query for a closed loop only, and a report longer than one part would join bytes from different moments.
- An optional `--call-log`. Every status would then have two shapes, and the reconciliation the plan asks for would be opt-in.
- One `--declare` flag in `steering.dump_declaration`'s JSON form, which is hard to type correctly in a shell.
- A `--timeout`. A steer waits for its child's opening, which can take more than a minute, so a timeout could report a steer that succeeded as failed.
- A required `--address`. The local dev server's default address serves every pilot command.
- Choosing `record` by the report's `ending`. A terminated loop has no ending and would read as running forever.
- Counts only, with no verdict, while the loop runs. A healthy loop never makes `pending` negative or leaves a recorded attempt unclosed, so the verdict raises no false alarm.

This changes two earlier records. [D063](#d063-run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-as-one-temporal-workflow-per-campaign) had no command that drives the loop. In [D013](#d013-version-campaign-specifications-as-content-addressed-revisions), the CLI now reads revision files.

These limits are deliberate:

- No command runs a research worker. A worker needs a provider route, a call log, and an operator `Setup`, which have no file format yet, so the live check and P20 run one from a script.
- Temporal answers a query by replaying the history on a worker, so `status` needs a worker on the campaign's queue even after the loop closes. Every check here had a worker polling, so what a command does without one was not measured.
- A running status checks counts, not each call. It cannot see a charge whose usage differs from its record until the loop closes.
- A researcher's `resume` ends every pause in effect, so a checkpoint reached right after it pauses the loop again, and the researcher resumes twice.
- The CLI has no command for `research.control`'s `annotate`, so a declared reason on `pause`, `resume`, or `cancel` is the only way a researcher's note reaches a prompt without a steer.
- `ledger_receipts` and `step_receipts` live in `__main__.py`, because P13 owns no other module. They are pure functions of the ledger and the call log entries and can move beside them.
- The end-to-end test spends about 20 seconds, mostly on 17 CLI processes that each import Temporal and connect.

The worker and live-status limits above are superseded by [D078](#d078-run-campaign-workers-and-reconcile-live-receipts-under-bounded-steers). The original closed receipt and archive contracts remain unchanged.

## D077. Align recovery records and pinned lesson authority

Selected on 23 October 2026 to complete five compatible items in [to dos](todos.md) in one PR. An agent chose the design under the user's autonomy grant after an advisor review, two design candidates, and a cross-judge. The researcher has not reviewed this decision. These are storage, replay, access, and input-handling changes, with no new research result or phase acceptance.

[Runner activities](../src/quant_research/activities.py) own one decorated `close` implementation, inherited by both workers. It keeps the registered Temporal name `close`, the `Lease` input, the serialized receipt output, and the existing scheduling and settlement rules. Compute-only campaign workers still need no gateway. `execution.start` reads an absent `plan` memo as `None`, so its existing plan comparison refuses a campaign whose research loop already owns the workflow ID through `RunnerError`. This completes the close and memo follow-up in [D063](#d063-run-the-platform-owned-research-loop-as-one-temporal-workflow-per-campaign).

[records.keep_object](../src/quant_research/records.py) owns the SHA-256 name, exclusive write, and comparison with an existing object's bytes. Sources, public data, and provider transcripts keep their directory creation and error translation. Source registration still binds the occurrence before writing its blob and preserves `IdentityConflict` separately from corrupt blob errors. The helper stores original bytes, creates no directory, and never replaces a corrupt object. This removes the duplicate write mechanism recorded in [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters).

[Provider records](../src/quant_research/providers.py) append a defaulted singular `reason` to `Failed`, `RateLimited`, `Cancelled`, and `CallRecord`, preserving their existing positional constructors. The singular reason is a bounded token describing an adapter failure. The plural `reasons` remains the independent list of policy refusals. Both real adapters map their local causes and known provider classifications into the fixed vocabulary. Unknown provider classifications map to `provider_error`. Backing exceptions and provider error text never become a reason. Raw diagnostic names remain in the existing worker log.

New call-log entries use schema 4. Readers accept the exact schema 3 and schema 4 shapes, including a mixed log, without rewriting old entries. Recorded activity results have no schema envelope, so their reader accepts both complete old and new record shapes separately. An absent historical diagnosis stays `None`, as does an ambiguous `unknown` seal. The seal still compares exactly with the record derived from its original claim. Receipts, request and reply bytes, their digests, and stored reply recovery keep their previous contracts. Recovery reuses a stored reply and never resends its call ID. This completes the failure-reason follow-up in [D057](#d057-call-claude-code-as-a-tool-disabled-completion-behind-a-local-first-turn-gate) and [D058](#d058-call-codex-through-a-fresh-app-server-in-an-isolated-home).

[Ledger.admissible_lesson_restrictions](../src/quant_research/findings.py) returns admitted IDs with the restrictions that admitted them from the exact prefix named by the run's memory pin. `admissible_lessons` projects the IDs and keeps its public return type. `read_lessons` consumes the supplied restrictions instead of consulting current declarations. Eligibility, context validation, and the read check remain one Ledger operation. Later declarations and suspensions affect a new pin, while an earlier pin retains its authority and rendered material. Repeated declarations still cannot loosen the current ledger's restrictions. Current-ledger review operations keep their existing behavior. This supersedes [D059](#d059-render-each-tool-call-as-gateway-material-under-the-restrictions-of-what-it-read)'s use of current declarations for a pinned lesson's send restrictions.

[Reconciliation](../src/quant_research/reconcile.py) refuses a selected trial with no attempts through `ReconcileError` before indexing its last attempt. Reference validation still runs first, and the existing no-selected-trial refusal stays distinct. A package may still contain a planned trial with no attempts, per [D060](#d060-let-a-package-record-no-selected-trial). The two-line refusal is necessary because that valid package state supplies no diagnostic to reconcile.

Verification uses offline fixtures, fake adapter executables, and the qualified local Temporal dev server. The affected storage tests preserve golden bytes, identity conflicts, directory behavior, repeat writes, and corrupt-object refusal. Provider and adapter tests cover version 3 recovery, mixed-version append and reopen, old activity results, exact unknown seals, positional constructors, fixed reasons, and secret-bearing unknown classifications. Lesson tests compare old and new pins after restriction and review changes, including identical replay bytes and material. Execution and activity tests exercise registered close contracts, receipt recovery, worker replacement, cancellation, replay, and a real open research loop's ownership refusal. A saved and reopened package with a selected empty trial now produces one CLI failure line without a traceback. The PR records the completed targeted checks and the merge gate's `Passed` line.

These limits remain:

- Arbitrary `CampaignRun` bodies still dispatch inside an activity and do not use workflow-only `ask_model`. Their cancellation conversion remains a separate unchecked protocol change. `Body` and `Dispatch` signatures are unchanged.
- Stop and upgrade every worker sharing a call log or queue before the first schema 4 write. Version 3 workers cannot read schema 4 entries or new activity results.
- Adapter outcome classification is unchanged. A terminal-only Claude cap or an object-shaped Codex cap keeps its existing `provider_error` outcome.
- Historical and ambiguous reasons remain unknown. No missing diagnosis is reconstructed from exception text.
- Pinned memory is historical authority. New contexts must pin a later head to observe later declarations or suspensions.
- The adapter and workflow checks make no live subscription call and establish no held-out capability evidence. The master plan's Phase 4 gate stays open.


## D078. Run campaign workers and reconcile live receipts under bounded steers

Selected on 23 October 2026 to complete three campaign-operations to dos in one PR after P17 merged. An agent chose the design under the user's autonomy grant after subsystem grounding, an advisor review, two design candidates and a cross-judge. The researcher has not reviewed this decision. The work adds operating commands and evidence checks, with no new scientific result or phase acceptance.

[The worker configuration](../src/quant_research/research_worker.py) gives `campaign worker CONFIG_FILE` one strict JSON version-one deployment record. It names a Temporal address and queue, a shared call log, an archive root, the operator's pilot `Setup` and one or both accepted subscription routes. Unknown fields, duplicate keys, invalid types and missing inputs fail before Temporal imports or a connection. Relative filesystem paths use the configuration's parent. Version one requires the resolved call-log root to be disjoint from each publication root, including parent and child paths. This is a conservative input contract that keeps the exclusively numbered call-log namespace under one owner; it does not assert that every ancestor layout would corrupt a store. The two publication roots may overlap. Bare executable names use `PATH`. Configuration files contain paths to sign-in files, never sign-in contents. [Architecture](architecture.md#drive-a-research-campaign) gives the complete example.

The adapters own their models and fixed public-material policies. The same policies enter the gateway and operator setup, and the archive and gateway share one call-log path. Roles and fallbacks remain revision-owned. P17's required archive publishes complete campaign packages, and P18's existing registration stores their prospective records under the archive's `prospective` directory. No second registration callback is added.

Version one is deliberately the local pilot deployment. It binds an empty findings ledger, `NO_MEMORY`, no generated-code runner and no registry importer. An optional rules directory selects existing frozen rules. Unsupported bindings fail instead of being silently ignored. The Python setup API remains available for deployments with other trusted bindings, and a generated-code revision retains the resolver's existing refusal without a runner.

[The research loop](../src/quant_research/research.py) adds `observe` beside the unchanged `status` and closed `record` APIs. One synchronous query captures the small report, the ledger's event count and workflow-owned settled attempts. A separate query reads at most 256 KiB of canonical ledger bytes per page, pinned to that run and event count. Ledger events append and its budgets stay fixed, so later charges cannot change the captured prefix. The client reconstructs its spent values from that ledger. The summary retains bounded assessment metadata and trial mappings, with evaluation bodies omitted; a trial status is not its scientific verdict. Large scientific text remains in the existing full record and archive. Spent strings stay out of the query head because legal exact arithmetic can render a value larger than one payload; the client reconstructs them from the captured ledger.

Actual ask settlement becomes visible only after every receipt has been charged. Receipt reconciliation also recognizes released reservations, including evaluation-only holds, as finished. It requires `Closed` for an actual settled ask even if that ask sent no call, while unused holds need no fictitious call or close. The call log may advance after the observation. Its later `Closed` entries do not establish settlement in the earlier workflow snapshot.

The CLI replaces count-only running reconciliation with per-call joins against that prefix. A known charge joins its call ID before its attempt, role and usage are compared, including the existing `cost_unreported` exception. A missing charged record is `unrecorded`. An uncharged record or unsealed claim is pending only for an unsettled reservation in the captured ledger. Settled discrepancies fail. Other campaigns and reservations created after the observation remain outside it. `agreed` may be true with explicit pending work; it means no demonstrated contradiction, not that all calls have finished. Closed receipt output and archived bytes retain their previous contracts.

[Steering](../src/quant_research/steering.py) owns a 65,536-byte UTF-8 revision limit. The CLI checks raw file bytes before parsing or importing Temporal. The Python client checks serialized bytes before sending. The workflow update validator checks first, before parsing or opening the child. The replayed handler is unchanged, because Temporal skips validators when replaying accepted updates. An earlier accepted oversized steer must still reconstruct its original state.

The opening activity already bounds the complete data-converter-encoded `Opened` result to 384 KiB, including escaped text and critics, and bounds its fixed request to 64 KiB before returning. This PR preserves that owner and verifies both bounds instead of adding a second result envelope.

Verification uses offline fixtures, fake Claude and Codex executables and the qualified local Temporal server. Completed targeted tests, process-level command checks, retained-history replay and cold-query equality belong in the PR. `make merge` posts its gate result there after a pass. Concurrent queries with caching disabled reproduced an SDK eviction error even through the unchanged legacy status query. The checks separate zero-cache sequential reconstruction and restart from concurrent readers with a one-entry cache. No check spends subscription credits or accesses a firm framework.

A separate local query-capacity fixture ran 100 ordinary steps with a 500-model role admitted at opening. Its synthetic activity persisted one real local scripted reply and 499 synthesized failure receipts per step. The production loop settled 50,000 model charges into a 14,839,845-byte ledger read through 57 actual pages. Five warm observations reconstructed identical canonical bytes with no query or deadlock failure, at a median 55.14 seconds and a range of 55.00 to 55.33 seconds. A cold worker restart and observation passed in 65.12 seconds. Query history stayed unchanged. The activity binds the revision and role instead of repeating their full assignment on every input, so this proves query capacity for that compact fixture; it does not establish that 100 real 500-call Gateway chains fit the history guard. Production orchestration can reach the guard earlier. Full-prefix serialization remains an operator-visible cost.

Alternatives were rejected:

- Reading mutable live report pages. One response could join different campaign moments.
- A second ledger event codec or event-fragment protocol. Canonical prefix paging reuses the existing storage owner. Repeated serialization per page is an explicit CPU cost, with no speed claim.
- A byte check in the replayed steer handler. That would reject previously accepted histories.
- Arbitrary imports, adapter arguments or runtime policies in configuration. The pilot needs only its accepted routes and trusted fixed bindings.

These limits remain:

- Every query still needs a worker on its original queue. Clients and workers need compatible query protocols, and workers sharing a queue must use the same configuration and compatible code.
- The metadata-head size checks cover the fixed pilot model records. A custom Python authority can produce a larger head; its opening bound is not a universal 256 KiB head guarantee. Binary ledger pages retain their 256 KiB bound.
- The 65,536-byte limit applies to each new steer. Failed or concurrent legal steers can still grow aggregate paused history. This PR does not claim a total history bound for that case.
- A direct RPC can reach Temporal's transport before the workflow validator rejects its oversized text. The CLI and Python client refuse it before sending.
- The worker configuration supplies no memory, generated execution or registry importer. It is not a serialization of every possible Python `Setup`.
- Synthetic adapter and Temporal checks establish operation and compatibility. Human acceptance, live subscription verification of this command and the independent Phase 4 capability gate remain open.

## D079. Name the reference pipeline's domain error

Selected on 23 October 2026 to complete the exception-rename item in [to dos](todos.md). `reference.ReferencePipelineError` replaces the project-defined `reference.ReferenceError`, which shadowed Python's builtin exception. The class remains a `ValueError`. Its raise sites, messages, causes and internal catches retain their behavior. The name covers experiment and rules parsing, allocation, search execution, shortlist validation and held-back evidence recovery.

All repository callers and tests use the new name. There is no compatibility alias. External imports, catches and pickled exceptions that name the removed class must migrate. Tracebacks and unwrapped Temporal failures use the new class name; the research activities retain their fixed failure types.

The offline prechange comparison preserves numerical result, input and contract bytes for all four synthetic reference cases, their statuses, frozen digests, 25 refusal messages and cause chains, and the malformed-input CLI result. Cause class names follow the rename. Prechange packages still open and verify with their original bytes. New implementation artifacts, specification IDs and package digests change because packages embed the module source. The PR records completed checks and the exact-commit merge gate result. This rename establishes no new scientific or real-framework acceptance.

## D080. Run new campaign model attempts through workflow-owned settlement

Selected on 24 October 2026 under the user's autonomy grant to complete the remaining [D061](#d061-run-model-calls-and-tool-calls-as-activities-that-stop-their-adapters) integration. A legacy `CampaignRun` body dispatched from inside its attempt activity. Cancelling that activity could close and charge its lease while its adapter was still running. The new protocol places the model lifecycle in the workflow through `ask_model`. [D077](#d077-align-recovery-records-and-pinned-lesson-authority)'s shared close remains the only close implementation.

`ModelJob` declares a body binding, argument, role, compute cost, dispatch ceiling and optional reply schema. Its `ModelBody` has preparation and interpretation callbacks. Preparation returns restricted materials. The worker resolves the pinned revision's active role and prompt; the workflow constructs one `Request` from that prompt and the materials. `ask_model` owns call IDs, fences, resumes, cancellation settlement and closure. Interpretation receives the exact request and `Answer` or `Unanswered`, then returns an ordinary domain `Outcome`. Its report carries the actual interpretation finish time. `ModelWork` supplies the stable acceptance attempt ID, revision, argument and resolved authority. Callbacks may run on different workers, so preparation must be repeatable and interpretation must publish idempotently under the attempt ID. Body names and resolver bindings are immutable operator contracts, not verified code hashes.

The workflow remains the single accounting and acceptance writer. It reserves the declared dispatch ceiling and ten minutes of wall occupancy before preparation. The wall charge covers preparation, model work, interpretation, queues and recovery, capped at that reservation, with receipt-close time excluded. Receipts are charged once before release. Rate-limited attempts remain inconclusive and unaccepted without spending a failure slot. Subscription pricing and unreported-cost fallback retain [D056](#d056-make-the-workflow-the-single-accounting-owner-with-durable-call-receipts)'s rules. Compute and external jobs keep the existing attempt activity with an empty dispatch lease.

For new executions, steering cancellation and direct Temporal cancellation settle running work and await the same close activity through repeated cancellation. They release the reservation and return a cancelled domain summary. Direct cancellation therefore ends the Temporal workflow successfully, as the research loop does; `handle.result()` returns that summary. An agent selected this policy under the autonomy grant. A valid interpretation report delivered with cancellation retains its actual `Completed` event but is never accepted. Missing or malformed reports create interruptions without invented domain completions. `RecordV2` records actual phase endings separately from interruptions. An external activity cancelled with `TRY_CANCEL` records the cancellation request time and leaves its ending unknown when only the request was observed.

One memoized `campaignrun-model-v2` patch decision at run entry selects the execution protocol. An unmarked recorded history retains its old commands, Task payloads, legacy record bytes and cancellation gap. A submission that has never executed may select the new protocol even if submitted before deployment. Fresh raw old-shaped model input fails with a non-retryable `LegacyModelProtocol` error before reservation or dispatch. New input uses a distinct entries envelope that an old worker cannot decode as `Plan`. The Python helper can join an exact retained old plan using its original canonical memo digest and returns a run-bound handle. It refuses mismatches, missing memos, other runner types and closed retained runs. A raw `USE_EXISTING` conflict is invisible to workflow code; only cooperating helper clients get this comparison.

The complete encoded private preparation, model and interpretation inputs are checked against the qualified default-codec transport limit of 2,097,152 bytes. A local probe measured the entire protobuf `Payloads` wrapper, including the revision and JSON escaping. Inputs of 132,267 and 2,094,251 bytes completed; 2,102,445 and 2,458,797 bytes failed workflow tasks in SDK completion validation before activity scheduling. A separate regression combines a legal initial plan and legal later steer into an oversized preparation input, then records that refusal without scheduling it. These checks belong at the private input producers. They establish no custom-codec or production transport configuration. An oversized stored model reply still closes and charges without resending; its full transcript remains in the call log.

Verification includes two frozen original histories with exact query-record bytes and canonical memo digests, an original pending Task that reuses its stored reply, fresh legacy-input refusal, new-path replay, cancellation at each model phase, same-task cancellation and report races, callback recovery, authority pinning across steering, receipt accounting, malformed results and transport failures. A separate actual local upgrade started the frozen old worker, stored one scripted reply, killed only that worker and recovered on the upgraded worker. The attempt and call IDs stayed fixed, the fence advanced from 1 to 2, original input and transcript bytes stayed unchanged, one receipt was charged and no new provider send occurred. Both client versions retrieved identical legacy record bytes, and the mixed history replayed with the upgraded failure converter. Evidence is preserved locally under `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-campaignrun-upgrade-20261024`, with manifest SHA-256 `223d02df8c6b7e60f68dccde147f7ce105454259c0fa2475ad8f7ad4cf9340aa`. The PR records the completed targeted checks and exact-commit merge gate.

The fixed preparation/request/interpretation protocol was selected over a general workflow-program engine, which would add sandbox registration and durable state serialization without a demonstrated caller. It adds two activity schedules per successful model attempt, and retries add more history. [D073](#d073-bound-the-campaign-runners-per-event-cost-and-payloads)'s old estimate of about 8,500 attempts is not a capacity claim for this protocol. Continue-as-new still waits for logs outside workflow state. There is no opaque callback continuation bag or arbitrary lower payload cap.

Deployment inventory and external body callers remain unknown. Operators must coordinate compatible clients, workers, immutable body bindings and retention on each queue; a merge does not upgrade running binaries or repair retained cancellation semantics. These offline and local scripted checks establish neither paid-provider behavior nor real-framework acceptance.

## D081. Enforce permitted months through pinned dataset contracts

Selected on 24 October 2026 under the user's autonomy grant to complete the conditional interval item in [to dos](todos.md). This extends [D062](#d062-resolve-a-campaign-revision-into-its-run-authority)'s dataset scope and [D045](#d045-read-approved-datasets-through-a-timing-and-access-adapter)'s reader. The six pilot contracts now declare `iso-month-start/closed-label/v1` in contract schema 2. Their structural part's source declaration includes the mapping, so the existing generation and manifest pins bind its interpretation. Version 1 publication retains its exact bytes and identities. Reads use the frozen declaration, and no read consults a current package contract.

An entirely legacy source set retains dataset-only authority, including when a new revision deliberately pins those old sources. Once a mapped structural generation is present, every permitted dataset needs one supported mapping. Missing mappings, unsupported reserved declarations and two distinct mapped generations for one dataset refuse resolution. Aliases of one generation count once, and ordinary legacy contract evidence may coexist with one mapped structural generation. This activation through existing source pins was selected over a new permission subtype and campaign file schema. A materialized allowed-key set was also rejected because it would scan and pin the data vintage during resolution.

`RunContext.dataset_periods` binds each dataset to its mapping, generation, manifest and original interval union. Issuance normalizes UTC endpoints and ordering without coalescing intervals. The context identity includes the binding only when present, so old identities and archive bytes remain exact. Changing an endpoint or mapping pin changes authority even when the currently selected rows are equal. `narrow` preserves the binding. Campaign file formats and `DataPermission` keep their existing shapes. `Authority.record` uses schema 3 for mapped contexts and preserves schema 2 bytes for legacy authority.

`datasets.read` remains the only live membership owner. After ordinary admission, it requires each mapped key to be canonical `YYYY-MM-01`, interprets its label as midnight UTC, and selects labels satisfying `start <= label <= end` in any permitted interval. Equal endpoints can include one month. A window from October 15 through December 15 includes November and December. Availability still uses the later of publication and arrival, and each selected key keeps its highest eligible revision. A malformed key fails even when its interval or availability would exclude it. An empty slice remains a supplied read, and its dataset remains listed and described.

The closed-label rule is a project operational policy selected by an agent. It is not a claim about the publisher's historical estimation policy. Mapping authority remains fixed through cutoff narrowing even before the structural source is visible to `describe_dataset`. The structural availability convention follows D062. Revised Part 0 occurrences carry newly recorded 24 October receipt metadata; their Evidence prose retains the original data acquisition dates, and unchanged later conclusions retain their original declarations. Human contract-field review remains open.

Every numerical and tool consumer inherits filtering through the existing reader. Permissions must explicitly include warmup input months. Missing initialization can make a signal uncomputable, and missing factor months retain their existing failure. Fixture authors now grant the months their existing held-back evaluation needs; the runtime never expands a permission.

The context owns its archive codec. New mapped archives retain the actual full-context `ReadRecord` for each admitted dataset, including empty reads, beside the whole vintage. Prospective import restores that authority, derives expected admitted datasets independently of artifact presence, checks the record's dataset, vintage, catalog, cutoff and context, and certifies its complete ordered selection through the same dataset selector as live reads. Omitted, duplicate, reordered, unavailable, stale and out-of-interval identities fail instead of becoming initialization evidence. General recorded-identity replay remains unchanged. These checks also run when rules are absent. Missing rules preserve incomplete-selection semantics while retaining available read evidence; missing read or vintage evidence fails. Legacy incomplete packages retain their existing interpretation. Whole vintage artifacts still expose excluded raw rows to package holders, and this change establishes no confirmation seal.

Verification preserves a prechange nonempty tool read of an interval-excluded observation, its description, authority, contexts, source generations, campaign and read bytes. They reproduce exactly after new mapping publication. The actual retained P20 first export also reproduces its original prospective procedure and read-evidence bytes. New checks cover disjoint unions, revisions and delayed arrivals, endpoint semantics, changed-authority replay refusal, narrowing, warmup, factor refusal, archived parent and child contexts, empty reads and missing or mismatched archive evidence. Preserved evidence is under `~/.codex/artifacts/quant-permitted-intervals-20261024`, with manifest SHA-256 `2b4eb565b8f1438403e512c32c7c2a15678ff0e0615ea8eb58664079acb57088`. The selection-certification probes, preserved failures and final source bindings are under the same artifact directory's `selection-fix` subdirectory, with manifest SHA-256 `8ac8748c465ad1b9a5598f62784dbb1d2a6a4cd7b003d378ba38bffa7ac40de8`. The PR records completed checks and the exact-commit merge gate.

This implements local read-time authority for the six declared monthly contracts. Opaque-key and other calendar mappings still need their own supported contract. Real future observations, human review, firm interfaces and Phase 4 acceptance remain open.
